- **Exact audit action-name strings**: `"created"`/`"updated"`/`"archived"`/`"unarchived"`/`"deleted"`/`"reordered"`/`"renamed"` (mirroring `action_types.py`'s own verbs exactly for the action-type sub-resource), plus two new ones this phase introduces for the version lifecycle: `"published"` and `"retired"`. Entity-type strings: `"compliance_standard"`, `"compliance_standard_version"`, `"compliance_requirement"`, `"compliance_required_action"`, `"compliance_action_type_definition"`.
- **No reparenting endpoint for requirements**: `ComplianceRequirementUpdate` has no `parent_requirement_id` field. Nothing in the spec asks for moving a requirement to a different parent after creation, and adding it would complicate `move`'s own sibling-group semantics (a requirement's sibling group is its `(standard_version_id, parent_requirement_id)` pair) for a capability nobody asked for yet — if a future session needs this, it should be a small, dedicated endpoint rather than folded into the general update payload.
- **RBAC composition confirmed, not reimplemented**: `require_module_role("compliance", "compliance_manager")` already composes `is_server_admin`/`OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN`/the direct grant (Phase 2's own design) — this phase's tests (`test_rbac_composition_manager_grant_admin_override_and_member_forbidden`) prove all three paths succeed and a plain member without either is rejected, per the spec's explicit instruction to confirm this rather than assume it. No new RBAC code was needed.
- **No new SOC 2 policy-document edits were needed this phase**, per the plan's own "SOC2 / Security Planning" guidance that Phase 6 opens no new trust boundary (it reuses Phase 0/2's already-hardened `require_module_role` and the existing `log_event` audit pattern verbatim). A short identify→verify→remediate pass was still recorded in `docs/decisions.md` per this repo's change-management policy for any RBAC/audit-touching change — it found nothing new to fix, which is itself the recorded outcome, not a skipped step.
- **Tests**: `backend/tests/test_compliance_standards_api.py` (new, 9 tests) covers the full CRUD happy path (standard → version → parent/child requirement → required action → action type) through the real HTTP API for the first time this module has had one, requirement/required-action reordering, the RBAC composition proof above, the publish/retire lifecycle plus immutability enforcement, version cloning, symmetric cross-org 404 isolation, audit-event assertions, and action-type delete-with-reassignment (including the "can be emptied to zero" case). `backend/tests/test_module_mcp_tools.py` gained a new section (`test_compliance_mcp_tools_resolve_against_the_real_registry`) that calls `build_mcp_tool_manifest()` against the real, non-fixture module registry and asserts all three `compliance_*` tools resolve with `mutates=False` and the right params — concrete proof `module.py`'s declared `path_template`s match real routes, not just that the strings look right. `backend/tests/test_compliance_data_model.py`'s two now-stale assertions (`get_router() is None`, `mcp_tools == ()`) were updated to their Phase 6 reality. Full backend suite re-run afterward with no other DB-touching process active to confirm no regression; `ruff check` clean on every touched/new file.

### Phase 7 — Project Compliance Assignment & Assessment

**Satisfies:** §7 (Applying Compliance to a Project), §8 (Project Compliance Requirements), §9 (Applicability incl. hierarchical inheritance/override), §10 (Compliance Status), §11 (Project Compliance Permissions), §16 (Rationale/Auditability), §20 (Overall Status calculation — define and document the exact rule per §20's explicit requirement), §26 (Security and Permissions, project-manager/compliance-officer split).

- `ProjectCompliance` (project ↔ standard version assignment) and `ProjectComplianceRequirement` (project-specific assessment per requirement) models + API.
- Hierarchical applicability: parent Not Applicable ⇒ children default Not Applicable, with explicit, audited, authorised override per child (§9) — UI must distinguish explicit/inherited/overridden (deferred to Phase 13, but the data model must carry enough to support it now: an `applicability_source` discriminator, not just a resolved boolean).
- Mandatory justification enforced at the API layer for Not Applicable and Non-Compliant (§9, §16 — "must not simply mean... ignored").
- Gated by `require_module_role("compliance", "compliance_officer")` for assigned projects, plus existing `ProjectRole.PROJECT_MANAGER`.
- Declare read-only Phase 4 MCP tools, each taking `project_id` as a required parameter (resolves the owning org implicitly the same way `get_project(project_id)` already does today — no separate `organization_id` needed once a project is named): `compliance_get_project_status(project_id)` (overall status + counts per §20), `compliance_list_non_compliant_requirements(project_id)`.

#### Phase 7 notes (completed 2026-09-05)

Implemented as specified, with a significant infrastructure addition and a number of judgment calls the spec text left open — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **Registry gains a second, optional router per module (`ModuleDefinition.get_project_router`)** — not anticipated by the spec text, but forced by its own explicit instruction that `compliance_get_project_status`/`compliance_list_non_compliant_requirements` take `project_id` as their *only* parameter, "no separate `organization_id` needed." That's impossible for a route nested under the existing org-scoped router (which necessarily carries `{organization_id}`), so `app.modules.registry.ModuleDefinition` gained `get_project_router: Callable[[], APIRouter | None] | None = None`, mounted by `app.main`'s loop exactly like `get_router`, and `build_mcp_tool_manifest` now validates a tool's `path_template` against *either* of a module's router prefixes (whichever one actually has the matching route), not just one. This is a real, deliberate module-system change, not a compliance-specific hack — `docs/modules.md` and `docs/solution-architecture.md` were both updated so a future module author can use the same mechanism. Compliance's own `router.py` (org-scoped, gains standard-to-project assignment/archival endpoints) and new `project_router.py` (project-scoped, `/api/v1/projects/{project_id}/modules/compliance/...`) are the first module to use both.
- **Assigning a standard to a project is a Compliance Manager (org-scoped) action, not a Project Manager one** — §26's own bullet lists don't actually say who assigns (only what each role can do with an assignment once it exists); `module.py`'s own Phase 5/6-authored role description ("assigns standards to projects") already committed to this reading, and it fits the operational model better regardless: the org-wide catalog owner decides which projects use which standards, day-to-day assessment is a project-level concern. This is why assignment/archive/unarchive live on the org router (`_require_manage`) while everything else — viewing, applicability, assessment, required actions, history, status, non-compliant list — lives on the project router (`require_module_role("compliance", "compliance_officer")`/`require_project_module_enabled`).
- **Only a `PUBLISHED` version may be assigned to a project** (409 for `DRAFT`; `ComplianceStandardVersionStatus.RETIRED`'s own Phase 5 docstring already says a retired version is "no longer assignable to **new** projects," so that case needed no new reasoning, just enforcement).
- **Every `ComplianceRequirement`/`ComplianceRequiredAction` in the assigned version gets a project-specific assessment row, materialised in full at assignment time** (`service.materialize_assessment_rows`), not lazily — a published version's content is immutable, so there is nothing to reconcile into these rows later. This includes section/parent requirements, not just leaves: the spec didn't ask for a leaf/parent distinction, and one would only complicate §20's own requirement-count semantics (its own worked example counts every requirement, not just leaves) for no benefit.
- **"Compliance Officer(s)" (§7's own field on `ProjectCompliance`) is not a column** — it's already fully answered by Phase 2's `UserModuleRole` grants (`module_key="compliance", role_key="compliance_officer", project_id=...`), exposed through the existing `GET/POST .../members/{user_id}/module-roles` endpoints. Adding a second field would just create a second, driftable source of truth for the same fact. Likewise "Review schedule" is deliberately absent — Phase 10's `ComplianceReview` entity owns it, mirroring the identical call already made for `ComplianceStandard` in Phase 5.
- **A single `justification` column serves both §9's mandatory Not-Applicable justification and §16's mandatory Non-Compliant rationale** — §8 itself lists this as one field ("Justification/rationale"), and the two conditions are never true at once in practice. Enforced at the API layer (400), not a schema validator or a NOT NULL constraint, since which condition applies depends on which other field is being changed in the same request.
- **`approval_state` exists on `ProjectComplianceRequirement` now, default `NOT_ASSESSED`, with no endpoint to change it** — added a phase ahead of Phase 9's actual workflow because §20's own overall-status calculation (which this phase is required to fully define) explicitly must fold approval state in ("a project may have 100% compliant requirements but still have an overall status of 'Pending Approval'"); splitting the column from the calculation that needs it across two phases would just require reconciling it later. In practice every row stays `NOT_ASSESSED` until Phase 9 ships, so `overall_approval_state` is currently always `not_assessed` — expected, not a bug.
- **§20's exact calculation** is `service.py::summarize_project_compliance`, fully documented in that function's own docstring: total/applicable/not-applicable counts, a per-`ComplianceStatus` breakdown (Not Applicable rows excluded from every count/percentage), `compliance_percentage = compliant / applicable * 100` (`100.0`, not a division error, when nothing is applicable), a `has_non_compliant` flag shown regardless of percentage, an `overall_compliance_state` (`compliant`/`non_compliant`/`in_progress`/`not_applicable`), and a **separately-reported** `overall_approval_state` (a "worst state wins" reduction — `REJECTED` > `REQUIRES_REASSESSMENT` > `PENDING_APPROVAL` > `ASSESSED` > `NOT_ASSESSED` > `APPROVED` — so overall is `APPROVED` only when every applicable row is), never folded into `overall_compliance_state`, per §20's own explicit instruction that the two stay separate.
- **§9's hierarchical applicability resolution is computed on every read, never stored** (`service.py::resolve_applicability_for_version`) — a single pass over one version's full requirement tree per call, classifying each row `EXPLICIT` (a user-set value, or the untouched default, with no conflicting ancestor), `INHERITED` (no explicit decision, nearest ancestor is Not Applicable), or `OVERRIDDEN` (explicitly Applicable despite a Not Applicable ancestor). `ProjectComplianceRequirement.explicit_applicability` stays nullable specifically so "never decided" is distinguishable from "explicitly Applicable," even though both currently render identically (`EXPLICIT`) — a deliberate small piece of future-proofing, not dead state.
- **A generic `AuditEventOut` schema (`app/schemas/audit.py`) was added for this phase's own "view compliance history" requirement (§8/§11/§16)** — `AuditEvent` had been write-only from the API's own perspective until now (confirmed by grepping the whole `routers/` tree: nothing anywhere read one back over HTTP). Rather than build a compliance-specific history mechanism, this phase exposes the existing audit trail generically, filtered by `entity_type="project_compliance_requirement"` — available to any future core or module feature with the same need, not just this one.
- **`ComplianceRequiredActionAssessment` mirrors `Requirement`'s own completion-overlay shape** (`is_completed`/`completed_at`/`completed_by`, plus `complete`/`uncomplete` action endpoints) rather than reusing the `ComplianceStatus` enum — §6's own field list ("Status, Assignee, Due date, Completion information") reads as a task's fields, not an assessment's, and a required action being completed is never allowed to silently imply its parent requirement is now Compliant — that stays a distinct, deliberate, human decision on `ProjectComplianceRequirement.compliance_status`, never auto-derived.
- **Cross-project viewing (§26's "Compliance Manager: view compliance across projects") lives on the org router** (`GET .../orgs/{organization_id}/modules/compliance/project-compliance`, `_require_manage`-gated, not view-gated) — reuses the identical `service.build_status_out` calculation the project-scoped `/status` endpoint uses, so there is exactly one §20 implementation, never two.
- Tests landed as `backend/tests/test_project_compliance_api.py` (new, 11 tests) covering: compliance-manager-can-assign/plain-member-cannot, draft-version-assignment-409, assignment materialisation + duplicate-assignment-400, hierarchical applicability inherit/override (plus mandatory-justification-400), mandatory-justification-for-non-compliant (plus history assertions), the officer-grant/PROJECT_MANAGER-override/plain-member-403/any-member-read RBAC composition, the full §20 calculation (including the has-non-compliant/overall-state flip and the cross-project compliance-manager view), required-action-assessment complete/uncomplete (plus 409-already-completed), cross-project/cross-scope isolation, and archive/unarchive. `backend/tests/test_module_mcp_tools.py`'s real-registry compliance section was extended to the two new tools, concretely proving the two-router manifest-validation mechanism works, not just the single-router case Phase 4 originally proved. `backend/tests/test_compliance_data_model.py`'s module-registration assertion was updated for `get_project_router`/five tools. Run against a live `reqtrack_pytest_test` database: the new/touched test files pass in isolation (66 passed across the four affected files), and the full backend suite was re-run afterward with no other DB-touching process active to confirm no regression. `ruff check` clean on every touched/new file. No `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` changes were made — per Phase 5/6's own established precedent, seeding compliance data (now including a real project assignment) remains Phase 15's own, already-documented responsibility.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 7" entry for the full account, including the identify→verify→remediate pass.
- **Lesson for future phases with their own new long-named FK columns**: a first full-suite run caught a real drift-test failure — `ComplianceRequiredActionAssessment.project_compliance_requirement_id`'s auto-generated index name (`ix_compliance_required_action_assessments_project_compliance_requirement_id`, 75 characters) exceeds Postgres's 63-byte identifier limit; Postgres silently truncates it at `CREATE INDEX` time, which then never matches SQLAlchemy's own untruncated computed name, permanently failing `test_schema_migrations_match_models.py` regardless of what the migration itself names it. Fixed by giving that one index an explicit, short name via `Index(...)` in `__table_args__` instead of `index=True`. No other table/column in this codebase was long enough to hit this before now — check for it (`len(index.name) > 63`) whenever a new table combines a long table name with a long FK column name.

### Phase 8 — Evidence

**Satisfies:** §13 (Evidence), §14 (Expiry Dates), §15 (Validity/Revalidation).

- Evidence model + `compliance_evidence_files` join table via `services/files.py::upload_file` (the established join-table-per-owner-type convention).
- Multi-requirement/multi-action linkage (§13's "single piece of evidence... support multiple compliance requirements").
- Expiry date, revalidation history (append-only, never overwrites — §15's explicit "must not overwrite the historical record").
- Valid/expiring/expired identification — a queryable derived state, not a stored one that can drift.
- Declare a read-only Phase 4 MCP tool: `compliance_list_expiring_evidence(project_id)`.

#### Phase 8 notes (completed 2026-09-05)

Implemented as specified, with a number of judgment calls the spec text left open — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **Evidence is project-scoped (`ComplianceEvidence.project_id`), not tied to one specific `ProjectCompliance` assignment** — the spec's own "multi-requirement/multi-action linkage" bullet, combined with §13's literal "supporting multiple compliance requirements and/or standards," reads most naturally as spanning more than one of a project's standard assignments at once (one certificate satisfying requirements under two different standards a project happens to be assigned). `ComplianceEvidenceRequirementLink`/`ComplianceEvidenceActionLink` are genuine many-to-many join tables pointing at `ProjectComplianceRequirement`/`ComplianceRequiredActionAssessment` — the project-specific assessment layer Phase 7 established — never at the reusable `ComplianceRequirement`/`ComplianceRequiredAction` definitions, extending Phase 7's own "state belongs to the project-specific assessment layer" principle one concept further.
- **A new, generic module-system hook, not a compliance-specific core-router edit**: making evidence file attachments downloadable through the existing single, generic `GET /api/v1/files/{id}` endpoint (`app.routers.files.download_file`) without that router importing `app.modules.compliance` directly needed a real (small) module-system addition — `ModuleDefinition.resolve_file_owner_project_id` (`app.modules.registry`) plus `resolve_module_file_project_id`, which tries every registered module's hook, in registry order, as a fallback after the router's existing core attachment-type checks. This mirrors Phase 7's own `get_project_router` precedent: a genuine, generic module-system capability discovered while building the first module that needs it, not a one-off. `docs/modules.md` was updated so a future module author with file attachments of their own can use it directly.
- **`is_archived`/`archived_at`/`archived_by` on `ComplianceEvidence` is this model's answer to §13's own listed attribute "Whether it remains applicable"** — mirrors every other compliance entity's soft-delete convention exactly rather than inventing a differently-named field for the same concept, and is kept a genuinely distinct axis from §14's expiry-derived validity: `GET .../expiring-evidence` excludes archived rows (a project has already said this evidence no longer matters), but `compute_evidence_validity_state` still computes a real value for an archived row rather than special-casing it to some third "n/a" state — "applicable" and "valid" are different questions, not one collapsed into the other.
- **`ComplianceEvidenceUpdate` has no `expiry_date` field at all**, not merely a validator rejecting attempts to change it — per §15's explicit "must not overwrite the historical record," the only sanctioned way to change an evidence row's expiry is `POST .../revalidate`, which also writes the append-only history row; omitting the field from the update schema is the simplest way to make a plain metadata edit structurally incapable of bypassing that.
- **`ComplianceEvidenceRevalidation` is a dedicated, typed append-only table**, not `services.audit.log_event` reuse (unlike Phase 7's choice for requirement-assessment history) — §15's own worked example needs structured `previous_expiry_date`/`new_expiry_date`/`revalidated_by`/`revalidated_at`/`justification` fields for a dedicated "this evidence's own revalidation history" listing, and a revalidation is itself a real column change (`ComplianceEvidence.expiry_date`) needing its *own* audit-log entry in addition (which `revalidate_evidence` also writes, via `log_event`, mirroring `update_requirement_applicability`'s "detail carries previous/new value" convention) — the two aren't a substitute for each other here the way they were for Phase 7's purely-computed-from-columns history.
- **`EVIDENCE_EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS = 30` is a plain module-level constant, not a configurable per-project/org setting** — §14 names no specific lead time, and unlike `Project.review_reminder_lead_days_default` (which exists because a real scheduler job already consumes it), Phase 8 has no notification delivery of its own yet; Phase 10 (Scheduled Reviews + Notifications) is the natural place to promote this to a real setting if/when it needs one, once there's an actual consumer to configure for.
- **Two convenience, read-only endpoints** (`GET .../requirements/{pcr_id}/evidence`, `GET .../required-action-assessments/{id}/evidence`) view evidence from the assessment side, mirroring §13's "a requirement or Required Action should be able to reference supporting evidence" — the canonical CRUD/linkage surface stays the project-level `/evidence` resource (a single evidence row can span assignments a single pcr's own nested path can't reach), and these two endpoints are pure reads over the same `ComplianceEvidenceRequirementLink`/`ComplianceEvidenceActionLink` tables, not a second way to mutate anything.
- **File attachment endpoints (`upload`/`link`/`list`/`unlink`) are near-verbatim copies of `routers.requirements`'s own four**, reusing `services.files.upload_file`/`delete_file` exactly per §13's "reuse ReqTrackManager's existing attachment/file mechanisms" — including one hardening detail carried over deliberately: `unlink_evidence_file` calls `db.flush()` between deleting the link row and (for a non-shared upload) deleting the `FileAsset`, exactly like `unlink_requirement_file` does, to avoid the `FileAsset`'s own `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `compliance_evidence_files.file_id` racing the ORM's pending `DELETE` for that same link row. This was actually caught the hard way — see "Tests" below — before being recognised as the same class of thing the core code already handles correctly.
- **No new SOC 2 policy-document edits were needed this phase** — evidence reuses Phase 2's already-hardened `require_module_role`, Phase 6's `_get_*_or_404`/404-not-403 convention, and the existing, already-reviewed `services.files.upload_file`/`delete_file` mechanism verbatim; the one new mechanism (`resolve_file_owner_project_id`) only ever *adds* an authorization path for a file a module itself created a link row for, it cannot grant access to anything a module doesn't already own a reference to. A short identify→verify→remediate pass was still recorded in `docs/decisions.md` per this repo's change-management policy.
- **Tests** landed as `backend/tests/test_compliance_evidence_api.py` (new, 10 tests). A first test run caught a real bug — not a pre-existing gap, introduced by this phase's own `unlink_evidence_file` initially omitting the `db.flush()` mentioned above — surfaced as a benign-looking `SAWarning` ("DELETE statement ... 0 were matched") rather than a test failure; re-run with `-W error::sqlalchemy.exc.SAWarning` after the fix to confirm it was actually gone, not just unobserved, per this repo's own "warnings must be fixed, not ignored" rule. `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` were not touched — per this plan's own established precedent, seeding compliance data (evidence included) remains Phase 15's own, already-documented responsibility.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 8" entry for the full account, including the identify→verify→remediate pass.

### Phase 9 — Approval / Sign-off Workflow

**Satisfies:** §12 (Formal Approval/Sign-off), §16 (Auditability), §27 (re-assessment triggers).

- State machine per §12: Not Assessed → Assessed → Pending Approval → Approved/Rejected → Requires Re-assessment.
- Full approval history (who assessed, who approved, timestamps, previous/current state).
- Auto-invalidation: a material change to evidence (expiry) or the underlying requirement (standard version change) flags the approval as requiring re-assessment rather than silently remaining "Approved" (§15, §27).
- MCP tools: a read-only `compliance_list_pending_approvals(project_id)` tool is fine (mirrors the existing `list_project_reviews_due(project_id)` shape). **The approve/reject/sign-off action itself must never be declared as an MCP tool** — mark it `is_approval_action: true` if it's ever tempting to add one, so Phase 4's manifest-builder exclusion catches it even if a future session forgets why. This mirrors `docs/mcp-server.md`'s existing, explicit rule that ReqTrackManager's core approval workflow is deliberately excluded from the tool surface for the same reason (accountable-human-decision, not an RBAC question).

#### Phase 9 notes (completed 2026-09-06)

Implemented as specified, with a few judgment calls the spec above left open — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:
- **Two different resulting states for the two kinds of "material change," not one.** The spec's own bullet list doesn't say whether a fresh reassessment and an evidence/applicability change should land on the same state. They don't: `service.advance_approval_state_on_assessment` always sets `ASSESSED` (from *any* prior state, including `APPROVED`) when `update_requirement_assessment` runs, since performing that endpoint's own action *is* the reassessment — landing on `REQUIRES_REASSESSMENT` would contradict that state's own Phase-7-authored meaning ("a reassessment is still needed"). `service.invalidate_approval_if_in_flight` instead downgrades `PENDING_APPROVAL`/`APPROVED` to `REQUIRES_REASSESSMENT` for a change *without* an accompanying reassessment — an applicability change (only when the value actually changes) or evidence being archived/revalidated.
- **§27's "standard version change" trigger is a deliberate no-op in this phase, not a gap.** `ProjectCompliance.standard_version_id` never changes after assignment (Phase 7's own pinned-forever design) and there is no version-migration action yet (Phase 11). `invalidate_approval_if_in_flight` is generic enough for Phase 11 to call per affected requirement when that migration action ships; nothing in this phase calls it for that case, since nothing in this phase can.
- **No bespoke approval-history table.** §12's "approval/sign-off history" is satisfied entirely by the existing `AuditEvent`/`get_requirement_history` trail Phase 7 already exposed — every new transition logs through `services.audit.log_event` like every other mutation on this router. `approval_decided_at`/`approval_decided_by`/`decision_note` are the only new columns, and `decision_note` is one field serving both an approval and a rejection decision (mirroring `justification`'s own established shape), not two.
- **Rejection requires a `decision_note` (400 if blank); approval doesn't.** §12 itself doesn't explicitly mandate this, but it's a direct, deliberate extension of §16's already-twice-established mandatory-rationale pattern (Not Applicable, Non-Compliant) to the same class of negative/blocking decision — flagged explicitly here per this repo's own "don't silently invent requirements" norm, rather than left undiscoverable as a judgment call.
- **`submit-for-approval` is also marked `APPROVAL_ACTION_ROUTE_EXTRA`**, not just `approve`/`reject` — even though it only queues a decision rather than making one. §11 describes the whole flow ("Request/perform assessment... Approve/sign off compliance") as one accountable-human action set, so all three got the same belt-and-braces treatment; the primary defence for all three is still simply that `module.py` declares no `McpToolDefinition` for any of them at all.
- Evidence-triggered invalidation (`_invalidate_approvals_supported_by_evidence`, `project_router.py`) logs with the *acting user's* id, not `None` — a human directly caused the archive/revalidate call, unlike a hypothetical future Phase 10 passive-expiry sweep (which would have no human actor and should log `actor_id=None` instead, per `services.audit.log_event`'s own "None for system-initiated actions" convention).
- `list_pending_approvals` was placed next to `list_non_compliant_requirements` (both cross-assignment listings), not down near the state-machine action endpoints it's most related to by feature — grouping by "shape of query" (loop over active assignments, filter, project into a summary schema) rather than "phase that added it" matched this file's own existing section organisation more closely.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 9" entry for the full account, including the identify→verify→remediate pass.

### Phase 10 — Scheduled Reviews + Notifications

**Satisfies:** §17 (Scheduled Compliance Reviews), §18 (Notifications and Reminders), §28 (Scheduled Processing).

- `ComplianceReview` model (frequency, next-due date, owner, status, history, outcome).
- New APScheduler jobs (`services/scheduler.py` pattern): evidence-expiry sweep, review due/overdue sweep, required-action due/overdue sweep, pending-approval sweep.
- New `NotificationType` members for every event listed in §18; label-map entries in `frontend/src/i18n/strings.ts` (fix the pre-existing `types.ts` union drift noted above while here).

#### Phase 10 notes (completed 2026-09-06)

Implemented as specified, with a number of judgment calls the spec text left open — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **A new generic module-system mechanism, not core-scheduler code importing Compliance directly**: `app.modules.registry.ModuleScheduledJob` (`job_id`/`hour`/`minute`/`run: Callable[[Session], None]`) plus `ModuleDefinition.scheduled_jobs` and `get_all_module_scheduled_jobs()` — `services/scheduler.py::start_scheduler` iterates every registered module's declared jobs generically (job-id-prefixed with the module's own key) alongside its own two core jobs, exactly the same "core code shouldn't need much modification per module" pattern Phase 7's `get_project_router`/Phase 8's `resolve_file_owner_project_id` already established when *they* were each the first thing to need a new capability. This wasn't explicitly asked for by the spec text ("New APScheduler jobs (`services/scheduler.py` pattern)" could have been read as "hand-add four calls to that file"), but the alternative would have broken this module system's own established principle the moment a *second* module ever needed a scheduled job.
- **`ComplianceReview` covers both of §17's named owners as one entity with a `CHECK` constraint**, not two separate tables or a shared "reviewable" polymorphic abstraction — a standard-level review (e.g. "annual audit of this standard itself") and a project-level review share every field except which one thing they're about. `frequency_label` (free text) + optional `recurrence_days` (nullable — one-off reviews like "review before product release" don't recur) was chosen over a fixed calendar-frequency enum specifically because §17's own examples include event-triggered, non-calendar reviews a rigid enum can't represent cleanly.
- **§17's "Review history" is the retained chain of `ComplianceReview` rows itself, not a separate table**: completing a recurring review creates a *new* `SCHEDULED` row for the next cycle (`service.complete_review`) rather than reopening/resetting the completed one, which stays `COMPLETED` forever. Listing every review for one owner, ordered by `created_at`, is that owner's full review history — the same "don't build a second history mechanism when the natural one already exists" call this module made for evidence (Phase 8) and approvals (Phase 9), applied to a different underlying shape (chained rows instead of `AuditEvent`).
- **`schedule_state` (upcoming/due/overdue) is computed at read time, never stored**, mirroring `ComplianceEvidenceValidityState`'s own "can't drift" precedent exactly — a `COMPLIANCE_REVIEW_DUE_WARNING_DAYS = 14` constant (shorter than evidence's 30-day window, since a review is an activity someone must schedule time to perform, not a certificate renewed well ahead of need).
- **A full CRUD + complete + evidence-linkage surface was built for reviews on both routers** (`router.py` for standard-level, `project_router.py` for project-level), not just the read-only "identify due/overdue" capability the spec's own bullet list technically only asked for — without create/update/complete endpoints, "Compliance Standards and Project Compliance records must support scheduled reviews" (§17) would have nothing to actually schedule a review *with*. A review can only be edited/deleted while still `SCHEDULED` (409 once `COMPLETED` — retained history must not be edited in place), mirroring this module's existing "immutable once it matters" pattern (published standard versions, decided approvals).
- **A new unified `GET /reviews-due` endpoint on the project router** gathers both a project's own project-level reviews *and* every review of a standard that project is currently (non-archived-ly) assigned to — a standard-level review affects every project assigned to it, so a project's own dashboard/MCP tool should surface both without the caller needing to separately query the org router. This is also the `compliance_list_reviews_due(project_id)` MCP tool, the 8th (all read-only, matching this module's existing cautious default).
- **Six event-driven notifications, plus four date-driven sweeps — ten of §18's fourteen listed events are new triggers wired into *existing* Phase 6/7/9 endpoints**, not built as part of this phase's own review/evidence models: a Non-Compliant assessment (`update_requirement_assessment`), submitting for approval (`submit_requirement_for_approval`), a rejection (`reject_requirement`, notifying the original assessor specifically), an applicability or evidence change invalidating an in-flight/decided approval (`update_requirement_applicability`/`_invalidate_approvals_supported_by_evidence`, via a new shared `_notify_approval_invalidated` helper), a new standard version being published (`publish_standard_version`, notifying compliance officers of every project still pinned to an older version of that standard), and a new project assignment (`create_project_compliance`). None of these had *any* notification wired up before this phase, confirmed by grepping the whole module for `notify`/`notifications.` before starting — Phase 9's own notes never mention notifications at all.
- **Two new recipient-resolution helpers, deliberately not authorization checks**: `service.get_effective_compliance_officers`/`get_effective_compliance_managers` resolve "who should be told," reusing `rbac.get_effective_project_managers` for the project case (so group-inherited/forward-inherited project managers are covered) but a direct-only `UserOrgRole` query for the org-admin case (this codebase's org groups have no `OrgGroupRole` table, unlike project groups — there is no inheritance path to cover). Explicitly documented as a different, lower-stakes class of correctness than an RBAC gate: missing an edge case here is a missed notification, not an access-control gap — real authorization on every mutating endpoint stays exactly `require_module_role`, untouched by this phase.
- **Every sweep is idempotent via a `*_reminder_sent_at`/`*_notified_at` timestamp pair on the owning row** (`ComplianceEvidence`, `ComplianceRequiredActionAssessment`, `ProjectCompliance`, `ComplianceReview`), mirroring `RequirementVersion.review_reminder_sent_at`'s own Massif-era convention exactly rather than inventing a new one — each pair is reset to `None` whenever the underlying due/expiry date is actually rescheduled (`update_required_action_assessment`, `revalidate_evidence`, the review `update`/`PATCH` endpoints), so a rescheduled date gets its own fresh reminder cycle instead of silently inheriting the old date's "already reminded" state. `ProjectCompliance.target_compliance_date` needed no reset logic — confirmed there is no update endpoint for it at all (set once, at assignment time), so it can never need rescheduling.
- **`EVIDENCE_EXPIRY_WARNING_DAYS` was *not* promoted to a configurable setting**, despite Phase 8's own comment flagging this phase as "the natural place... if that phase needs one" — it doesn't: the sweep is a real consumer, but §14/§18 name no specific lead time and nothing in this phase's own requirements calls for per-project/org configurability, only for the notification to exist at all. Promoting it now would be scope beyond what was asked; revisit if a future request specifically asks for configurable lead times.
- **`docs/mcp-server.md`'s "Nothing to configure for the common case" paragraph was also fixed in passing** — it still said "Compliance itself doesn't land until a later phase," stale since Phase 6 actually shipped it; corrected while already editing this section for the 8th tool, per this repo's own "fix issues found incidentally" rule.
- **`docs/solution-architecture.md`'s exhaustive table-count list had a larger, pre-existing drift, found and fixed in the same pass**: its "Compliance module" group had been left at only Phase 5's original five tables ever since Phase 7 shipped — the eight tables Phases 7 and 8 actually added were each described in this document's own prose sections and present in the ER diagram, but never carried over into that exhaustive list. Discovered by cross-checking the list's implied total against a live migrated database's real table count (69, confirmed via `information_schema.tables`) rather than assumed correct, the same method the fifth/sixth drift-corrections already recorded there used. Fixed alongside adding Phase 10's own two new tables, not left as a second, unrelated gap.
- **Tests**: `backend/tests/test_compliance_reviews_and_notifications.py` (new, 20 tests) covers project-level and standard-level review CRUD/RBAC, `schedule_state` computation across upcoming/due/overdue, update-only-while-scheduled (plus reminder-stamp reset), complete-with-recurrence creating the next cycle (and without recurrence not recurring), delete-only-while-scheduled, evidence linkage, the unified `reviews-due` listing spanning both owner types, all six event-driven notifications, and all four sweeps run twice each to prove idempotency. `backend/tests/test_compliance_data_model.py`'s module-registration assertion was updated for eight tools and four scheduled jobs; `backend/tests/test_module_mcp_tools.py`'s real-registry compliance section was extended to the eighth tool. Full backend suite re-run afterward with no other DB-touching process active to confirm no regression (`test_schema_migrations_match_models.py` included, confirming migration 0030 matches the models with zero drift). `ruff check` clean on every touched/new file. No `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` changes were made — per this plan's own established precedent, seeding compliance data (reviews included) remains Phase 15's own, already-documented responsibility.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 10" entry for the full account, including the identify→verify→remediate pass.

### Phase 11 — Cross-Standard Mapping + Version Impact

**Satisfies:** §19 (Cross-Standard Requirement Mapping), §27 (Version Changes and Impact Assessment).

- Mapping model with a configurable/extensible relationship-type vocabulary (Equivalent/Satisfies/Derived From/Related To/Overlaps/Conflicts With — §19), navigable from both requirements, auditable.
- Version diff: identify added/removed/modified/replaced/re-mapped requirements between two versions of a standard (§27).
- Explicit, user-triggered project migration action to adopt a newer standard version — never silent (§4, §27).

#### Phase 11 notes (completed 2026-09-06)

Implemented as specified, with a number of judgment calls the spec text left open — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **A new durable lineage column, `ComplianceRequirement.cloned_from_requirement_id`, drives the version diff** — not a name/reference-matching heuristic. `router.py::_clone_requirement_tree` now stamps it at clone time; `service.diff_standard_versions` walks it (multiple hops when the two diffed versions aren't directly adjacent) to resolve each new-version requirement's old-version ancestor, if any. `ON DELETE SET NULL`, deliberately not `CASCADE` like `parent_requirement_id` one column up — see `models.py`'s own Phase 11 design-decisions section for the full reasoning.
- **One mapping table, `ComplianceRequirementMapping`, serves both §19's cross-standard mapping and §27's "replaced" category** — a same-standard, cross-version "this new requirement replaces that removed one" link is structurally the same fact as a cross-standard "these are equivalent" link, so the diff's `replaced` category is computed by looking for an explicit mapping between a lineage-orphaned new requirement and a not-yet-matched old requirement of the *same* standard, rather than building a second "supersedes" mechanism.
- **`re_mapped` relies on mapping links never being auto-cloned forward** — a freshly cloned requirement always starts with zero mappings of its own (`_clone_requirement_tree` never touches the mapping table), and `diff_standard_versions` treats "old had mappings, new doesn't (or has a different set)" as precisely the signal a Compliance Manager needs to know which requirements' cross-standard relationships need re-establishing after a new version — not a gap to silently paper over by carrying mappings forward automatically.
- **The relationship-type vocabulary table is named `compliance_mapping_relationship_types`, not `..._type_definitions`** despite the model class being `ComplianceMappingRelationshipTypeDefinition` (matching `ComplianceActionTypeDefinition`'s naming) — the longer table name's auto-generated `organization_id` index name would exceed Postgres's 63-byte identifier limit, the same class of problem this file's `ComplianceRequiredActionAssessment` already hit for a different table.
- **Migration carries forward an assessment automatically only when the diff proves lineage-matched *and* content-identical** (`unmodified_lineage_map`) — a `modified` or `added` requirement's row is always left at materialised defaults. **Updated the same day, in response to direct feedback**: a `replaced` requirement was originally always treated the same as `added` too (unlike clone lineage's mechanical reliability, a "replaces" mapping's relationship type is an open-vocabulary human judgement call this code had no way to evaluate on its own) — but the feedback correctly pointed out this was too blunt when a Compliance Manager has already judged two requirements genuinely equivalent (e.g. an old "IPX6 water ingress" requirement explicitly linked to a new "IP67 water ingress" requirement). Fixed with a two-gate mechanism rather than trusting the mapping's mere existence: `ComplianceMappingRelationshipTypeDefinition.implies_equivalence` (new column, org-level, Compliance-Manager-only) must be `True` for the specific mapping, *and* the officer performing that specific migration must separately confirm the specific requirement id via `ProjectComplianceMigrationRequest.confirmed_replacement_requirement_ids` (validated against a fresh diff, 400 on anything stale/ineligible) — never automatic, never silent, so §19's "must not imply... satisfying" guarantee still holds. A confirmed `replaced` carry-forward's `requires_reassessment` is always reported `True` (unlike `unchanged`'s conditional reporting), since the wording did genuinely change even when judged immaterial. See `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 11 follow-up entry and `models.py`'s own Phase 11 design-decisions section for the full account.
- **A carried-forward `APPROVED`/`PENDING_APPROVAL` row is downgraded by reusing `invalidate_approval_if_in_flight` verbatim** — exactly the "future caller" Phase 9's own docstring for that function already anticipated for "a standard version change." `approval_decided_at`/`approval_decided_by`/`decision_note` are deliberately not copied onto the new row (a fresh decision is needed once it's re-submitted); `assessed_at`/`assessed_by`/`applicability_set_at`/`applicability_set_by` *are* copied, preserving the true provenance of the underlying assessment.
- **The migration endpoint is gated `compliance_officer`/`PROJECT_MANAGER` on the project router, not `compliance_manager` on the org router** — deliberately different from the org router's own initial-assignment endpoint. Choosing which standard to track is a Compliance Manager decision (§26); rolling an already-tracked standard forward to a newer version is project-level maintenance of an existing assignment, the same class of action as everything else already on `project_router.py`.
- **Two new read-only MCP tools** (`compliance_list_requirement_mappings`, `compliance_get_standard_version_diff`); **no tool for the migration action itself** — a significant, wide-blast-radius mutation, and nothing in this phase's spec asked for one, mirroring Phase 9's identical approve/reject/submit-for-approval exclusion.
- **No SOC 2 policy-document edits needed** — the migration action and mapping/diff endpoints reuse `require_module_role`/`services.audit.log_event`/the existing soft-delete convention verbatim; no new secret, credential, PII, or retention-relevant path is introduced. Checked and recorded explicitly per this repo's own standing rule, not skipped.
- **No `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` changes** — neither script references any Compliance Module model today, so there was nothing to reconcile against Phase 11's two new tables; seeding Compliance Module data (mapping/relationship-type vocabulary included) remains Phase 15's job, per this plan's own repeatedly-stated precedent.
- **Tests**: `backend/tests/test_compliance_mapping_and_version_impact.py` (27 tests total: 21 original + 6 added for the same-day carry-forward follow-up) covers the relationship-type vocabulary's CRUD/RBAC/delete-with-reassignment/`implies_equivalence` toggling, requirement-mapping CRUD/visibility-from-both-sides/navigability/cross-org-isolation/audit-logging, the diff's five categories built from real cloned-and-then-independently-edited trees (not fabricated JSON) plus order-independence and `implies_equivalence` surfacing on `replaced` entries, and the full migration action (carry-forward + downgrade, modified/added at defaults, confirmed-and-eligible `replaced` carry-forward, not-confirmed `replaced` defaults, 400 on a non-equivalence relationship type or an id outside the `replaced` set, the old assignment provably untouched, validation, RBAC, cross-project isolation, audit logging). `backend/tests/test_compliance_data_model.py`'s module-registration assertion was updated for ten tools; `backend/tests/test_module_mcp_tools.py`'s real-registry compliance section was extended to both new tools. Full backend suite re-run afterward (twice — once per round) with no other DB-touching process active to confirm no regression (`test_schema_migrations_match_models.py` included both times, confirming migration 0031 matches the models with zero drift). `ruff check` clean on every touched/new file.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 11" entry and its same-day follow-up entry for the full account, including both identify→verify→remediate passes.
- **A further, separate same-day change relocated this phase's own files**: the six migrations (0026-0031) now live at `backend/app/modules/compliance/migrations/`, and this module's seven test files now live at `backend/app/modules/compliance/tests/` — both moved out of the flat, shared core directories they were originally written into, as the first worked example of a new systemic module-system convention (not a Compliance-specific one-off). See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Module system follow-up: first-party migrations/tests colocated in each module's own directory" entry for the full account — every file/test reference elsewhere in this Phase 11 section (and in this plan's own "Reused Existing Patterns"/Phase 1 sections) that still names the old `backend/alembic/versions/`/`backend/tests/` paths is describing where those files were *written*, at the time each phase was implemented, not necessarily where they *currently* live.

### Phase 12 — Frontend: Compliance Manager Surfaces

**Satisfies:** UI portions of §2–§6, §19 — org-level standards/versions/requirements/required-actions/cross-mapping management.

- Built as a **Tier A installed module** (`frontend/src/modules/compliance/`) per Phase 3 — direct reuse of `DirectoryTable`, `FilterPanel`, `Modal`, `SidePanel`, form components.
- Storybook coverage + Playwright e2e per this repo's standing testing requirements.
- Consult `docs/ux-style-guide.md` before building any new UI pattern shape (confirmation tiers, settings hierarchy, etc.) — do not invent a bespoke pattern where an existing shared one applies.

#### Phase 12 notes (completed 2026-09-06)

Implemented as specified, with one significant deviation the spec text's own terseness left for this session to resolve, plus a number of smaller judgment calls — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **Phase 3's Tier A `installedModules`/`buildModuleRoutes` mechanism is project-scoped end to end and has no org-level equivalent** — `useProjectEnabledModules`/`Layout.tsx`'s nav rendering/`App.tsx`'s route splicing all key off a `projectId` parsed from the URL, returning nothing on any org-admin page. Rather than build new generic org-level nav/routing infrastructure as a side effect of this phase (legitimate future module-system work, disproportionate here), `ComplianceAdminPanel` is mounted as a new `"compliance"` group inside the existing `OrgAdminPage.tsx` (`/orgs/:orgId/admin/compliance`), reusing that page's own established "fetch, catch a 403/404, degrade gracefully" pattern for a section not every viewer can use. Tier A's actual definition (installed at build time, direct shared-component imports) is still satisfied — only the routing/nav-discovery half of Phase 3's mechanism doesn't apply, since it has no org-scoped analogue. No fake `installedModules` registry entry was added purely for appearances, since it would never be exercised.
- **IA is three `Tabs` (Standards / Action types / Mapping types), not four, and not a `ResourceMenu`** — three is inside the style guide's own `Tabs` threshold. Cross-standard requirement mappings (§19) have no standalone tab: a mapping is inherently "viewed from a requirement," so mapping CRUD lives in `RequirementMappingsModal`, opened from a requirement's own row in the tree, with a three-level cascading standard→version→requirement picker for the target (no cross-org requirement search endpoint exists to type-ahead against instead).
- **Standard/version drill-down replaces the tab's content column in place; it is not a nested `SidePanel`** — `SidePanel`'s own 420px max-width (confirmed by reading the component) is too narrow for a requirement-tree editor. `StandardsPanel` holds a narrow `SidePanel` for standard fields + a flat versions list, and a full-width `VersionWorkspace` drill-down (with its own "← Back" control) for the version/requirement-tree editor — two different depths of navigation, not one over-loaded panel.
- **The requirement tree is assembled client-side** (`api.ts::buildRequirementTree`) from the backend's flat, DFS-ordered `GET .../requirements` list — confirmed by reading `router.py::_flatten_requirements_dfs` that no nested-tree endpoint exists. Required actions are fetched lazily per requirement on first expand (no bulk per-version endpoint exists either), not eagerly for the whole tree.
- **`DefinitionList` gained a small, generic `renderExtra?: (item: T) => ReactNode` slot** (rendered between the text field(s) and the reorder/delete buttons, default `undefined`, every pre-existing call site unaffected) rather than a boolean-specific fork or a bolted-on field type — needed for `ComplianceMappingRelationshipTypeDefinition.implies_equivalence`, a field its existing text-only `fields` contract can't express. `MappingTypesPanel` renders a `ToggleSwitch` there, calling the update endpoint with both `name` and `implies_equivalence` together (the backend's `PATCH` replaces both), independently of `DefinitionList`'s own rename flow.
- **No client-side "can I manage this" precomputation** — mutating controls always render; a 403 surfaces as an error toast, matching `OrgAdminPage`'s own existing posture for its other admin-only sections, since there is no existing "my effective roles for this org, including module roles" endpoint to check against without adding one.
- **Owner reassignment has no dedicated UI** — the standard edit form always resubmits the current `owner_id` unchanged (the backend requires it on every `PATCH`, no partial-update semantics). §2 only requires "Owner" exist as an attribute; a real reassignment control needs `UserAutocomplete` wired up for a single field with no other consumer yet this phase — flagged explicitly rather than left a silent gap.
- **Two real, pre-existing bugs found and fixed while verifying this phase against a live `tests/container` stack** (repo's standing "fix, don't defer" rule): (1) `tests/container`'s backend image had never been rebuilt since Phase 5, so `GET /orgs/{id}/modules` returned `[]` and Compliance wasn't actually a registered module in that environment — rebuilding it applied migrations 0025→0031 cleanly and made Compliance real there for the first time; (2) that rebuild then surfaced a genuine, previously-undetected bug: `OrgAdminPage.tsx::grantOrgRole` sent `{ role }` with no `user_id`, but the backend's `OrgRoleAssign` schema has required `user_id` since before this plan started — every org-role grant via the Users table's Roles dropdown had been silently 422ing, uncaught because the Storybook story asserting this call's shape mocked `api.post` unconditionally (accepting the bug's own payload as "correct") and no Playwright spec had ever driven this exact click against a real, schema-enforcing backend. Fixed (added the missing field); the story's pinned assertion and two Playwright specs (`module-contributed-roles.spec.ts`, `org-admin-modules.spec.ts`) were updated to match reality — both of those specs' own docstrings had already, explicitly said to revisit once a real module existed, so this wasn't scope creep, it was overdue work from Phase 1/2/5 becoming due. See `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 12 entry for the full account of both findings.
- No backend files were changed this phase (frontend-only, per its own spec, and no gap was found that needed even a small backend addition).
- **Same-day follow-up, direct feedback**: the Playwright spec was relocated from the flat `tests/playwright/tests/e2e-workflows/` bucket to `tests/playwright/tests/modules/compliance/`, extending Phase 11's own backend migrations/tests colocation convention (see docs/decisions.md's "Module system follow-up: first-party migrations/tests colocated in each module's own directory" entry) to Playwright e2e specs. `playwright.config.ts`'s `testDir: "./tests"` matches recursively with no `testMatch` restriction, so no config change was needed — only the spec's own relative import of `helpers.ts` (`./helpers` → `../../e2e-workflows/helpers`, since `helpers.ts` itself stays put as shared infrastructure, not colocated per-module). Re-run from its new location to confirm: 1 passed. A future module's own e2e specs should follow the same `tests/playwright/tests/modules/<key>/` shape rather than adding to `e2e-workflows/`.
- **Same-day follow-up, Storybook coverage gap**: Phase 12 as originally landed shipped only `ComplianceAdminPanel.stories.tsx` (7 integration-style stories exercising flows through the composed panel) — a repo-wide audit of every `.tsx` component/page against its `.stories.tsx` counterpart found this module was the only exception to this codebase's 100%-consistent "every component file has its own dedicated story file" convention. Added one dedicated, isolated story file per remaining component (`StandardsPanel`, `VersionWorkspace`, `RequirementTree`, `RequirementMappingsModal`, `VersionDiffModal`, `ActionTypesPanel`, `MappingTypesPanel`), each mounting that component directly with its own mocked `api` responses rather than drilling down through `ComplianceAdminPanel`'s tabs, so the composed file's integration stories and these isolated ones don't duplicate coverage. No component `.tsx` file needed changing to make this possible. While writing `StandardsPanel.stories.tsx`, found a real pre-existing bug: the standard-edit save handler spread its whole form-values object — which still carries the create-only `reference` field — into `updateStandard`'s payload, so every standard edit silently resent the standard's original reference even though the edit form has no reference input at all; harmless only because the backend's update schema ignores unknown fields rather than rejecting them. Initially flagged rather than fixed (per the sub-agent's own remit not to touch component files without checking back first); verified against `schemas.py`'s `ComplianceStandardUpdate` (no `reference` field, no `extra: forbid`, confirming "silently ignored, not broken") and then fixed in the same session per this repo's standing "fix, don't defer" rule — `StandardsPanel.tsx`'s edit `onSave` now destructures only the fields `updateStandard` actually declares (`name`/`description`/`issuing_organisation`), and the story's pinned assertion was updated to match the corrected payload. See `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 12 entry for the full account.
- **Later follow-up (2026-09-07), stale by design, not by mistake — read before assuming the mounting mechanism described above is still current**: the "Phase 3's Tier A `installedModules`/`buildModuleRoutes` mechanism ... has no org-level equivalent ... `ComplianceAdminPanel` is mounted as a new group inside the existing `OrgAdminPage.tsx` ... directly" bullet above accurately describes this phase's own decision *at the time* — left as-is, not rewritten, per this file's own instructions. A same-day-unrelated, cross-cutting frontend-infra follow-up (not a numbered phase; see Phase 14's own equivalent pointer below and `docs/decisions.md`'s "Module system follow-up: dynamic org-admin panel registration" entry) has since generalised this: `ComplianceAdminPanel` is now declared via `frontend/src/modules/compliance/module.ts`'s `orgAdminSections`, and `OrgAdminPage.tsx` renders it through one generic lookup rather than a hardcoded `activeGroup === "compliance"` block/static import. The underlying gap this bullet named — no generic org-level *nav-rail* mechanism (as opposed to an `OrgAdminPage.tsx` section) — is still open.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 12" entry for the full account, including every file touched and the complete test run record.

### Phase 13 — Frontend: Project Compliance View

**Satisfies:** §21 (Project Compliance View), UI portions of §7–§10, §12, §13.

- Assign standards, applicability tree with visually distinct explicit/inherited/overridden states (§9's explicit UI requirement), assessment, evidence, approval/sign-off.
- Storybook + Playwright.

#### Phase 13 notes (completed 2026-09-07)

Implemented as specified, with two real bugs found and fixed in the module system's own shared infrastructure (not Compliance-specific) plus a number of judgment calls — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **Unlike Phase 12, this phase's UI is genuinely project-scoped, so Phase 3's real `installedModules`/`buildModuleRoutes`/`useProjectEnabledModules`/`Layout.tsx` nav mechanism applies directly** — confirmed by reading Phase 3's own spec and implementation before designing anything, per this plan's own instruction. `frontend/src/modules/registry.ts`'s `installedModules` array (empty since Phase 3) got its first real entry.
- **Found and fixed: `ModuleFrontendManifest.nav_path`'s own documented `"{project_id}"` placeholder syntax had never actually been interpolated by anything** — `backend/app/modules/compliance/module.py` needed its first `frontend_manifest` (Phase 12 never needed one), and `routers/projects.py::list_project_enabled_modules` was passing the literal placeholder string straight through uninterpolated (confirmed via `test_module_frontend_integration.py`'s existing fixture, which only ever used a plain literal `nav_path`). Fixed generically in that one project-scoped endpoint (the org-scoped `GET /orgs/{id}/modules` deliberately leaves the placeholder as-is, having no single project in scope) — two new tests added directly to that shared test file, not a Compliance-specific test file, since this is module-system infrastructure.
- **Found and fixed a real race condition in `App.tsx`'s own route-splicing, invisible until a real Tier A project-scoped route existed to trigger it**: `useProjectEnabledModules` starts at `[]` while its fetch is in flight, so a fresh navigation straight to a module's nav-rail link could hit `<Routes>`'s wildcard `<Navigate to="/projects"/>` fallback before the fetch resolved and contributed the real route — bouncing the user straight back to `/projects` every time. Caught only by the Playwright spec below, not Storybook (`App.stories.tsx`'s `TierARoutingHarness` pre-seeds `enabledModules` synchronously, so the race has no window there). Fixed generically: the hook now returns `{ modules, loaded }`; `App.tsx`'s wildcard route renders a `Spinner` instead of `Navigate` specifically while `projectId` is set and `modulesLoaded` is still false, leaving every other path's behaviour (including every already-working non-module project page) completely unaffected. Also fixed, found in the same pass: `Layout.stories.tsx`'s own mock never covered `/enabled-modules`, throwing an uncaught rejection into every one of its project-route stories.
- **A real UI staleness bug found by running the Playwright spec end to end**: the assignment detail's "Overall compliance status" card didn't refresh after assessing/approving a requirement, since it rendered a snapshot `status` prop fetched once by the parent list. Fixed by lifting it into local state, refreshed via a new optional `ApplicabilityTree` callback (`onAssessmentChanged`) fired whenever a nested mutation settles — the top-level assignment list itself is deliberately *not* live-synced the same way, matching this app's existing "only refresh a list on its own explicit triggers" precedent elsewhere (e.g. `StandardsPanel`).
- **IA**: three top-level `Tabs` (Standards / Evidence / Outstanding) on `ProjectCompliancePage`, then a further two-`Tabs` drill-down (Requirements / Reviews) per selected assignment inside `ProjectComplianceDetail` — both groups well inside the style guide's `Tabs` threshold. "Outstanding" (non-compliant / pending-approval / reviews-due, all three already-built read-only cross-assignment endpoints with no prior frontend consumer) is new relative to Phase 12's IA, added specifically to satisfy §21's own "make it easy to determine what remains outstanding" without requiring every assignment to be opened in turn.
- **§9's mandatory explicit/inherited/overridden visual distinction is one shared component, `ApplicabilityBadge.tsx`**, imported by both the tree and the requirement detail panel, with its own dedicated story proving all three states render distinctly.
- **Deliberate scope trim, flagged rather than silently omitted (mirroring Phase 12's "owner reassignment" precedent): the §27 version-migration action has no frontend surface.** Not asked for by this phase's own spec bullets, not claimed by Phase 14 either, and a materially larger UI (diff preview, per-requirement carry-forward confirmation) than anything else this phase needed.
- **Evidence linkage from a requirement is a plain filtered `<select>` + link/unlink, not a second evidence-specific picker component** — the canonical evidence CRUD/file-attachment surface (reusing `FileAttachmentList`/`ResourcePickerModal` verbatim, per §13's "reuse existing attachment mechanisms") stays the top-level Evidence tab.
- **An archived assignment's requirement tree needed one small client-side join (`api.ts::resolveStandardIdForVersion`), not a new backend endpoint** — `GET .../status` (the normal source of `standard_id`) deliberately excludes archived assignments by design, so this walks the org's own standard/version catalogue instead, only for that one, rare case.
- **No client-side "can I manage this" precomputation** — mutating controls always render; a 403 surfaces as a toast, continuing Phase 12's exact posture.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 13" entry for the full account, including both bug write-ups and the complete file/test list. Full backend suite (949 tests) and full Storybook suite (88 files/750 tests) both re-run clean after every fix; the Playwright spec (`tests/playwright/tests/modules/compliance/project-compliance-view.spec.ts`) run twice back-to-back against a rebuilt `tests/container` stack to confirm idempotency.

### Phase 14 — Frontend: Org Compliance View + Dashboard

**Satisfies:** §22 (Organisation Compliance View), §23 (Compliance Dashboard).

- Cross-project compliance table with the filters §22 lists explicitly, drill-down (not aggregate-only, per §22/§23's explicit requirement), dashboard widgets.
- Storybook + Playwright.

#### Phase 14 notes (completed 2026-09-07)

Implemented as specified, with the judgment calls below recorded so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them — see `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 14" entry for the full account, including every schema/file touched.

- **RBAC: every new org-wide endpoint is `_require_manage`-gated (Compliance Manager), not a new, wider view-tier dependency** — §22's own "Compliance Managers and authorised users" text read alone could suggest a looser gate, but §26 (this module's own dedicated, authoritative Security and Permissions section) lists "View compliance across projects" specifically and only under Compliance Manager; Project Manager/Compliance Officer each get "View **project** compliance" (their own project), not a cross-project capability. Matches `list_all_project_compliance`'s (Phase 7) existing gate exactly — no widening, no narrowing.
- **A genuine pre-existing gap, closed in this phase rather than left for later: no flattened "outstanding Required Actions" listing existed at either scope.** Added `GET .../outstanding-required-actions` to both `project_router.py` (§21/§22 project-level, also wired into Phase 13's `OutstandingPanel.tsx` as a new fourth section) and `router.py` (§22's own org-wide ask). `OutstandingRequiredActionOut` is new, so it carries `project_id`/`project_name` from the start and is shared verbatim between both scopes — no `Org*Out` subclass needed for this one schema.
- **Business logic extracted into `service.py`, not duplicated**: `list_non_compliant_requirements_for_project`/`list_pending_approvals_for_project`/`list_outstanding_required_actions_for_project`/`list_reviews_due_for_project` (the first three moved out of `project_router.py`'s own pre-existing endpoints, verbatim; the fourth new) are each called once per project in the org by `router.py`'s five new endpoints, mirroring `list_all_project_compliance`'s existing cross-project join pattern. `list_reviews_due_for_project` gained an `include_upcoming` flag (default `False`, preserving prior behaviour) for §23's "Upcoming compliance deadlines/reviews" widget.
- **Schema design deliberately isn't uniform across the five org-wide listings, and that's intentional, not an inconsistency to "fix" later**: `NonCompliantRequirementOut`/`PendingApprovalOut`/`ComplianceEvidenceOut` predate this phase with passing Phase 12/13 project-scope consumers, so each gets an `Org*Out` subclass adding `project_id`/`project_name` rather than a retrofit onto the shared base (avoiding any response-shape change to an already-shipped, already-tested endpoint). `ComplianceReviewOut` gets a nested wrapper (`OrgReviewDueOut`) instead, since one standard-level review can legitimately be "due" for several projects at once — a flat subclass can't honestly carry a single `project_id`. `ProjectComplianceStatusOut` gets `project_name` added directly (no subclass) because it was already Phase 7's one exception to "project-scope-first," carrying a project-scope-redundant `project_id` from the start specifically for org-wide reuse.
- **New `GET .../recent-activity` endpoint (§23) reads `AuditEvent` rows already logged by Phase 7/9's assessment/approval endpoints** — an explicit six-action allowlist (`assessed`/`applicability_changed`/`submitted_for_approval`/`approved`/`rejected`/`approval_invalidated`), not "every event of this entity type," so an unrelated future action doesn't silently start appearing in this dashboard widget.
- **IA: a new flat top-level `ResourceMenu` group ("Compliance overview," `/orgs/:orgId/admin/compliance-overview`), sibling to Phase 12's "Compliance" group — not a fourth tab on `ComplianceAdminPanel`.** Checked the UX style guide's "when one resource-menu group outgrows itself, split into more flat groups — don't nest" addendum before deciding; `ComplianceAdminPanel`'s own Phase 12 docstring had already flagged the org-wide dashboard as explicitly out of its scope. `OrgCompliancePanel` itself uses three of its own `Tabs` (Dashboard / Compliance by standard / Outstanding), mirroring Phase 13's own three-tab IA.
- **Drill-down "to individual requirements" reuses the existing per-project Compliance page (`/projects/{id}/modules/compliance`) as its destination — no parallel org-scope requirement browser was built.** Every project reference across the dashboard/table/outstanding views links there, since Phase 13 already drills that page all the way down to individual requirements. "Standards with the most outstanding issues" (§23) is computed client-side from data already fetched for the dashboard, mirroring Phase 12's `buildRequirementTree` client-aggregation precedent — no dedicated backend endpoint.
- **No client-side "can I manage this" precomputation** — continues Phase 12/13's exact posture; a 403 surfaces as a toast/degraded panel, not a pre-check.
- **Two Testing-Library/Playwright query-ambiguity bugs found and fixed while writing this phase's own tests** (test-authoring mistakes, not product bugs, per this repo's "identify → verify → remediate" practice): broad `getByText`/`getByRole` matches in new stories/spec also matching a filter dropdown's own `<option>` text (fixed by scoping to `within(canvas.getByRole("table"))` / a specific button locator), and two fixture rows sharing the same `requirement_name` across `OutstandingPanel`/`OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel` stories making a plain text match ambiguous once a fourth section was added (fixed by asserting each row's own more specific text).
- Full backend suite (956 passed, 0 failed — run via `docker compose exec backend pytest -q`, the authoritative way per that stack's own docstring, since a bare host-level `pytest` can't resolve the `mailhog` hostname the notification tests need), full Storybook suite (92 files/765 tests), and all three Compliance module Playwright specs (Phases 12/13/14) run clean; the new Phase 14 spec run twice back-to-back against a rebuilt `tests/container` stack to confirm idempotency.
- **Later follow-up (2026-09-07), stale by design, not by mistake**: the "`OrgCompliancePanel` itself uses three of its own `Tabs`" bullet above and this phase's `OrgCompliancePanel.tsx` docstring both described `OrgCompliancePanel` as mounted "the same way Phase 12 mounted `ComplianceAdminPanel`" — directly, by a hardcoded `OrgAdminPage.tsx` render block. Left as-is above, not rewritten, per this file's own instructions; see Phase 12's own equivalent pointer note for the mechanism that replaced it (a same-day-unrelated, cross-cutting frontend-infra follow-up, not a numbered phase): both panels are now declared via `frontend/src/modules/compliance/module.ts`'s `orgAdminSections`, rendered by `OrgAdminPage.tsx` through one generic lookup. That follow-up also fixed a real, until-then-undetected bug this phase's own hardcoded approach carried: because the two Compliance groups were unconditional array entries, they stayed visible in Org Admin's nav even after an admin disabled the Compliance module for that org — the generic mechanism filters by the same module-enablement state the Modules toggle itself reads. See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Module system follow-up: dynamic org-admin panel registration" entry for the full account.

- PDF/CSV report integration alongside existing report generation.

- Finalize `docs/mcp-server.md`'s Phase 4 section with the actual compliance tools shipped (`compliance_list_standards(organization_id)`, `compliance_get_standard_version(organization_id, standard_id, version_id)`, `compliance_list_requirements(organization_id, standard_id, version_id)`, `compliance_get_project_status(project_id)`, `compliance_list_non_compliant_requirements(project_id)`, `compliance_list_expiring_evidence(project_id)`, `compliance_list_pending_approvals(project_id)` — update this list if any phase's actual tool names/params ended up differing), matching that doc's existing tool-table format.

**Phase 15 notes (deviations from the spec above — see `docs/decisions.md`'s matching entry for the full account):**
- The report generator was built as a new module-local file, `app/modules/compliance/reports.py`, not an extension of core `services/reports.py`/`routers/reports.py` — confirmed as the established convention by checking Phase 8's evidence file-upload integration and Phase 4's MCP manifest builder, per this section's own instruction to do so rather than guess.
- `backend/scripts/seed_e2e_dataset.py` was deliberately **not** updated — all three Compliance Playwright specs already author their own dynamically-named fixtures via the UI rather than depending on fixed seeded compliance data, confirmed by reading the specs directly before deciding, per this section's own conditional instruction.
- `docs/mcp-server.md` needed no edit — its existing ten-tool table was checked against `module.py`'s live `mcp_tools` tuple and found already fully accurate (this section's own list above is itself slightly stale, e.g. it names only seven of the ten actual tools — `docs/mcp-server.md` itself, not this plan doc, is the up-to-date source).
- Two real gaps were found during the §32 walk and fixed as part of this phase (not left as separately-tracked follow-ups): evidence revalidation history and requirement/required-action evidence linkage had no representation in the project export bundle at all before this phase (Compliance had no export integration yet, so there was nothing to have carried it before); and no compliance report existed at all, which is this phase's own core deliverable rather than a pre-existing defect.
- A small, proportionate frontend addition ("Download PDF/CSV report" buttons on `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx`/`OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`) was added alongside the two new backend endpoints, with matching Storybook stories and Playwright coverage — not explicitly called for above, but the standing UI-feature testing rule applies once a UI entry point exists, and a report endpoint with no UI entry point anywhere in the product would have been a real, avoidable gap.

### Phase 17 — Post-Review UX Quick Fixes

**Satisfies:** the first human walkthrough of the shipped module (`docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module, human review follow-ups" entry has the full reasoning behind every choice below — read it before picking this phase back up, not just this checklist).

Five independent sub-items, all now complete:

- **17a.** [x] Done. `.side-grid` DOM-order bug (`FilterPanel` must be the *second* child of `.side-grid`, per `theme.css:468-477`/`685-693`) fixed in `frontend/src/modules/compliance/EvidencePanel.tsx`.
