### Phase 18 — "Compliance Standards" as a First-Class, Cross-Org, Project-Like Nav Entity

**Satisfies:** the human-review request to promote Standards to "a special type of project" — its own top-level nav tab, a cross-org list like Projects, and a per-entity left-nav workspace once you open one — rather than three clicks deep inside Org Admin.

Depends on Phase 17 being complete (relocates `StandardsPanel.tsx`/`ComplianceAdminPanel.tsx`, which 17a/17b touch).

- New top-level nav rail item **"Compliance Standards"** (`Layout.tsx`'s Global section, sibling to Projects), route `/standards`, gated by a new nav-visibility check (not shown unconditionally like Projects — this tab is niche, Projects is universal).
- **Nav-visibility check**: new compliance-module-owned `GET /api/v1/compliance/nav-visibility` → `{ visible: bool }` (keeps compliance-specific logic inside the compliance module rather than bolting fields onto core `/auth/me/memberships`). True if, for any org the caller belongs to, compliance is effectively enabled AND (caller is `compliance_manager`/org admin/server admin, OR that org has ≥1 non-archived standard). Frontend: one hook fetched once at `Layout.tsx` shell level, same pattern as `hasFavourites`, gating both this tab and Phase 19's.
- **`/standards`** — new `StandardListPage.tsx`, modeled on `ProjectListPage.tsx`. No cross-org backend listing endpoint exists (every compliance route requires `organization_id` in the path) — fetch `GET /orgs?mine=true`, fan out `listStandards(orgId)` per org in parallel, let a 404 (module disabled there) drop that org silently. `organization_name` per row, `showOrgColumn` when >1 org (same convention as `ProjectListItem`). Correct `.side-grid` order from the start. "New standard" opens the combined dialog from 17b (org picker first if the caller can manage standards in more than one org). Each row → `/standards/:standardId`. A per-org "Compliance settings" entry → `/standards/settings/:orgId`.
- **`/standards/:standardId`** — the per-standard workspace. New backend endpoint `GET /api/v1/compliance/standards/{standard_id}` (no org prefix) resolves a standard by id alone and returns its `organization_id`, mirroring exactly how a project's own id resolves to its org today — every existing org-scoped nested call (versions, requirements) threads that id through unchanged. `Layout.tsx`: when `standardId` is present in the URL (same trigger pattern as today's `projectId` check), render a new "Standard" left-nav section — Overview/Details, Versions (→ the existing `VersionWorkspace`, reused as-is), History — as a sibling structural pattern to "Project," not nested inside it.
- **`/standards/settings/:orgId`** — org-level compliance settings workspace: `ActionTypesPanel`/`MappingTypesPanel` reused near-unchanged (already take only `orgId`), re-hosted as a routed page. Internal navigation between the two uses **`ResourceMenu`** (`frontend/src/components/ResourceMenu.tsx` — this codebase's existing "one page, persistent side menu of a handful of named sections" pattern, already used by `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`/`OrgAdminPage.tsx`/`ServerManagementPage.tsx`), not `Tabs` and not a nav-rail section — a nav-rail section is reserved for an actual project-like *entity* (a single Standard), not a fixed pair of org-wide settings screens.
- **Retire the old location**: remove the `compliance` `orgAdminSection` (`module.ts`) and `ComplianceAdminPanel.tsx`'s org-admin-hosted wrapper — fully superseded.
- **Docs**: record the IA decision in `docs/decisions.md`; check whether `docs/ux-style-guide.md` needs a new named pattern for this second "project-like drill-down" entity type; update `docs/solution-architecture.md` if this changes the documented navigation architecture.
- **Tests**: backend permission-boundary tests for the two new endpoints; Playwright e2e for tab visibility on/off, one-step standard+version creation, standard workspace navigation, and org compliance settings; Storybook stories for new page-level components.

#### Phase 18 notes (completed 2026-09-08)

Implemented per the spec above, with three real deviations recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **The nav-visibility hook and the "Standard" nav-rail section were not implemented as spec'd on first pass, and had to be corrected.** The spec's own "one hook fetched once at `Layout.tsx` shell level" and "`Layout.tsx`: when `standardId` is present..." language was read too literally on the first implementation pass — it hardcoded a `useComplianceNavVisibility()` Context import and an inline `/^\/standards\//` regex/"Standard" label directly into `Layout.tsx`, exactly the "module coupled into a core file" shape `docs/compliance-module-plan.md`'s own "Design history" section already records as rejected twice during the module system's original design. Caught and fixed before this phase was reported done (see `docs/decisions.md`'s "Phase 18 complete" entry for the full account): `frontend/src/modules/types.ts`'s `TierAModuleDefinition` gained `globalNavItems`/`standaloneWorkspaces`, mirroring `orgAdminSections`'s existing "module hands the parent a render function" shape; `Layout.tsx` now contains zero compliance-specific code. The nav-visibility value itself is served by a self-contained hook backed by a tiny module-level `useSyncExternalStore` store (`useComplianceNavVisibility.ts`), not a Context/Provider `Layout.tsx` would have to mount — closer in spirit to "fetched once, cached" than to `hasFavourites`'s literal Context shape, since nothing outside the compliance module needs to read this value.
- **The identical mistake recurred once more, in `App.tsx`, and was caught in a second review pass after the `Layout.tsx` fix.** `App.tsx` directly imported `StandardListPage`/`StandardWorkspacePage`/`ComplianceSettingsPage` and hardcoded three `/standards*` routes for them, and those three page components lived under `frontend/src/pages/` despite depending heavily on `modules/compliance/` internals — unlike the correct existing precedent (`ProjectCompliancePage` lives inside `modules/compliance/` itself). Fixed the same way: `TierAModuleDefinition` gained `globalRoutes?: TierAModuleRoute[]` (the always-mounted counterpart to the existing project-gated `routes`), `App.tsx` now consumes it generically with no compliance import at all, and all three pages (+ stories) moved into `frontend/src/modules/compliance/`, registered via `module.ts`.
- **A third small backend addition beyond the two the spec names**: `GET .../standards/{id}/history` (org-scoped, added to the *existing* `router.py`, not the new global router) backs the "History" section of the Standard workspace, which the spec requires but doesn't say how to serve. Mirrors `project_router.py::get_requirement_history`'s exact shape against `compliance_standard`-entity audit events.

Also not literally spec'd but implied by "fully superseded": `StandardsPanel.tsx`/`ComplianceAdminPanel.tsx` were deleted outright (not left in place unused), and a pre-existing `.side-grid` order bug in the deleted `StandardsPanel.tsx` (FilterPanel before content, backwards from every other list page) was not carried forward into `StandardListPage.tsx` — found and fixed in the same pass, along with the identical bug in the still-live `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`.

Playwright e2e specs were rewritten/updated (`compliance-standards-management.spec.ts` fully rewritten for the new IA; `project-compliance-view.spec.ts`/`org-compliance-view.spec.ts` had their now-defunct org-admin setup steps replaced) but **not executed against a live stack in this session** — no Docker Compose stack was available in this sandboxed environment. Whoever next has the stack up should run them before treating this phase as fully closed end-to-end; see `docs/decisions.md`'s entry for exactly what was and wasn't verified.

### Phase 19 — "Organisation Overview" Page

**Satisfies:** the human-review request for a new org-wide overview page at the same nav depth as Projects, plus (added during plan review) basic org stats — project/requirement/user counts and total file storage — that belong there regardless of whether compliance applies to that org.

Depends on Phase 18 (relocates `OrgCompliancePanel.tsx`/`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`; conceptually follows the same nav-visibility groundwork, though see below — this tab's own visibility is deliberately different).

- New top-level nav rail item **"Organisation Overview"** (Global section, sibling to Projects/Compliance Standards). Unlike Phase 18's tab, this one is **not** compliance-gated — it carries general-purpose org stats useful to any org member, so it's visible unconditionally to any user who belongs to at least one org (same precedent as the always-shown Projects link).
- Reuse the existing `/orgs` auto-redirect convention (single org → straight in; multiple orgs → picker) for a new `/org-overview` entry point → `/orgs/:orgId/overview`.
- **New stats header**: project count, requirement count, org member count, total uploaded file size. **Every count is scoped to what the calling user can actually see, not the org's raw totals — except an org admin (or server admin), who gets the real org-wide totals.** New backend endpoint `GET /orgs/{organization_id}/overview-stats`:
  - Org admin/server admin: plain org-wide counts (`count(Project)`/`count(Requirement) join Project`/`sum(FileAsset.size_bytes)`, all `where organization_id = :id`, regardless of the caller's own per-project roles).
  - Everyone else: reuse `_accessible_project_ids(db, current_user.id)` (`backend/app/routers/projects.py:147` — the exact visibility computation `list_projects` already uses) filtered to this org, for both the project and requirement counts, and for file size (org-resource files, `is_org_resource=True`, plus files attached within that same accessible-project set via the existing `RequirementFile`/`RequirementActionFile` join path). Move `_accessible_project_ids` to a shared service (or import it) rather than duplicating the visibility logic.
  - Org member count needs no admin/member branch — `list_org_users`'s own permission (`require_org_role(ORG_ADMIN, PROJECT_CREATOR, MEMBER)`, i.e. any role at all) already lets any member see the full list.
  - New frontend stat-row component — check whether `OrgComplianceDashboard`'s existing stat cards are already reusable before adding a second implementation.
- Below the stats header, compliance content — today's `OrgCompliancePanel`'s three views (Dashboard / Compliance by standard / Outstanding) — shown only when compliance applies to this org, navigated via **`ResourceMenu`** (not `Tabs` — same reasoning as Phase 18's settings page: this is the "one page, persistent side menu of named sections" shape `ResourceMenu` already exists for). Fix `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel`'s `.side-grid` order as part of the move.
- Remove the `compliance-overview` `orgAdminSection` from `module.ts` — superseded.
- **Tests**: backend test for the stats endpoint — a member with partial project access sees counts matching only their accessible set, while an org admin/server admin with the same partial personal access sees true org-wide totals — plus permission boundary; Playwright coverage for reaching the page, correct scoped-vs-admin stats, and the compliance `ResourceMenu` sections when applicable.

#### Phase 19 notes (completed 2026-09-08)

Implemented per the spec above, with a few deviations/additions recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:

- **This page is deliberately *not* built through the module-registration mechanism at all** — it's a core route/nav link (`pages/OrgOverviewPage.tsx`, wired into `App.tsx`/`Layout.tsx` directly, the same unconditional treatment `/projects` gets), since its stats apply regardless of which modules are installed. Only the *module-contributed content below the stats header* goes through a registration mechanism — a new `orgOverviewSections` field on `TierAModuleDefinition`, reusing `orgAdminSections`' exact `{key, label, render({orgId})}` shape rather than a new interface. `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: project-like drill-down entities" gained a short paragraph making this distinction explicit (a singleton per-org page isn't a drill-down entity), since the two could otherwise be confused given how similar the underlying extension mechanism is.
- **The stats endpoint's admin/server-admin bypass is scoped correctly against I-M-05**: `GET /orgs/{id}/overview-stats` is gated by `require_org_role(ORG_ADMIN, PROJECT_CREATOR, MEMBER)` — the same bar as `list_org_users` — never `require_org_admin_or_server_admin` (that dependency's own docstring restricts it to the one documented carve-out, creating an org's initial user). A server admin with no real role in this org still gets 403. The "sees full totals" branch only decides what a caller who already passed that gate sees, matching the human-review decision's exact wording ("an org admin is the exception... should see the raw proper totals") without reopening the org-scoped-content boundary I-M-05 established.
- **`_accessible_project_ids` was imported into `routers/orgs.py` from `routers/projects.py`, not relocated to a shared service** — the plan text's own "(or import it)" alternative, chosen because moving ~150 lines of heavily-relied-upon RBAC logic (four existing call sites) for one new read-only endpoint was a disproportionate refactor risk. See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Phase 19 complete" entry for the full reasoning and the precedent this follows (`routers/projects.py` already imports underscore-prefixed helpers from `services/rbac.py`).
- **A real, disclosed gap**: a scoped (non-admin) caller's `total_file_size_bytes` excludes `ReviewComment` discussion-thread attachments (`CommentFile`) — resolving `ReviewComment`'s polymorphic `target_id` back to a project id would need a three-way branch per target type, judged disproportionate to what these attachments actually weigh. Documented in the endpoint's and schema's own docstrings; a strict undercount, never an over-exposure.
- **Two extractions beyond what the spec named, following this repo's own "extract and update the existing call site" rule** rather than adding a second implementation: `components/StatCard.tsx` (out of `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`, which now imports the shared version too — the spec's own "check whether OrgComplianceDashboard's existing stat cards are already reusable before adding a second implementation" line, answered "not directly, since a core page can't import from `modules/compliance/` — so extract it") and `utils/formatFileSize.ts` (out of `ProjectFilesPage.tsx`, whose own comment had already named "a second call site" as the promotion trigger this phase turned out to be).
- **`OrgListPage.tsx` gained a `target?: "admin" | "overview"` prop** rather than a second, near-duplicate component, reusing its existing single-org/multi-org auto-redirect fetch logic for `/org-overview`. Triggered a minor TypeScript/Storybook wrinkle (giving the whole props parameter a `= {}` default broke `Meta<typeof OrgListPage>`'s `Args` inference) — fixed by keeping the parameter itself required with all-optional fields instead; see `docs/decisions.md` for the exact mechanism.
- Not executed in this session: the new/updated Playwright specs (`org-overview.spec.ts`, `org-compliance-view.spec.ts`'s updated IA) were not run against a live stack — no full e2e dataset was seeded/exercised end-to-end here, the same caveat Phase 18's own session recorded. Everything else was verified against the real `tests/container` Docker Compose stack: `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean, a targeted Storybook run (8 changed/new story files, 35/35 passing), the **full backend pytest suite (998 passed, 0 failed)**, and `ruff check .` clean repo-wide — see `docs/decisions.md`'s "Phase 19 complete" entry for the concurrent-pytest-run false-failure this session hit and re-ran past on the way to that clean number.

## Compliance Module Phases 20-26 — Second Human-Review Round

Source: direct human review of the shipped module, captured 2026-09-08, after using Phases 17-19's own IA for a while. Unlike the first review round (Phases 17-19, which corrected navigation/IA problems), this round asks for real new capability (applicability defaults, import/export, working-group RBAC) alongside further UX correction — read each phase below in full before implementing; the reasoning is recorded inline rather than in a separate `docs/decisions.md` entry, since none of this has been implemented yet for there to be a decision to record. Every phase below was grounded directly against the current code (file:line references throughout) and against `docs/Compliance_Module_Requirements.md`'s numbered sections, not assumed — cite the same sections when implementing rather than re-deriving them.

### Phase 20 — Standard Applicability Defaults, Exceptions, and Project-Manager Assignment

**Satisfies:** the human-review request that a standard be able to say "applies to all projects by default, except..." rather than every project link being a fully manual, one-at-a-time act; and — the **default, primary path**, not a secondary addition — that a Project Manager, not only an org-scoped Compliance Manager, be able to assign a standard to their own project. Grounded in §7 (Applying Compliance to a Project) and §3/§26's role tables — confirmed by direct reading that §26's "at minimum" framing is a floor, not a ceiling: today's Phase 7 design (assignment is `compliance_manager`-only, `router.py:1485`'s `create_project_compliance`, org-scoped router) is consistent with §26's letter (Project Manager's own list there has no "assign standards" bullet) but not with its spirit, and §3 itself already qualifies Compliance Manager's assignment power as "subject to appropriate permissions" — leaving room for this extension rather than contradicting it. §11's own framing ("Project Managers can manage the project's compliance") already treats the Project Manager as the natural day-to-day owner of their project's compliance posture, which assigning a standard to that same project is a part of, not an exception to.

Today, confirmed directly: `ProjectCompliance` (`models.py:698-754`) has no "applies to all" concept at all — every project↔standard link is one explicit, manually-created row (`router.py:1485`). This phase adds two related but distinct capabilities, and the ordering below reflects which is the default path:

- **Project-Manager-initiated assignment is the default mechanism.** A `ProjectRole.PROJECT_MANAGER` may create a `ProjectCompliance` row for their own project against any `PUBLISHED` version of any non-archived standard in their project's org — a new endpoint on `project_router.py` (project-scoped, mirroring where every other project-compliance mutation already lives, rather than staying solely on the org-scoped router), gated by the same `_require_officer` composition Phase 7 already established (`compliance_officer` grant OR `PROJECT_MANAGER`). This is the everyday, expected way a project picks up a standard — a Project Manager browsing `/standards` or their own `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx` should be able to assign any published standard in their org without needing a Compliance Manager to act on their behalf first. The existing org-scoped Compliance-Manager assignment endpoint (`router.py:1485`) remains available alongside it, for a Compliance Manager assigning on a project's behalf or doing so in bulk — it does not go away, but it is the secondary/administrative path, not the default one.
- **A per-standard default-applicability setting, for org-wide mandates.** `ComplianceStandard` gains a mode — e.g. `applicability_default: "opt_in" | "applies_to_all_projects"`, default `"opt_in"` (i.e. Project-Manager-initiated assignment as above, project by project; every existing standard, including the seeded `ASA-1`, keeps today's fully-manual behaviour unchanged). Only a Compliance Manager (or org admin/server admin, via the existing `require_module_role` composition) may switch a standard to `"applies_to_all_projects"` — this is the org-wide, centrally-mandated case (e.g. a corporate security standard every project must track), distinct from and secondary to the Project-Manager-driven default above, matching §3's "Assign standards to projects" being a Compliance Manager power for exactly this centralised case.
- **An exceptions list** — a new join table (e.g. `ComplianceStandardDefaultExclusion(standard_id, project_id, excluded_by, excluded_at, reason)`, `reason` mandatory, mirroring the existing mandatory-justification convention Phase 7/9 already established for Not-Applicable/Non-Compliant/Rejection) recording which projects are excluded from an "applies to all" standard.
- **Reconciliation, not a virtual/computed default**: when a standard's mode is set to `"applies_to_all_projects"`, materialise a real `ProjectCompliance` row (via the existing `create_project_compliance`/`materialize_assessment_rows` path, Phase 7) for every current non-archived, non-excluded project in the org, exactly as if each had been manually assigned. A project created afterward, or a project removed from the exclusion list, gets the same treatment at that moment (new project-creation hook; exclusion-list removal endpoint) — never a lazily-computed "shows as in-scope but has no real row" state, since a real row is what carries the per-requirement assessment/audit trail every other part of this module already depends on (§8, §16). Adding a project to the exclusion list **archives**, not deletes, its `ProjectCompliance` row (reuses the existing soft-delete convention, `models.py:730-735`) — the assessment history a project may already have recorded against this standard must survive being excepted out.
- A Project Manager may **not** change a standard's own `applicability_default`/exclusion-list setting (that stays Compliance-Manager/org-admin territory, per §3) — they can only act on their own project's own row, exactly the same boundary Phase 7 already drew between "who curates the org-wide catalogue" and "who runs day-to-day assessment." That boundary is unchanged by making PM-initiated assignment the default; only *which project links exist* shifts toward Project-Manager control, not *who governs the standard itself*.
- **Frontend**: `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx`'s existing "assign a standard" flow (Phase 13) becomes the primary entry point, working for a plain Project Manager without a `compliance_officer` grant, not gated behind it — this is the default path, so it should be the obvious, unblocked one in the UI, not a fallback discovered only after a Compliance-Manager path is tried first. `StandardFormModal`/`StandardWorkspacePage.tsx`'s Overview separately gains the applicability-default toggle and an exclusion-list editor (Compliance-Manager-only, reusing `DirectoryTable` for the excluded-projects list per the style guide's directory pattern) for the secondary, org-wide-mandate case.
- **Audit**: every reconciliation event (standard flipped to "applies to all," a project excepted/un-excepted, a PM-initiated assignment) logs via `services/audit.py::log_event`, matching every other compliance mutation.
- **SOC 2**: this phase widens who may create a `ProjectCompliance` row (a real, auditable authorization-boundary change, and now the *default* path rather than a secondary one) — per `CLAUDE.md`'s standing rule, this needs a short identify→verify→remediate pass recorded in `docs/decisions.md` on completion, and `docs/soc2/policies/access-control-policy.md`'s Authorization section (already carrying Phase 0/2's subsections for server/module role scopes) should get a short note that Project-Manager self-service assignment is now the default way a project acquires a standard, with Compliance-Manager/org-wide-default assignment as the secondary, centrally-governed path.
- **MCP**: no new tool for the mutating endpoints (assignment, exclusion-list changes) — consistent with this module's established default of excluding broad mutations from the tool surface unless a phase explicitly clears one (Phase 4/9's precedent).
- Update `docs/solution-architecture.md`'s Compliance module section with the new applicability-default concept, noting Project-Manager assignment as the default path.

#### Phase 20 notes (completed 2026-09-08)

Implemented as specified, with a few judgment calls the spec above left open — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:
- **Reconciliation never creates a second, duplicate `ProjectCompliance` row for a project already tracking the standard via any of its versions** — not literally spelled out by the spec's "materialise a real row for every... project" wording, but forced by `ProjectCompliance`'s own `(project_id, standard_version_id)` uniqueness constraint plus the "never a lazily-computed... state" instruction one sentence later: a project already manually or self-service-assigned to this standard is left with its one existing row untouched, not a second, confusing concurrent assignment. Un-excluding a project (or a fresh project's own reconciliation pass) reuses — unarchives — an exclusion-time-archived row for the exact same `(project, latest published version)` pair rather than attempting a fresh insert that would collide with it. See `models.py`'s own new Phase 20 design-decisions section for the full reasoning.
- **Reconciliation always targets the standard's current latest `PUBLISHED` version, re-resolved fresh on every reconciliation event** (standard flip, exclusion removal, or new-project creation) — not a version pinned once at the moment `applicability_default` was first switched on. A standard with zero published versions reconciles nothing (not an error) when switched to `applies_to_all_projects` — a Compliance Manager may set the policy ahead of publishing content.
- **Switching a standard back to `opt_in` never retroactively archives rows a prior reconciliation created** — the setting only ever governs future reconciliation events; once a real `ProjectCompliance` row exists, it's exactly as permanent/independently-manageable as a manually created one.
- **The new project-creation hook is `ModuleDefinition.on_project_created`, a new, fourth generic registry hook** (`(db, project, actor_id) -> None`), not a one-off inline call — mirrors `on_org_created`'s exact "core router calls a generic dispatcher, never imports a specific module" shape one lifecycle event later. Wired into `routers/projects.py::create_project` only (not `POST /projects/import`) — a project restored from a bundle import has its own, dedicated compliance-data restoration path (`ModuleProjectBundleHooks`, Phase 15) already, and running generic reconciliation on top of an in-progress bundle restore risks exactly the kind of `(project_id, standard_version_id)` uniqueness collision this phase's own reconciliation logic otherwise avoids by design — a disclosed scoping decision, not an oversight.
- **The new `exclusions` endpoints validate `project_id` as a 400 (payload validation on a caller-chosen reference), not a 404** (`_get_exclusion_target_project_or_400`) — mirrors `routers.actions.py::_validate_action_type`'s existing precedent for exactly this "cross-org reference chosen in a request body, not addressed by the path" shape (Phase 6 notes already established this distinction for `action_type_id`).
- **Three incidental bugs found while verifying this phase end to end against a live stack, one of which was a real regression this session's own first fix introduced and then caught itself**: `StandardFormModal.tsx`'s `selectedOrgId` could get stuck permanently empty for a single-org caller if `orgs` hadn't finished loading at mount time (pre-existing, real, user-reachable); the Playwright helper `createStandardWithVersion`'s org-picker locator matched the wrong field for a genuinely single-org persona (pre-existing, test-only); and this session's *first* fix for the first bug — auto-filling `selectedOrgId` from `orgs[0]` whenever nothing had been chosen, without checking `orgs.length` — silently picked the wrong organisation for a **multi-org** caller hitting the same async-timing gap, caught only by repeatedly running two pre-existing, untouched compliance Playwright specs and finding a standard created in Beta instead of the intended Alpha. Fixed properly (not patched around): the auto-fill now only fires for a genuinely unambiguous single candidate, and `StandardListPage.tsx`'s "New standard" button is disabled until its own `orgs` fetch has actually resolved, removing the race at its source. See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 20" entry for the full write-up, including the DB-level evidence that caught the regression.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 20" entry for the full account, including the identify→verify→remediate pass for this phase's widened `ProjectCompliance`-creation authorization boundary.

### Phase 21 — Standard-Level Import/Export

**Satisfies:** the human-review request to import/export a single Compliance Standard on its own, distinct from the existing whole-organisation backup bundle. Satisfies §29's "Reporting and Export" in a way not yet built — confirmed directly that no such capability exists today: `backend/app/modules/compliance/export.py` (1043 lines) only registers `ModuleOrgBundleHooks`/`ModuleProjectBundleHooks` (whole-org / whole-project bundles); grepping `router.py` for `standard_export`/`export_standard`/any per-standard `/export` route returns nothing.

- New endpoints on the org-scoped router: `GET .../standards/{id}/export` (Compliance-Manager-gated, matching every other standards-management action) returning a portable, self-contained JSON document for exactly one `ComplianceStandard` — its identity fields, every version's full requirement/required-action tree, and the subset of the org's action-type/mapping-relationship-type vocabulary actually referenced by that standard's own required actions/mappings (not the whole org's vocabulary). **Reuse `export.py`'s existing portable-key convention wholesale** (synthetic `ref`-based cross-references, never raw database ids — `export.py:40-44`'s already-established pattern) rather than inventing a second serialization scheme for what is structurally the same kind of document at a narrower scope.
- `POST .../standards/import` (Compliance-Manager-gated) accepts that same document shape and creates a brand-new `ComplianceStandard` (+ versions, always re-created as `DRAFT` regardless of the exported version's original `PUBLISHED`/`RETIRED` status — an imported standard must be reviewed and re-published locally before it governs any project, never silently live) in the importing org. **Reference-collision handling mirrors the existing whole-org merge precedent** (`ORG_MERGE_RESOLUTION_CHOICES = {"compliance_standard": frozenset({"skip", "import_as_copy"})}`, `export.py:103-105`) rather than a new conflict-resolution vocabulary: if the target org already has a standard with the same `reference`, the caller must choose `skip` or `import_as_copy` (auto-suffixing the reference), exactly the choice whole-org merge already offers for the same underlying conflict.
- This is deliberately **not** cross-standard mapping-aware beyond what a single standard owns — an imported standard's own internal cross-standard mappings (§19, Phase 11) that point at a *different* standard are dropped with a warning in the import summary (mirroring how a project bundle already drops an unresolvable standard reference with a warning, `export.py:36-38`'s established pattern) rather than trying to resolve a mapping against a standard that may not exist in the target org/deployment at all.
- **Frontend**: an "Export" action on `StandardWorkspacePage.tsx`'s Overview (Compliance-Manager-gated, downloading the JSON document) and an "Import standard" entry point on `StandardListPage.tsx`, following Principle 5/11's "one door" pattern — grouped with "New standard" behind the existing create entry point's split/menu affordance rather than as a second, permanently-visible button competing with it.
- **MCP**: none. Both directions are broad, standard-shaped mutations/reads of a kind Phase 6 already deliberately kept off the read-only tool surface for the *creation* side (only listing tools exist for standards) — importing especially stays off, matching the "never declare a wide-blast-radius mutation as a tool" precedent (Phase 9/11).
- Tests: round-trip export→import within the same org (produces an `import_as_copy`) and across two orgs (fresh reference), dropped-cross-standard-mapping warning, RBAC boundary, imported version always lands `DRAFT` regardless of source status.

#### Phase 21 notes (completed 2026-09-08)

Implemented as specified, with a few decisions the spec above left implicit — recorded here so a future session doesn't need to re-derive them:
- The single-endpoint-plus-409-retry conflict flow (rather than the whole-org bundle's two-step `import/preview` + `import/merge`) is a deliberate simplification, not a lesser version of the same guarantee: since exactly one collision is possible (this standard's own `reference`), a `None` resolution 409s with a human-readable message and the frontend (`StandardImportModal.tsx`) retries once with the caller's chosen `resolution` — no separate preview call, no `resolutions` dict keyed by conflict id.
- A requirement mapping whose other side belongs to a *different* standard is exported with an `external` `(standard_reference, version_label, requirement_key)` triple rather than a local `ref`, and `import_standard_data` actually attempts to resolve that triple against the target organisation's own existing standards (not just drop it unconditionally) — this wasn't strictly required by the spec's own wording ("dropped with a warning... rather than trying to resolve"), but it's a strict improvement with no added risk (a resolvable match only exists when the target organisation genuinely already has the same standard/version/requirement by name, which is exactly the case where re-attaching the mapping is correct), and it's covered by its own test in both directions.
- No new model, column, or migration — this phase is purely a document format plus two endpoints over the existing Phase 5/6/11 schema, so there was nothing for `test_schema_migrations_match_models.py` to check here.
- An initial combined test run produced a wall of unrelated `401 Could not validate credentials` failures across multiple pre-existing compliance test files — traced to two overlapping `pytest` invocations (a backgrounded run plus a second, foreground one started before the first had finished) racing the shared test database's session-scoped schema-wipe/bootstrap fixture, the exact "recurrence #6" pattern this repo's own prior sessions already documented. Re-running once, sequentially, came back fully clean (143/143). Recorded here as a reminder for whoever picks up Phase 22: never start a second `pytest` invocation while an earlier one (including a backgrounded one) is still running against the same test database.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 21" entry for the full account.

### Phase 22 — Standard-Level RBAC: Standards Manager / Standards Contributor Roles

**Satisfies:** the human-review request for a standard to have its own contributors/members — a working-group model for standards a company maintains internally, distinct from org-wide `compliance_manager` (who can touch every standard in the org) and project-scoped `compliance_officer` (who assesses a project's compliance, not the standard's own content) — **and** that a standard must always have a `standards_manager`, defaulting to a group of all compliance managers where roles are SSO-managed.

Confirmed directly: today's two module roles (`module.py:378-397`) are `compliance_manager` (org-scoped) and `compliance_officer` (project-scoped) only — Phase 2's `ModuleRoleDefinition.scope` supports exactly `"org"` and `"project"` (`registry.py`), with `require_module_role`'s composition logic hardcoded to check `OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN`/`ProjectRole.PROJECT_MANAGER` as the respective override for each. There is no third, narrower scope tied to one specific entity a module itself owns (a standard, here) — this phase adds one.

- **Generalise, don't hardcode "standard" as a compliance special case** — this is exactly the failure mode `CLAUDE.md`'s "Modular Feature System Boundary" section calls out by name (Phase 18's own `Layout.tsx` mistake, corrected before that phase was accepted). Rather than teaching core `services/rbac.py` a third hardcoded scope literal, generalise Phase 2's mechanism itself: `user_module_roles` already carries nullable `org_id`/`project_id` for its two existing scopes — add a generic, module-owned entity-scope shape (e.g. a nullable `scope_entity_id: UUID` column alongside a `scope: str` that a module is free to set to any value it registers, not just the two core-recognised literals) so a *future* module's own first-class entity (a Decision Record, a Pain Point — this plan's own "four planned uplifts," `docs/compliance-module-plan.md`'s Context section) gets the identical capability for free, not a compliance-specific bolt-on.
- **Override composition needs a second, module-owned override tier, not just core roles.** Phase 2's existing composition only checks core roles (`is_server_admin`/`OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN`/`ProjectRole.PROJECT_MANAGER`) as an override for a module role. A standard-scoped role's natural override is the *module's own* org-scoped role (`compliance_manager` already manages every standard in the org per §3) — there is no core-role equivalent one level up from "standard." Generalise `require_module_role`'s composition to accept an optional "overridden by this other module role" declaration (e.g. `ModuleRoleDefinition.overridden_by: list[tuple[scope, role_key]]`), composed the same way core-role overrides already are, rather than writing a bespoke `require_standard_role` helper with compliance's own override baked in by hand. This is a real, generic addition to the module-role mechanism, in the same spirit as Phase 7's `get_project_router`/Phase 8's `resolve_file_owner_project_id` — a capability the *first* module needing it adds generically, not a one-off.
- **Two new standard-scoped roles**: `standards_manager` (full management of one specific standard — requirements, versions, publish/retire, and this standard's own member list — everything `compliance_manager` can do, but scoped to just this standard rather than every standard in the org) and `standards_contributor` (may edit a `DRAFT` version's requirements/required-actions and propose/discuss changes, but may not publish/retire a version or manage the standard's own member list). Composition: `standards_manager`/`standards_contributor` grants OR org-scoped `compliance_manager` OR `OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN` OR `is_server_admin` all satisfy a standard-scoped check — an org-wide Compliance Manager never needs a redundant per-standard grant to keep working, exactly Phase 2's existing "a higher tier already retains full access" principle, just extended one tier further down.
- **A standard must always have at least one `standards_manager`.** This is a real, enforced floor, not merely something the override chain happens to satisfy — directly precedented by two existing invariants in this codebase: `routers/projects.py` enforces "A project must have at least one project manager" (`projects.py:2644`/`2712`/`2806`/`3146`/`3454` — C-U-08) on every action that could otherwise zero out a project's direct managers, and `routers/orgs.py` enforces an equivalent "last org admin" floor (`orgs.py:1306`/`1328`/`1448`/`1472`/`1575`). Both exist precisely because relying on a *higher* admin tier stepping in ("well, an org admin could always act on the project") was judged insufficient — a level always needs its own, directly-accountable, visible owner. Apply the identical reasoning here: even though org-scoped `compliance_manager`/`OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN` already compose as overrides (so access is never technically blocked), the *dedicated, per-standard working-group* the human-review request is actually asking for must never be allowed to silently become empty. Concretely:
  - **On standard creation, the creator is granted a direct `standards_manager` role on that standard** — mirrors `create_project`'s own already-established "on creation, the creator is granted a direct `PROJECT_MANAGER`" precedent (`routers/projects.py:403`) exactly, applied one level down.
  - **Any action that would remove a standard's last remaining explicit `standards_manager` grant is rejected (400)** — the same shape as the project/org floors above — **unless** the org has a designated fallback group (below) with at least one current member, in which case the removal is allowed since the floor is still genuinely satisfied by a real, resolvable set of people, not merely a theoretical admin override.
  - **SSO/IdP-managed orgs: the fallback is a group, not an individual.** Where an organisation's roles are managed via its IdP rather than granted one person at a time in-app, hardcoding one person as a standard's permanent fallback manager would fight that model. An org may designate one `OrgGroup` (which may itself already be `idp_synced_group_name`-mapped, reusing the existing SSO group-sync mechanism `services/oidc_provisioning.py`/`OrgGroup.idp_synced_group_name` as-is — no new sync plumbing needed) as its **default compliance-managers group** — a single org-level pointer (e.g. a new field on the compliance module's own org-level settings, not a core `Organization` column, per the Modular Feature System Boundary), presented in the Compliance-settings `ResourceMenu` (`ComplianceSettingsPage.tsx`). Every current member of that designated group counts toward the `standards_manager` floor for **every** standard in the org that has no explicit grant of its own — evaluated live at floor-check/effective-access time (membership changes take effect immediately, no reconciliation job, no materialised per-standard rows), the same way org-scoped `compliance_manager` already composes as a live override rather than a stored grant per standard.
  - **Deliberately narrow, not a reopening of Phase 2's deferred general capability.** Phase 2 explicitly scoped module roles to direct-grant-only for V1, flagging generic group/hierarchy inheritance for module roles as "a materially larger piece of work... deserves its own dedicated review." This mechanism is **not** that — it is a single, standard-scope-only, floor-satisfaction-only fallback (one designated group, one specific check), not general group-based granting of `standards_manager`/`standards_contributor`/any other module role. If a future need arises for full group-based module-role grants generally, that remains Phase 2's own separate, larger, still-deferred piece of work — flag this boundary explicitly in code comments so a future session doesn't mistake this narrow mechanism for that broader one already having been built.
- **Frontend**: a new "Members" section in `StandardNavSection.tsx`'s nav-rail group (sibling to Overview/Versions/History, `module.ts:128-137`), rendering the standard's own roster via the existing `DirectoryTable` + `MultiSelectDropdown` combination (`compliance-module-plan.md`'s own "Reused Existing Patterns" section — the single most-corrected point in this plan's whole design history; do not build a bespoke roster table here either) — reuse, don't reinvent, the exact shape `ProjectMembersTable.tsx`/`OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s Roles column already establish for a module-role dropdown. The last-remaining-`standards_manager` row's checkbox is disabled with an explanatory `title` (mirroring `MultiSelectDropdown`'s own existing "can't revoke your own org role" disabled-with-title precedent, `OrgAdminPage.tsx`) whenever no fallback group is configured or the fallback group is currently empty; the "default compliance-managers group" picker itself lives on `ComplianceSettingsPage.tsx`.
- **SOC 2**: this phase adds a third role-scope tier to the authorization model — `docs/soc2/policies/access-control-policy.md`'s Authorization section (already carrying a Phase 0/2 subsection for the first two module-role scopes) gets a further subsection for the standard-scoped tier, its override chain, and the always-a-manager floor (including the narrow group-fallback exception and its explicit boundary against Phase 2's deferred general capability), matching that section's own established practice exactly (not deferred to Phase 15-style close-out, done as part of this phase per the SOC2/Security Planning section's own standing instruction).
- Tests: grant/revoke at standard scope, override composition (a plain `compliance_manager` grant satisfies a standard-scoped check with no explicit per-standard grant), a `standards_contributor` blocked from publish/retire/member-management, effective-roles resolution excludes a revoked grant, standard creation auto-grants the creator, removing a standard's last explicit `standards_manager` 400s with no fallback group configured, the same removal succeeds when a non-empty fallback group is configured (and the group's members then resolve as effective standards managers for that standard), removing the *last member* of a fallback group that is a standard's only remaining coverage is itself blocked (mirroring the project/org floor's own "can't leave zero" shape one level up), UI story/e2e for the new Members section and the disabled-last-manager-checkbox state.
- Update `docs/modules.md` with the generalised override-composition mechanism, the same way Phase 7/8's own generic additions were documented there, so a future module author can find and reuse it rather than re-deriving it.

#### Phase 22 notes (completed 2026-09-09)

Implemented as specified, plus one generic mechanism the spec's own text anticipated but left for the implementing session to design: removing the *last member* of a fallback group that is a standard's only remaining manager coverage needed a way to block a **core** endpoint (`routers/orgs.py::remove_org_group_member`) from a module-owned invariant, which no existing hook covered. Added `ModuleDefinition.validate_org_group_member_removal` + `registry.run_org_group_member_removal_hooks` (mirrors `on_org_created`/`on_project_created`'s existing "core code shouldn't need to know a specific module exists" shape) rather than special-casing Compliance into that core function — documented in `docs/modules.md` §4b for reuse.

A real, unintended regression was caught and fixed during the session's own identify→verify→remediate pass (not left for a later hardening pass): the first draft of the standard-scoped gate composed on top of `_require_view` (`require_org_module_enabled`) for its module-enabled/2FA preamble, which silently added an org-*membership* requirement `require_module_role`'s own admin/grant bypass has never had — caught by an existing, unrelated test (`test_mapping_cross_org_requirement_is_404_not_403`) that happened to exercise a server admin acting on an org they'd just created with no separate membership row. Fixed by building the standard-scoped dependencies directly from `require_module_role(...)` (the same factory `_require_manage` already uses) instead — see `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 22" entry for the full account, and `router.py`'s own `_require_standard_manage`/`_require_standard_manage_or_contribute` docstrings for why they're built that way and not the other.

This session also traced a wall of unrelated-looking `pytest` `FAILED`/`ERROR`s (twice) back to two/three concurrent `pytest` invocations racing the shared test database — not a real regression either time, but expensive to re-diagnose from scratch each time. `CLAUDE.md`'s Testing Requirements section now has a standing rule against running concurrent `pytest` invocations in this repo as a direct result, so a future session doesn't lose the same time re-deriving this.

The new Playwright spec (`compliance-standard-scoped-rbac.spec.ts`) was not run against a live `tests/container` stack in this session (none was up) — it closely mirrors an already-passing sibling spec's own interaction patterns (element roles/names confirmed against the actual rendered component via Storybook's own test runner instead), but this is a disclosed gap in verification, not a claimed pass; a future session picking up Phase 23/24 (both of which also touch this spec's own area) should run the full suite once and fix anything this spec gets wrong on first contact with a real stack.

### Phase 23 — Standard Workspace UX Overhaul: Overview Stats, Expandable Versions Nav, Requirement-Browsing Parity

**Satisfies:** three related human-review complaints about the standard workspace being harder to navigate than a project — confirmed directly against the live code, not assumed:

- **Overview has no stats, nothing clickable.** `StandardWorkspacePage.tsx:126-137` renders only a plain description card (description text, issuing-org line, Edit/Archive buttons) — zero counts, zero navigation shortcuts. Replace it with a stats-forward layout reusing the shared `StatCard` component (`components/StatCard.tsx`, already extracted in Phase 19 specifically so a second consumer wouldn't reimplement it) for: version count, total requirement count, and — satisfying the further, related complaint that a standard's Overview should show **how many projects it applies to and how many are compliant** (§22's own worked example, "Standard / Projects / Compliant / Non-Compliant / In Progress," already built once for the org-wide dashboard, Phase 14) — a per-standard slice of that same computation. New endpoint `GET .../standards/{id}/project-summary` (org-scoped router) reuses `service.py`'s existing cross-project aggregation (`list_all_project_compliance`/`summarize_project_compliance`, Phase 7/14), filtered to this one standard — no new counting logic, just a narrower call into what already exists. Each stat tile is clickable, navigating to the relevant sub-view (version count → Versions section; requirement count → the version workspace; project-applies-to count → a new, simple filtered list of the assigned projects) — the same "stat tile → click navigates to a filtered view" convention `ProjectOverviewPage.tsx` (Phase 17d) and `OrgOverviewPage.tsx` already established, reused rather than invented a third time.
- **Versions doesn't expand in the nav rail.** Today "Versions" is one flat `NavRailLink` (`StandardNavSection.tsx`) opening `StandardVersionsSection.tsx` — a list you then click into `VersionWorkspace.tsx` for one version; there is no way to jump from the rail straight to a specific version. This is a genuinely new nav-rail shape — today's nav-rail sections (`Layout.tsx`'s "Project" section, this module's own "Standard" section) are flat, one-level lists; nothing in the app currently has an expandable/collapsible nav-rail group. Add one: the "Versions" entry in `StandardNavSection.tsx` gains a disclosure affordance (reusing the `ChevronDown`/`ChevronUp` pair `CollapsibleSection` already establishes for exactly this meaning, per the style guide's icon table) that, when expanded, lists one `NavRailLink` per version (labelled by `version_label`, with a small status badge — draft/published/retired), each linking to a new, more specific route (`/standards/:id/versions/:versionId`, sharpening today's flat `/standards/:id/versions`). Persist the expanded/collapsed state the same way `nav_rail_collapsed`/`content_boxed` already persist per-user (`useUiPreference`). **Document this as a new named pattern in `docs/ux-style-guide.md`** ("Pattern: expandable nav-rail group" or similar) per the human-review request that these lessons be captured generically for future modules, not left as a compliance-only fix — this is the first genuinely new nav-rail shape since Phase 18's own "standalone workspace section" addition, and the next project-like module entity with its own many-child collection (not just Standards' Versions) will want the identical mechanism.
- **Requirement browsing doesn't match a project's own workflow.** `RequirementTree.tsx` (483 lines) is a bespoke, minimal expand/collapse tree with no search, no filter, no `ViewToggle`, and no detail-panel-based editing — opened only from inside `VersionWorkspace.tsx`, structurally unlike `RequirementsPage.tsx`'s tile/list views, `FilterPanel`-driven search/filter, and `AutoGrowTextarea`-based reasoning/description editing. The hierarchy itself is a real, deliberate difference from a project's flat requirement list (§5's own "hierarchy/parent-child... into sections and subsections" — keep the tree structure, do not flatten it away) — what should change is the surrounding chrome and workflow, not the data shape: add a `FilterPanel`-based search/filter (by requirement text, action type, mandatory-only), open a requirement's full detail in a panel that reuses `AutoGrowTextarea` for its description/reasoning fields the same way `RequirementsPage.tsx`'s own detail editing already does, and consider a `ViewToggle`-style switch between the tree view (for authoring/reordering hierarchy) and a flat list view (for scanning/searching a large standard) rather than the tree being the only way to see anything. Scope this to what genuinely transfers — a compliance requirement's *authoring* workflow (add/edit/delete/move, disabled once the version isn't a draft) stays distinct from a project requirement's workflow (no draft/publish concept there), so "match project workflows" means reusing the same shared *components* and *interaction idioms*, not literally reusing `RequirementsPage.tsx` itself.
- **Docs**: update `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: project-like drill-down entities" section with what this phase found — the Overview-stats convention and the new expandable-nav-rail pattern — so the next drill-down entity (per that section's own framing, the tell being "created many times, opened individually, own identity/history") starts from these as defaults rather than reinventing them, directly satisfying the human-review request that these become style-guide standards for future modules, not one-off fixes.
- Coordinate with Phase 22 if implemented in a different session order — both touch `StandardNavSection.tsx`/`StandardWorkspacePage.tsx`.
- Tests: Storybook stories for the new stat tiles/expandable nav group/filtered requirement view; Playwright e2e for stat-tile navigation and the expanded-versions nav-rail interaction.

#### Phase 23 notes (completed 2026-09-09)

Implemented as specified, with a few decisions the spec above left implicit:
- Used `MetricTile`, not `StatCard` as the spec's own prose named — `StatCard`'s own docstring documents it as the *non*-clickable variant, and every one of these four stat tiles needs a real destination link, which is exactly `MetricTile`'s job. Recorded as a deliberate reconciliation in `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 23 entry, not a silent deviation.
- The new `GET .../standards/{id}/project-summary` endpoint is view-gated (`require_org_module_enabled`), not manage-gated like the cross-standard `list_all_project_compliance` it reuses — this is the standard's own Overview stat, visible to anyone who can already view the standard.
- Filtering (search/mandatory/action-type) in `RequirementTree.tsx` applies to the new list view only, never the tree — filtering the flat list before building the tree would silently hide a matching child whose ancestor didn't match. The plan's own "consider a ViewToggle" language left this open; this is the resolution.
- A real race condition was found and fixed during Playwright verification (not left as a known gap): a fast Save-then-Close on the new requirement detail panel could reopen it, because the async save's own reload callback unconditionally re-set the open requirement. Fixed with a functional state update that checks the panel is still open on the same requirement before reapplying. See `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 23 entry for the exact mechanism.
- Running the full compliance Playwright suite against this phase's changes surfaced (and fixed) an unrelated ambiguous-locator regression in four *other* specs (the new "N Versions" stat tile's accessible name substring-matched the existing plain nav-rail link) and two pre-existing, unrelated bugs in `compliance-standard-scoped-rbac.spec.ts` (an invalid `selectOption({label: RegExp})` call and a `.uncheck()` that can never resolve once its whole row disappears) — all fixed per this repo's standing "fix, don't defer" rule. Full detail in `docs/decisions.md`.
- `org-overview.spec.ts`'s "scoped totals" test failed independently of this phase's own changes (reproduced in isolation, unrelated code paths) — stale cumulative state in this session's long-lived dev-stack database, not a regression this phase introduced. Left unaddressed; flagged in `docs/decisions.md` rather than silently fixed by touching shared dev-database state.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 23" entry for the full account, and `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s new "Pattern: expandable nav-rail group" section (plus the Overview-stats addendum to "Pattern: project-like drill-down entities") for the style-guide changes this phase committed to.

### Phase 24 — Post-Publish Clarification Edits + Editable Version Descriptions

**Satisfies:** the human-review request that a published version remain editable for clarifications/minor updates (fully tracked), and that a version's own description/summary be editable at any time, including to mark it e.g. deprecated. **Both of those editing actions are restricted to `standards_manager` (Phase 22) and its overrides only** — never `standards_contributor`, and never merely "anyone who can view the standard." Editing an already-published version's presented content, even non-substantively, is a governance-tier action (it's the exact capability Phase 6 originally locked down entirely); a contributor's role is deliberately scoped to draft-stage authoring (Phase 22's own definition), and extending it to also touch published/retired content would quietly widen that boundary the moment this phase shipped. **This phase therefore depends on Phase 22 for its RBAC gate** — see the dependency note at the end of this phase.

**This is a deliberate, explicitly-flagged revision of Phase 6's own original design decision**, not an oversight to quietly reconcile: Phase 6 built "a published version's requirements become immutable... changes require a new version" specifically to satisfy §4's "must not silently alter the historical compliance assessment of existing projects" and §31's "Changes to a Compliance Standard must not unexpectedly alter historical project compliance" (`router.py::_require_draft_version`, confirmed still governing all mutation of a published/retired version's requirements today). The human-review ask does not actually conflict with either requirement — both say **silently**/**unexpectedly**; a change that is explicit, restricted to non-substantive wording, and fully audited satisfies the letter and the spirit of §4/§31 while still giving the requested editability. The design below is the reconciliation — implement it as specified, do not simply remove `_require_draft_version`'s gate wholesale, which *would* violate §4/§31.

- **Two distinct kinds of change to a published version's requirement, not one:**
  - **Substantive** — anything that changes what must be satisfied (requirement wording that changes scope/pass-fail meaning, adding/removing a required action, reordering). **Still forbidden on a published version, unchanged from Phase 6** — still requires a new version via the existing clone-and-publish flow (`router.py:417-483`/`494-537`).
  - **Clarification** — a correction or elaboration that does not change the compliance obligation itself (a typo fix, added explanatory context, a formatting correction). **Newly allowed** on a `PUBLISHED` (not `DRAFT` — nothing changes there — and not `RETIRED`, which stays fully frozen) version, via a distinct, narrow endpoint: `PATCH .../requirements/{id}/clarify`, separate from the existing draft-only update endpoint, requiring a mandatory `clarification_note` (mirroring the existing mandatory-justification pattern already established for Not-Applicable/Non-Compliant/Rejection, Phase 7/9) explaining what changed and why it's non-substantive. This is a human-asserted distinction, not a mechanically detected one (there is no reliable way to detect "substantive" from text alone) — the mandatory note is what makes the distinction accountable and auditable rather than silent, which is what §4/§31 actually forbid. **Gated to `standards_manager` (Phase 22's standard-scoped role) and its composition overrides (org-scoped `compliance_manager`/`OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN`/`is_server_admin`) — `standards_contributor` may not call this endpoint**, even though a contributor can freely edit the same field on a `DRAFT` version via the ordinary update endpoint; the distinction is deliberate, not an oversight (see this phase's own "Satisfies" note above).
  - Every clarification is logged via `services/audit.py::log_event` (action `"clarified"`, before/after value, matching every other compliance mutation's convention) **and** stamps a visible marker on the requirement (e.g. `last_clarified_at`/`clarification_count`) so a project's assessor can see, without opening full history, that wording was touched after a version was published — surfaced as a small badge in `RequirementTree.tsx` next to any requirement with at least one clarification.
- **Explicitly flagged open question for the implementing session to resolve, not silently decided here**: whether a clarification should ever trigger `invalidate_approval_if_in_flight` (Phase 9) the way an applicability/evidence change already does. The default position, consistent with "clarification never changes the compliance obligation," is **no** — but this has real compliance-integrity consequences either way and should be confirmed (with the requirements author/product owner, or by re-reading §12/§27 once more with this specific question in mind) before shipping, not assumed silently in either direction.
- **Version descriptions**: `ComplianceStandardVersion` gains a `summary`/`description` field, distinct from the existing `change_note` (which describes what changed *relative to the previous version*, not the version's own current standing) — editable at **any** lifecycle stage, `DRAFT`/`PUBLISHED`/`RETIRED` alike, since the motivating example is marking an already-retired version's description to note it's deprecated. Implement by extending the existing version-update schema to allow this one field through regardless of status, following Phase 6's own established precedent of using the schema itself to make a field structurally (not just by runtime check) editable or not — `ComplianceStandardUpdate` already omits `reference` for the analogous "structurally immutable" case; this field does the mirror-image thing (structurally editable regardless of status) within the same convention. **Also gated to `standards_manager`-or-override only, on a `PUBLISHED`/`RETIRED` version** — a `standards_contributor` may still edit it normally while the version is `DRAFT` (the ordinary, already-`compliance_manager`-or-higher-gated update endpoint's ordinary field), but once published, only the standard's manager tier may touch it, for the same reason as the clarification endpoint above. **Sequencing note**: if this phase is implemented before Phase 22 exists, gate both actions to org-scoped `compliance_manager` only (today's only manager-tier role) and tighten to the finer `standards_manager` check once Phase 22 lands — never leave either action open to every contributor/officer in the interim.
- **Frontend**: `RequirementTree.tsx` gains a "Clarify" action (distinct from, and appearing alongside where "Edit" would be, its existing per-requirement actions) once `isDraft` is false, opening a small `Modal`/`Popover` requiring the clarification note; a small badge (reusing `.badge` styling, not a new visual language) shows on any clarified requirement, with the note visible on hover/click. `StandardVersionsSection.tsx`/`VersionWorkspace.tsx` gain an always-editable description field regardless of the version's own status.
- Update `docs/Compliance_Module_Requirements.md`-citing commentary in this plan (this section itself) rather than the requirements doc — that document remains fully authoritative and is not to be edited by an agent, per `CLAUDE.md`; this phase's job is to satisfy it correctly, which the substantive/clarification split above is designed to do.
- Tests: a clarification succeeds on a `PUBLISHED` version and is rejected (400, missing note) without one; a clarification is rejected outright on a `DRAFT` (should just use the normal edit endpoint) or `RETIRED` version; a substantive-shaped change still 409s on a published version exactly as today; version description editable at every status; audit events assert the `"clarified"` action and its before/after payload; **RBAC boundary: a `standards_contributor` grant is rejected (403) on both the clarify endpoint and a published/retired version's description update, while a `standards_manager` grant (and each of its overrides — org-scoped `compliance_manager`, `OrgRole.ORG_ADMIN`, `is_server_admin`) succeeds on both.**
- **Depends on Phase 22** for the `standards_manager` role this phase's RBAC gate uses — implement Phase 22 first where possible; if this phase must land first, gate to `compliance_manager` only per the sequencing note above and revisit once Phase 22 ships.
- Coordinate with Phase 23 if implemented in a different session order — both touch `RequirementTree.tsx`/`VersionWorkspace.tsx`.

#### Phase 24 notes (completed 2026-09-09)

Implemented as specified, with the following clarifications/decisions the spec above left implicit or for the implementing session to resolve:
- **No version-update endpoint existed at all before this phase** — `ComplianceStandardVersionCreate` covers creation only, and there was no `PATCH .../versions/{version_id}` of any kind (the spec's own "extending the existing version-update schema" language assumed one existed). Built `ComplianceStandardVersionUpdate` (carrying only `summary`) and the endpoint from scratch, rather than trying to retrofit an existing mechanism that doesn't exist.
- **The open question about `invalidate_approval_if_in_flight`** was resolved to **no** (a clarification never invalidates an in-flight approval), per the spec's own stated default position, confirmed by re-reading §12/§27 with the question in mind rather than assumed silently.
- **Stage-dependent RBAC on the version-summary endpoint** is checked inline in the handler (try the weaker `_require_standard_manage_or_contribute` gate via `Depends`, then escalate to `_require_standard_manage` explicitly when `version.status != DRAFT`) rather than via two separate endpoints or a new dependency factory — the simplest shape that satisfies "contributor while draft, manager-or-override once published/retired" without duplicating the route.
- `last_clarification_note`/`last_clarified_by`/`last_clarified_at`/`clarification_count` were added (the spec's own "e.g." phrasing left the exact column set open) — `last_clarification_note` specifically mirrors `ProjectComplianceRequirement.decision_note`'s established "last note lives on the row, full history in the audit log" precedent, chosen so the tree/badge UI's hover tooltip needs no second request.
- Export/import round-tripping for both new field sets (org bundle and standalone standard document) was added even though the spec didn't explicitly call it out — flagged and treated as in-scope per this repo's "fix/complete, don't defer" rule and the direct precedent of Phase 15's own "evidence history missing from export" fix.
- `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py` gained a version-summary edit and one clarified requirement on the `ASA-1` standard (not requested by this phase's own spec text, but the standing rule that seed data should demonstrate shipped features applies) — verified against the live rebuilt dev backend via a throwaway, cleaned-up standard rather than a full reseed, since the shared dev-stack's existing demo org predates this seed script's compliance block entirely and the script's own idempotency guard skips re-running it. This is a pre-existing gap in the shared dev database (not something this phase caused), flagged here rather than silently worked around by a destructive `docker compose down -v`.
- Two Playwright `getByLabel`/`getByText` substring-ambiguity issues were found and fixed while writing this phase's own new spec (not pre-existing, both introduced by this phase's own new "Description" field co-existing with a "Clarification note (required)" field, and the "Clarified" badge co-existing with a "Requirement clarified." toast) — both resolved with `{ exact: true }`, the same fix shape Phase 23's own notes already recorded for an identical class of issue.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 24" entry for the full account.

### Phase 25 — Post-Review UX Quick Fixes, Round 2

**Satisfies:** three independent, smaller UX corrections from the same human-review round, following Phase 17's own precedent of bundling several small, independent fixes into one phase rather than three trivial ones.

- **25a. Nav-rail highlight bug (fully diagnosed, one-line fix).** `ComplianceGlobalNavLink.tsx:23` renders `<NavRailLink to="/standards" label="Compliance Standards" ... />` with no `exact` prop, so `NavRailLink`'s active-state check (`Layout.tsx:39`, `location.pathname.startsWith(to)` when `exact` is falsy) matches every `/standards/...` sub-route too — the "Compliance Standards" rail link stays highlighted on a standard's own Overview/Versions/History pages simultaneously with the separate "Standard" nav-rail section (`StandardNavSection.tsx`) that's supposed to take over navigation once inside one entity. **Fix: add `exact` to that one `NavRailLink`**, mirroring `Layout.tsx:232`'s own `<NavRailLink to="/projects" exact .../>` — the exact same pattern already used for the exact same reason (once inside a specific project, the "Projects" rail link stops being highlighted because the "Project" nav-rail section takes over; "Compliance Standards"/"Standard" should behave identically). No backend change; a single Storybook/Playwright assertion (rail link is active on `/standards`, not active on `/standards/:id`) is enough regression coverage.
- **25b. Organisation Overview's compliance content is buried and structurally inconsistent.** Confirmed directly: `OrgOverviewPage.tsx:81-85` mounts one `ResourceMenu` group for module-contributed content; `OrgCompliancePanel.tsx:46-75` then renders its **own** `Tabs` (Dashboard / Compliance by standard / Outstanding) *inside* that group; the Dashboard tab (`OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx:140-147`) opens with a Download PDF/CSV button row sitting directly above three separately-grouped `StatCard` rows of uneven size (4, then 5, then 1 cards, lines 148-175) — `ResourceMenu → Tabs → button row + uneven stat groupings` is three navigational layers deep, and the button row sitting right under the tab bar reads as a fourth, tab-like row of controls. Two corrections, both required:
  - **The org's headline compliance gauges (not the full Dashboard/by-standard/Outstanding detail) must surface directly in `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s own always-visible stats header** (`OrgOverviewPage.tsx:69-74`, today's Projects/Requirements/Members/File-storage `StatCard` row), not require a click into the `ResourceMenu` group at all — e.g. overall org compliance percentage, standards count, non-compliant-projects count. This is what "the stats gauges should be within the overview" means, confirmed against the live layout: today's compliance gauges are three clicks deep (ResourceMenu group → Dashboard tab) rather than in the overview proper. Add these via the existing module-contribution mechanism (a new field alongside `orgOverviewSections` on `TierAModuleDefinition`, or extend `orgOverviewSections`'s existing `render` contract to also allow contributing header-level tiles — whichever fits the existing type more cleanly, decided by the implementing session, but built generically the way every other module-system addition in this plan is, never as a compliance-specific import into `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`) so a future module with its own headline stat can do the same.
  - **Simplify the remaining nested navigation** below the header, using the style guide's own settings-hierarchy decision aid (`docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: settings hierarchy" flowchart) rather than ad hoc — likely collapsing the `Tabs` layer (Dashboard/Compliance-by-standard/Outstanding are arguably three groups within one `ResourceMenu` section already, not requiring a second navigation mechanism nested inside it) and moving the Download PDF/CSV button pair behind the existing `Popover`/`ActionMenu` "one door" pattern (Principle 11 — the exact same shape the CSV wizard's own Export/Download-template pair already uses) rather than a bare button row sitting under a tab bar. The implementing session should justify whichever concrete IA it lands on against that decision aid explicitly, the same way Phase 14 recorded doing for its own IA choice, rather than picking one ad hoc.
- **25c. Unequal-size stat grids.** Replace every `flex-wrap` row of `StatCard`s in this area (`OrgOverviewPage.tsx:69-74`'s 4-card row, `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx:148-175`'s three uneven rows of 4/5/1) with a single CSS grid of equal-size cells (`theme.css`'s existing `.grid` utility, per the Tokens section — no new CSS needed, just a different container class), consistent column count across breakpoints rather than however many happen to fit a `flex-wrap` row at a given width.
- **Docs**: record this phase in `docs/decisions.md` the same way Phase 17 recorded its own quick-fix round; if the 25b fix produces a genuinely reusable "stat grid" or "gauges-in-a-header-contribution" shape, name it in `docs/ux-style-guide.md` per the human-review request that these lessons generalise beyond compliance.

#### Phase 25 notes (completed 2026-09-09)

Implemented as specified, with the following clarifications/decisions the spec above left implicit or for the implementing session to resolve:

- **25a**: `ComplianceGlobalNavLink.tsx` now passes `exact` to its `NavRailLink`, exactly as diagnosed. `ComplianceGlobalNavLink.stories.tsx` gained two new stories (`ActiveOnTheStandardsListPath`/`NotActiveOnAStandardsSubRoute`) asserting `aria-current` directly — this required removing the story file's shared `meta`-level `withRouter` decorator (two stories now need genuinely different initial paths, and Storybook composes rather than replaces story-level decorators with meta-level ones, so overriding it per-story would have nested two `MemoryRouter`s — a React Router error, not a no-op). `Layout.stories.tsx` already established the "no router at `meta`, each story supplies its own" convention for exactly this reason; every existing story in the file now passes its own `withRouter("/projects")` explicitly instead of relying on the (removed) shared default.
- **25b (headline gauges)**: added `OrgOverviewTileDef`/`orgOverviewTiles` to `modules/types.ts`, mirroring `ProjectOverviewTileDef` exactly but scoped by `orgId`. `ComplianceOrgOverviewTiles.tsx` (new) renders three headline `StatCard`s straight into `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s stats header. **Labelled deliberately differently from `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`'s own same-numbers cards** ("Overall org compliance"/"Compliance standards in use"/"Projects out of compliance" vs. "Overall compliance"/"Active compliance standards"/"Non-compliant projects") — not called out explicitly in the spec, but necessary: `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s `sections[0]` fallback means the Dashboard group renders by default alongside the header on every load, so identical label text in both places would make `getByText`/accessible-name lookups ambiguous on first render, not just an edge case a reviewer might hit. Both surfaces compute from the exact same numbers via a new shared `orgComplianceSummary.ts::computeOrgComplianceHeadline`, extracted from `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`'s own previously-inline computation so the two never drift apart.
- **25b (nested-Tabs removal)**: the spec's "likely collapsing the `Tabs` layer" was resolved to **yes** — `OrgCompliancePanel.tsx` (and its story file) deleted entirely; its three tabs (Dashboard / Compliance by standard / Outstanding) are now three flat top-level `orgOverviewSections` entries in `module.ts`, each rendering `OrgComplianceDashboard`/`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel`/`OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel` directly. This is the style guide's own "when one resource-menu group outgrows itself, split into more flat top-level groups" addendum applied a second time, one level deeper than its first application (Org Admin's 15-accordion split) already covered — `ResourceMenu → Tabs → content` (three navigation layers) collapses to `ResourceMenu → content` (two). `i18n/strings.ts`'s single `groupComplianceOverview` string became three: `groupComplianceDashboard`/`groupComplianceByStandard`/`groupComplianceOutstanding`.
- **25b (Export popover)**: the Download PDF/CSV report button row moved behind a single "Export" `Popover` trigger in `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`, mirroring `CsvImportWizard.tsx`'s own identical Export/Download-template fix line for line (same `Popover`/`anchorRef`/menu-button shape).
- **25c**: `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s 4-card header row and `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`'s three uneven rows (4/5/1, now folded into one 9-card block since the Tabs removal above already merged them structurally) both converted from `.row.flex-wrap` to the existing `.grid.grid-metrics` utility — no new CSS.
- **Playwright**: `org-compliance-view.spec.ts` updated for the new IA — `selectOrgOverviewGroup(page, "Compliance overview")` → three separate calls with the new group labels; `getByRole("tab", ...)` clicks removed (no tabs left); the PDF download assertion now opens the Export popover first; new assertions for the three headline gauges appearing on page load with no group selected.
- **Storybook**: `OrgOverviewPage.stories.tsx` gained a fixture `orgOverviewTiles` contribution (alongside the pre-existing `orgOverviewSections` fixture) and two new stories (`CoreStatsRenderAsAGrid`, plus tile-visibility assertions folded into the existing enabled/disabled stories); `ComplianceOrgOverviewTiles.stories.tsx` (new); `OrgComplianceDashboard.stories.tsx` gained `StatsRenderAsOneEqualSizeGrid` and updated `DownloadReport` for the popover; `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.stories.tsx`/`OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel.stories.tsx`/`OrgComplianceDashboard.stories.tsx` had their `withRouter` initial paths corrected from the retired `/orgs/org-1/overview/compliance-overview` to each panel's own new group key (cosmetic, same as the identical correction Phase 19's own notes recorded).
- **`docs/ux-style-guide.md`**: gained a short paragraph naming the "headline tile in a page header, via a module-contributed render-prop field" shape (`orgOverviewTiles`) as a reusable pattern alongside `orgOverviewSections`, per the human-review request that these lessons generalise beyond compliance.
