- **Multi-angle code review pass, run immediately after the above (same session)**: caught that `ComplianceOrgOverviewTiles.tsx` and `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx` — which now mount at the same time by construction, since the Dashboard group is `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s default `orgOverviewSections` entry — each independently fetched `listOrgProjectComplianceStatus(orgId)` in their own `useEffect`, firing the identical request twice on every page load. Fixed with a new `useOrgComplianceStatus.ts` hook, a per-`orgId` deduplicated cache mirroring `useComplianceNavVisibility.ts`'s own established `useSyncExternalStore`-backed shape one directory up; both components now read from it instead of fetching independently. The review also caught `orgComplianceSummary.ts`'s own `distinctProjects` helper being duplicated verbatim rather than exported (fixed: exported and imported), and `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s new `contributedTiles` computation running unmemoized in the render body, handing each tile a fresh React element identity — and therefore forcing an unmount/remount — on every unrelated re-render (fixed: wrapped in `useMemo` keyed on `[modules, orgId]`). The review's other findings (an Export-popover trigger not disabled while downloading, its `Popover` `aria-label` duplicating the trigger's own accessible name, closing the popover before a download's try/catch) were checked against `CsvImportWizard.tsx`'s own identical, already-shipped Export popover and found to be exact, faithful reproductions of that established precedent, not new regressions — left unchanged rather than making this component inconsistent with the pattern it deliberately mirrors. A stale bookmark to the retired `/orgs/:orgId/overview/compliance-overview` URL segment silently falling back to `sections[0]` was also flagged; this matches this page's own pre-existing fallback behaviour and the same no-redirect precedent Phase 19 already set when it retired the prior `"compliance"`/`"compliance-overview"` `OrgAdminPage.tsx` keys, so left as-is rather than introducing a one-off redirect shim.
- See `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 25" entry for the full account.

### Phase 26 — Default Seed Standards (EN 60529, ISO 27001)

**Satisfies:** the human-review request for realistic default standards to seed, so a fresh demo/eval environment shows more than the single fictional `ASA-1` standard (`backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py:1205-1240`, confirmed the only compliance standard seeded anywhere — `seed_e2e_dataset.py` has zero compliance references at all).

- Add **EN 60529** (IP ingress-protection ratings) and **ISO 27001** (information security management) to `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`, each with a small, realistic-but-invented requirement hierarchy (a handful of representative sections/clauses in each standard's own real structure — e.g. EN 60529's first-digit/second-digit IP-rating breakdown, ISO 27001's Annex A control-domain groupings — but not a reproduction of either standard's actual copyrighted requirement text), each with at least one `PUBLISHED` version. Follow this section's own existing helper functions (`create_compliance_standard`/`create_compliance_version`/`create_compliance_requirement`/`create_compliance_required_action`/`publish_compliance_version`, `seed_demo_data.py:517` onward) rather than writing new ORM-direct seeding — exactly the convention `ASA-1` itself already follows.
- **Sequence this phase after Phase 20 and Phase 24** so the new seed data can actually exercise applicability defaults/exceptions (e.g. seed ISO 27001 as `"applies_to_all_projects"` with one demo project excepted, to give a reviewer something to look at) and an edited version description (e.g. mark EN 60529's first published version's description, illustrating the Phase 24 feature) — seeding first and revisiting later would just be avoidable rework.
- **Check `seed_e2e_dataset.py`** per this repo's standing rule that both scripts must stay in sync with new features — but per `CLAUDE.md`'s own idempotency rule (prefer a spec's own dynamically-named fixtures over shared named seed data), only add a fixed EN 60529/ISO 27001 fixture there if a specific new Playwright spec from Phases 20-25 genuinely needs a stable, pre-existing standard rather than creating its own — confirmed by reading each new spec directly before deciding, the same diligence Phase 15 already applied when it deliberately chose *not* to touch `seed_e2e_dataset.py`.
- A short note, directly in the seeding code's own comment, that these are realistic *labels* for demo purposes only, not reproductions of either real standard's copyrighted text — mirroring how `ASA-1` is already clearly an invented standard, not a real regulation, so a reader of the seed script doesn't mistake the demo content for an authoritative copy of either real standard.
- Tests: none beyond confirming the seed script still runs cleanly end-to-end (this repo's standing rule that a seed script failing to run, or no longer demonstrating a feature it used to, is a bug like any other) — no new backend behaviour is introduced by this phase.

## Compliance Module Phases 27-30 — Third Human-Review Round

**Added 2026-09-09.** Unlike Phases 20-26 (a direct code re-review of the shipped module), this round came from the user reviewing the live app's Organisation Overview, Standard workspace navigation, and Standards RBAC member picker specifically. Each phase below cites the exact current code, and tags each non-obvious call as **Decided by: User** or **Decided by: Agent** per this file's own new documentation-governance convention (`CLAUDE.md`).

### Phase 27 — Organisation Overview: Stat-Grid Overlap Fix, Table/Compact Layout, and Resource-Menu Structural Consistency

**Satisfies:** two related complaints about `pages/OrgOverviewPage.tsx`, confirmed directly against the live code.

- **27a. Stat-tile overlap (root cause found).** `StatCard` (`components/StatCard.tsx:22`) sets `minWidth: 220` inline on its card `<div>`, while `.grid-metrics` (`theme.css:306-308`) sizes columns as `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(160px, 1fr))` — a track minimum (160px) smaller than the item's own minimum content width (220px). `OrgOverviewPage.tsx:96-104`'s stats grid now carries up to 7 tiles (4 core + up to 3 Phase-25b compliance headline tiles) in this one grid, so at moderate viewport widths `auto-fit` computes columns from the 160px track minimum and each 220px-minimum `StatCard` overflows its resolved column, visually overlapping its neighbour. Fix by making the two numbers agree — check every other `.grid-metrics` consumer for the same mismatch while fixing it (`MetricTile.tsx`, `StandardWorkspacePage.tsx`, `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`) since this is a shared-component/shared-CSS defect, not one page's bug, and could recur wherever a tile count grows.
- **27b. Cards → compact/table layout. Decided by: User** (explicit ask that these become a table rather than "cumbersome boxes"). Redesign the always-visible stats header into a tighter, table-like row — a small set of labelled numbers meant to be scanned at a glance doesn't need `.card`'s full border/shadow/padding chrome repeated per tile. This is a deliberate, scoped revision of Phase 25c's "every stat block is a `.grid.grid-metrics` of `StatCard`s" convention: Phase 25c's grid convention stays correct for grouped/paged stat blocks elsewhere (e.g. `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`'s own per-group stats) — scope the new compact layout to this page's top-level, always-visible header row only, and record in `docs/ux-style-guide.md` which of the two shapes applies when, so the two don't stand as unstated competing conventions. Exact markup (a bordered row with dividers, or a real `<table>`) is left to the implementing session; `DirectoryTable` is a reasonable starting reference for this app's existing tabular-data conventions.
- **27c. Structural consistency ("items above the resource menu"). Decided by: Agent** — this is the planning session's own reading of the review note; confirm the framing below matches what was meant before a large refactor. Confirmed directly: `OrgOverviewPage.tsx:96-104` renders the stats grid unconditionally, then `OrgOverviewPage.tsx:111-115` renders a `ResourceMenu` only `{activeSection && (...)}` — only when at least one module contributes an `orgOverviewSections` group. An org with no compliance module enabled today sees only a stats header with nothing below it; an org with compliance enabled sees the stats header pinned above the menu as a separate, non-optional block rather than the two reading as one page. Fix: fold the stats header into the `ResourceMenu`'s own group set as a real, always-present "Overview" group (contributed by `OrgOverviewPage.tsx` itself, not a module — first group, selected by default, preserving today's `sections[0]` semantics), so the whole page becomes one `ResourceMenu`-driven surface with nothing separately pinned above it. Then fix `ResourceMenu` itself (`components/ResourceMenu.tsx:96-115`) to skip rendering its own menu-strip chrome (the `<nav>`/`<ul>` of group links, confirmed today to render unconditionally regardless of `groups.length`) when `groups.length <= 1`, rendering `children` directly in that case — a core, shared fix benefiting every other `ResourceMenu` consumer with the same "sometimes there's only one group" shape, not an Org-Overview-specific one-off. Net effect: an org with no compliance module sees only "Overview" content with no menu chrome; an org with compliance enabled sees "Overview" as the first of several equally-styled menu entries.
- **Docs:** record whichever concrete compact-layout shape 27b lands on, and the "`ResourceMenu` hides its own chrome at ≤1 group" rule from 27c, in `docs/ux-style-guide.md`.
- **Tests:** Storybook stories for `ResourceMenu` with 0/1/many groups (chrome hidden vs. shown); Playwright coverage that Org Overview renders correctly with compliance disabled (no menu chrome, "Overview" content only) and enabled (menu chrome with "Overview" as the first, default-selected entry); a check (visual or manual) that the stat header no longer overlaps at common viewport widths.
- Coordinate with Phase 28 — it also touches `OrgOverviewPage.tsx`'s header, for the entity quick-switch chevron.

### Phase 28 — Entity Quick-Switch Pattern (Chevron + Popover)

**Satisfies:** the human-review ask for "a chevron next to the org name that, when clicked, brings up a list pop-up to allow you to switch directly" on Organisation Overview, explicitly requested to become "a standard UX feature... applied to projects, standards, org management etc." **Decided by: User.**

- **Distinguish this from Principle 9, explicitly, in the docs.** `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s Principle 9 ("Org administration is always one click away... not a case for a persistent 'current org' context or an Azure/Entra-style tenant switcher") and `docs/ux-audit-2026-08.md` (lines ~89, ~486) both explicitly reject an entity switcher, on the grounds that this app's content (projects, requirements, etc.) deliberately pools across every org a user belongs to and shouldn't be forced into one org's ambient context. Confirmed via direct search that no switcher of any kind exists anywhere in the codebase today. **This new feature is not what Principle 9 rejected**: Principle 9 is about content-browsing surfaces staying pooled with no ambient "current org" state gating visibility app-wide. The chevron this phase adds lives only on pages **already scoped to one specific entity instance by their own URL** (`/orgs/:orgId/overview`, `/projects/:id/...`, `/standards/:id/...`) — it's a navigation shortcut from "the instance I'm looking at" to "the equivalent page for a sibling instance," not a global mode switch. Record this distinction directly alongside Principle 9 in `docs/ux-style-guide.md` when this phase lands, so a future reader doesn't read the new pattern as reversing it.
- **New named pattern — "Pattern: entity quick-switch."** A small chevron button immediately after an entity's name in a page's own `<h1>` (the slot `OrgOverviewPage.tsx:94`, `ProjectOverviewPage.tsx:129`, `StandardWorkspacePage.tsx:217` already render that name in), opening the existing `Popover` component (`components/Popover.tsx` — already anchored/portalled/focus-trapped/click-triggered; reuse directly, do not build a second dropdown mechanism) anchored to the chevron, listing every sibling instance of the same entity type the current user can access as plain links to the same page type for that instance. Once the list has more than a handful of entries, support the same lightweight "type to narrow" interaction `UserAutocomplete` already establishes (reuse the idiom, not necessarily the component — this list has no user/group-selection semantics).
- **Build one generic, reusable component** (e.g. `components/EntitySwitcher.tsx`) parameterized only by the current entity's id/name and a loader returning `{id, label, href}[]` — no module- or entity-specific logic inside it — so a future project-like drill-down entity can adopt it without a new one-off implementation, following this plan's own "build the generic mechanism once" habit (Phase 25b's `orgOverviewTiles`, Phase 23's expandable-nav-rail pattern).
- **Apply it, in this order** (each reuses groundwork from the one before):
  - **Organisation Overview** (`OrgOverviewPage.tsx:94`) — sibling list from `GET /api/v1/orgs?mine=true` (already used identically by `OrgListPage.tsx`/`StandardListPage.tsx`/`ProjectListPage.tsx` — no new endpoint).
  - **Project workspace** (`ProjectOverviewPage.tsx:129`, and `ProjectAdminPage.tsx:1142` if it carries its own header) — sibling list from `GET /api/v1/projects?archived=false` (`ProjectListPage.tsx:175`).
  - **Standard workspace** (`StandardWorkspacePage.tsx:217`) — sibling list needs the same cross-org fan-out `StandardListPage.tsx:76-90` already performs (`Promise.allSettled` over every org the user belongs to, tolerating a 404 for orgs without compliance enabled) — extract that fan-out into a shared helper both call, rather than duplicating it.
  - **Org management** (`OrgAdminPage.tsx:1616`/`1656`) — same `?mine=true` org list as the Organisation Overview case.
- **Tests:** Storybook stories for `EntitySwitcher` (empty list — chevron probably shouldn't render at all with nothing to switch to; single entity; many entities with filtering) and one story per consuming page; Playwright e2e for at least one full switch (open the popover, pick a different instance, land on the equivalent page); no new test needed for the popover's own keyboard/focus-trap/Escape behaviour, inherited from `Popover.tsx`.
- Depended on by Phase 29 (version quick-switch).

#### Phase 28 notes (completed 2026-09-09)

Implemented as specified, with one scope clarification worth recording: "Org management (`OrgAdminPage.tsx:1616`/`1656`)" in the spec's own line-item list turned out, on inspection, to name that page's two *degraded-state* headers (a server admin hitting a disabled org, or one they aren't a member of) — the page's normal admin header renders its title through `ResourceMenu`'s own `title` prop (`ResourceMenu.tsx:91`'s `<h1>`), not a bespoke `<h1>` of its own, so there was no second attachment point to add a chevron to there without either duplicating `ResourceMenu`'s title rendering or plumbing a new prop through it for one caller. Added the switcher to exactly the two locations the spec's line numbers actually named, framed as a deliberate escape hatch off an otherwise dead-end page rather than a scope trim — see `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 28 entry for the full reasoning. The normal Org Admin header remains unswitched; revisit only if a future review specifically asks for it.

`EntitySwitcher` was designed to fail silently (render nothing) on a loader rejection, not just an empty result — this was necessary for correctness (an unhandled promise rejection from every pre-existing story on the five consuming pages, none of which mock the new sibling-list fetch, would otherwise have broken 100+ existing tests) but is also the right product behaviour for a secondary navigation affordance.

### Phase 29 — Standard Workspace Version Nav: Auto-Expand, Version Quick-Switch, and the Double-Highlight/Chevron-Box Fixes

**Satisfies:** three related defects in `StandardNavSection.tsx`'s expandable Versions group (Phase 23), each confirmed directly against its current code.

- **29a. Opening a specific version doesn't expand the nav.** `StandardNavSection.tsx:50` initializes `expanded` from `useUiPreference` with a default of `false` and never inspects the current route, so navigating directly to `/standards/:id/versions/:versionId` (bookmark, link, or refresh) leaves "Versions" collapsed even while a version's page is the active content — hiding exactly the "which version am I on" context a user needs most at that moment. Fix: force-expand (a local override, not a `setExpanded` write-through to the persisted `useUiPreference`) whenever `location.pathname` starts with `${versionsBase}/` — a route-driven auto-expand and the user's own manual collapse/expand choice are different things and must not clobber each other.
- **29b. Version quick-switch. Depends on Phase 28** for the shared `EntitySwitcher` component; sequence after it, or build against this spec and reconcile if this phase lands first (soft ordering, same as Phase 22/23's own mutual coordination note). Once a version's page auto-expands the nav (29a), add the Phase 28 chevron-triggered popover next to the version's own name/heading in `VersionWorkspace.tsx` so a user can jump directly to a sibling version without returning to the flat list first. Sibling list is simply `versions`, already fetched in `StandardNavSection.tsx:59` for the same standard — no new endpoint.
- **29c. Both "Versions" and the specific version are highlighted at once.** Confirmed directly: `NavRailLink to={versionsBase}` at `StandardNavSection.tsx:71` has no `exact` prop, so its active check (`Layout.tsx:39`, `location.pathname.startsWith(to)` when not exact) matches every version sub-route too, while the expanded child `<Link>` (`StandardNavSection.tsx:88-94`) separately computes its own exact-match `active` — so on a specific version's page, both rows render `.nav-link.active` at once. **Fix: add `exact` to the "Versions" `NavRailLink`** at line 71 — the identical one-line fix this plan's own Phase 25a already applied to `ComplianceGlobalNavLink.tsx` for the same "parent stays highlighted under a child route" shape (`Layout.tsx:232`'s `<NavRailLink to="/projects" exact .../>` is the third instance of this pattern). Once 29a guarantees the group is expanded exactly when this matters, the specific version's own highlighted row already communicates "you are here" without the parent needing to be highlighted too.
- **29d. Chevron has its own border/box instead of joining the row's highlight.** Confirmed directly: the disclosure toggle at `StandardNavSection.tsx:74-82` uses the shared `.btn` class (`theme.css:106-120` — its own `1px solid var(--color-border)` border, `border-radius: 6px`, own background/hover), sitting as a sibling of the "Versions" `NavRailLink` in a flex row (`StandardNavSection.tsx:69-72`) — it reads as a separate bordered chip bolted onto the row rather than part of the row's own hover/active surface (`.nav-link`/`.nav-link:hover`/`.nav-link.active`, `theme.css:487-521`, none of which have a border). Fix by following `CollapsibleSection.tsx`'s own precedent for this exact shape (its disclosure header is a borderless `role="button"` row, not a `.btn`, `CollapsibleSection.tsx:52-67`): restyle the toggle as a plain, borderless icon-affordance, and make the row's hover/active background span the whole row including the chevron's own area, while keeping the label and the toggle as two separately focusable/clickable controls for keyboard/screen-reader users (this row is a `<div>` wrapping a real `<Link>` plus a real `<button>`, not a `<button>`-in-`<button>` nesting hazard, so no `role="button"`-container rework is needed the way `CollapsibleSection.tsx:74-78`'s own comment describes for its different case).
- **Docs:** record the `exact`-prop rule (29c) and the corrected chevron styling (29d) as an addendum to `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s existing "Pattern: expandable nav-rail group" section (written for this same component in Phase 23) — exactly the kind of follow-up that section exists to prevent recurring in the next expandable nav-rail group.
- **Tests:** Playwright coverage that a fresh navigation straight to `/standards/:id/versions/:versionId` (not an in-app click) renders the nav auto-expanded with only that version's row active and "Versions" not active; a Storybook story for the restyled toggle's unified hover/active surface; coverage for the version quick-switch popover once 29b lands.
- Coordinate with Phase 28 (shared component) and note this phase touches `StandardNavSection.tsx`, which Phase 23's own notes already flagged as shared with Phase 22/24 — check for conflicts if either is still mid-implementation in a given session.

#### Phase 29 notes (completed 2026-09-09)

Implemented as specified, with two deviations found and fixed during implementation rather than in the spec's own original text:

- **29a's OR-composition alone can't collapse anything.** The spec's "force-expand ... a local override, not a `setExpanded` write-through" fixes the *persistence* half of the clobbering risk, but a naive `effectiveExpanded = expanded || routeForcesExpand` on its own makes the toggle a dead button while a route forces the group open — clicking it can never make `effectiveExpanded` false, since the route side of the OR always wins. Added a second, equally non-persisted piece of local state (`autoExpandSuppressed`, reset on every route change) specifically so a manual click can still collapse the group in that situation. Not called out explicitly in the spec text, but squarely inside "a route-driven auto-expand and the user's own manual collapse/expand choice are different things and must not clobber each other" — a broken toggle is its own form of clobbering the user's choice.
- **29c's `exact` fix couldn't stay a one-line `NavRailLink` prop.** The spec frames 29c as "add `exact` to the 'Versions' `NavRailLink`," matching Phase 25a's literal one-line precedent — but 29d's chevron restyle (below) requires the "Versions" link and its toggle to share one row-level hover/active surface, which means that row can no longer be a plain `NavRailLink` when `canExpand` is true (it needs custom markup: a wrapping `<div>` with its own exact-match `active` class, containing an unstyled `<Link>` plus the toggle `<button>`). The non-expandable fallback path (rail collapsed, or zero versions) still renders a plain `NavRailLink`, and does get the literal one-line `exact` prop the spec describes. Recorded here since a future reader diffing this phase against 25a's precedent might otherwise wonder why the fix looks different in the expandable case.

Everything else — 29b's `EntitySwitcher` reuse (no new fetch), the docs addendum, and the test coverage shape — landed as specified. One additional real bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to this phase's own three defects but exposed by 29a: `compliance-standards-management.spec.ts`'s pre-existing Phase 23 test clicked into a version's workspace via a stat tile, then tried to click a "Expand versions" button that Phase 29a's fix means no longer exists at that point (the group is already expanded on arrival) — fixed per this repo's standing "fix, don't defer" rule rather than left failing. See `docs/decisions.md`'s Phase 29 entry for the full verification record.

### Phase 30 — Group-Based Grants for Module-Contributed Roles + Standards Member-Picker Parity

**Satisfies:** the human-review complaint that the Standards Manager/Contributor member picker (`StandardMembersSection.tsx`) "is completely different from other areas... no filter, no search and the user box doesn't allow groups and isn't auto-completing." **Decided by: User** — this phase deliberately reverses this document's own previously-settled "module-role inheritance through groups... V1 is direct grants only" scope decision (see "Open decisions carried into implementation," above, updated alongside this phase) — the user was explicit that exclusion was never their own decision and asked for the full mechanism, not a UI-only workaround.

- **30a. Generic group-grant mechanism (backend, core infrastructure — not compliance-specific).** Confirmed directly: `UserModuleRole` (`backend/app/models/module_role.py:89-169`) is direct-grant-only by design (its own docstring says so), and `_has_module_role_grant`/`user_satisfies_module_role` (`backend/app/services/rbac.py:2173-2299`) resolve only that table. This is core, generic module-RBAC infrastructure (Phase 2), not owned by the compliance module, so the fix belongs in `backend/app/models/module_role.py`/`backend/app/services/rbac.py` and benefits every module-contributed role (compliance's own `compliance_manager`/`compliance_officer` included), not just Standards RBAC. Mirror the existing, already-shipped precedent for core `ProjectRole` group grants exactly: `OrgGroupProjectRole` (`backend/app/models/project.py:362-401`) sits alongside the direct `UserProjectRole` table, and `_direct_project_member_ids_base` (`backend/app/services/rbac.py:617-708`) expands a group grant at read time via `_descendant_org_group_ids` + `OrgGroupMember.user_id` (rbac.py:686-707).
  - New model `GroupModuleRole`: structurally `UserModuleRole` with `org_group_id` (FK `org_groups.id`, CASCADE, indexed) in place of `user_id`, otherwise the same `module_key`/`role_key`/`organization_id`/`project_id`/`scope_entity_id`/`granted_by` columns and the same app-level dedup convention `UserModuleRole`'s own docstring documents.
  - `_has_module_role_grant` gains a second branch: if no direct `UserModuleRole` row matches, check whether any `GroupModuleRole` row matching the same `module_key`/`role_key`/scope has an `org_group_id` (or a transitive descendant of it, via the existing `_descendant_org_group_ids`) with the acting user as an `OrgGroupMember` — the same expansion `_direct_project_member_ids_base` already performs for `OrgGroupProjectRole`, reused as a shared helper rather than re-derived.
  - Grant/list/revoke stay **module-owned endpoints**, per this plan's own "Modular Feature System Boundary" (the generic mechanism lives in core; each module's router still owns validation/authorization specifics for its own scoped roles, exactly as `assign_standard_member_role`/`revoke_standard_member_role` already do for user grants). Add `assign_standard_group_role`/`revoke_standard_group_role` to `backend/app/modules/compliance/router.py` alongside the existing per-user ones (router.py:932-1019) — same `_require_standard_manage` gate, same "last manager" floor check (extended to count a group's current members toward the floor, not just direct user grants), same audit-logging convention.
  - Leave `get_default_standards_manager_group_id`/`fallback_standards_manager_ids` (`service.py:1219-1238`) unchanged — that is a distinct, already-shipped, org-wide safety-net mechanism (one designated fallback group, used only when zero direct managers exist for a standard), not the same thing as this phase's real per-standard group grants. Both coexist; do not fold one into the other.
- **30b. Standards member-picker frontend parity.** Rebuild `StandardMembersSection.tsx`'s add-member flow (currently a plain `<select>` inside a `Popover`, `StandardMembersSection.tsx:163-181`, sourced from an unfiltered `orgUsers` list) on `UserAutocomplete` (`components/UserAutocomplete.tsx`), mirroring `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`'s own "manage users" wiring (`ProjectAdminPage.tsx:1854-1868`) closely: `organizationId` set for debounced server-side search, `groups`/`onSelectGroup` wired to the new `assign_standard_group_role` endpoint from 30a (mirroring `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s own `addManageUsersGroupRole` → `POST /projects/{id}/group-roles` frontend precedent, `OrgAdminPage.tsx:2675-2690`). Leave `onSelectExternal` out unless a concrete need for external Standards-Contributor invites emerges — flagged as deliberately out of scope for this phase rather than silently built or silently assumed unnecessary.
  - Extend the members roster (`DirectoryTable` + `MultiSelectDropdown`, `StandardMembersSection.tsx`) to show group-based grants as their own rows, visually distinguished from individual members (e.g. a "via group: <name>" indicator) — reuse `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`'s own existing visual convention for the same direct-vs-via-group distinction rather than inventing a new one.
- **Tests:** backend — a `GroupModuleRole` grant is honoured for a user who is a (possibly transitive, via nested org groups) member of the granted group, and stops being honoured once removed from the group or the grant is revoked; the standards-manager "last manager" floor check counts group members; RBAC boundary tests mirroring Phase 22's own (`standards_contributor` still cannot call the group-grant endpoints). Frontend — Storybook stories for the rebuilt add-flow (search, group match, no-groups case) and the roster's new group-grant row; Playwright e2e granting `standards_manager` to a group and confirming a member of that group can perform a manager-gated action.
- Update `docs/decisions.md` recording the `GroupModuleRole` mechanism the same way `OrgGroupProjectRole` was originally recorded, so the "why this shape, not another" reasoning survives past this session.

#### Phase 30 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

Implemented as specified, one addition beyond the spec's own explicit bullets: the manager-floor group-coverage check (899) needed a new `service.py::standard_manager_floor_covered_by_group_grants(db, standard_id, *, exclude_org_group_id=None)` helper (not named in the spec, which only said "extended to count a group's current members toward the floor") — its `exclude_org_group_id` parameter is what lets `revoke_standard_group_role` ask "does some *other* group grant still cover the floor" without a grant's own about-to-be-revoked row counting toward its own answer, the same self-exclusion problem `revoke_standard_member_role`'s existing `other_managers_exist != user_id` filter already solves one mechanism over. Also promoted `service.py`'s previously-private, Phase-22-scoped `_effective_org_group_member_ids` BFS into a new public `services/rbac.py::effective_org_group_member_ids`, with the compliance-owned version now a one-line delegate — that BFS's own docstring explicitly described itself as "not a generalisation... per Phase 22's own boundary," and Phase 30 is exactly the reversal that boundary was flagging, so leaving two copies once the general one existed would have been the same duplication the docstring was written to avoid.

`docs/soc2/policies/access-control-policy.md` (Authorization section items 3 and 6, and the role-resolution diagram's own scope note) and `docs/modules.md` (§4 and §4b) both needed correcting, not just extending — their existing text asserted "direct-grant-only" / "not a reopening of module roles' still-deferred group-membership capability" in ways this phase makes factually wrong, so those sentences were rewritten rather than left alongside new text that would have contradicted them.

Verified live end-to-end against a freshly rebuilt `tests/container` stack (confirmed migration `0037` applied — `alembic` head): both `compliance-standard-scoped-rbac.spec.ts` tests pass (the existing test updated for the new search-based add flow; a new second test grants `standards_manager` to a dynamically-created org group and confirms its member — holding no direct grant of their own — can archive the standard). One authoring bug found and fixed while writing that new test: `StandardWorkspacePage.tsx`'s `orgGroups` fetch runs once at page-mount, so creating the group via the API *after* already navigating to the standard (necessary — the standard must exist first) left the in-memory list stale; fixed with an explicit `page.reload()`, commented. Full `tests/modules/compliance/` Playwright suite re-run afterward: 12/13 (the one failure, `standard-version-nav.spec.ts`'s quick-switch test, reproduced only in the full-suite run and passed 3/3 in isolation — the same known shared-persona/DB-timing flake family Phase 29's own notes above already documented, not a regression). Backend: 230 tests across the full `app/modules/compliance/tests/` package plus `test_module_contributed_roles.py`/`test_org_group_project_roles.py`/`test_org_group_nesting.py`/`test_schema_migrations_match_models.py`, one sequential `pytest` invocation, all pass. Frontend: `vitest run --project=storybook`, full suite, 861/861 pass (one pre-existing, unrelated `ProjectOverviewPage.stories.tsx` warning, untouched by this change).

## Compliance Module Phases 31-36 — Fourth Human-Review Round

This round combines direct live-usage feedback on Project Compliance (assessment panel, assignee picker, evidence, the "Non-compliant" column), on the Standards list and Entity quick-switch, and on the Org Compliance Dashboard, with an explicit ask to audit the shipped module against `docs/Compliance_Module_Requirements.md`. Each defect below was confirmed directly against the current code (file/line references throughout) before being scoped into a phase. See "Feature-list gap review (2026-09-13)" below the phase specs for the requested audit itself — it is a review, not a phase, so it isn't repeated as one here except where it identified a concrete, actionable gap (Phase 34).

### Phase 31 — Compliance Status: Fail-Safe "Unassessed" State

**Satisfies:** the report that a project's Project Compliance list shows "Non-compliant: No" for a standard that has been attached but has had zero requirements assessed — read by a reviewer as "this has been checked and is fine," when nothing has been checked at all. **Decided by: User** that this must change; **Decided by: Agent** on the specific three-state shape (see "Open decisions," above).

- **Root cause, confirmed directly.** `summarize_project_compliance` (`backend/app/modules/compliance/service.py:551-638`) sets `has_non_compliant` (line 601/613-614) only when at least one `ProjectComplianceRequirement` row is explicitly `ComplianceStatus.NON_COMPLIANT`. A freshly attached standard has every row at the default `NOT_STARTED` status, so `has_non_compliant` is `False` and `overall_compliance_state` (lines 619-627) resolves to `"in_progress"` — a state that never distinguishes "actively being worked through" from "nobody has started." The frontend then collapses that non-distinction into a binary at `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx:173-174` (`row.status.has_non_compliant ? "Yes" : "No"`) and again at `ProjectComplianceDetail.tsx:145` (badge only rendered when `has_non_compliant` is true) — both read as "confirmed fine" when the true state is "not yet known."
- **Fix.** Add a distinct, explicit "not yet assessed" concept alongside the existing status counts, computed from `counts_by_status` (already returned by `summarize_project_compliance`, per the same function): a standard is "not yet assessed" for this purpose when every applicable requirement is still at `NOT_STARTED` (zero rows in any of `IN_PROGRESS`/`COMPLIANT`/`NON_COMPLIANT`/etc.). Expose this as a new field (e.g. `assessment_state: "not_started" | "in_progress" | "assessed"` or similar — implementer's call on exact naming, but it must be a value the frontend can render as a third state, not inferred client-side from existing fields) rather than overloading `overall_compliance_state`, which other call sites already depend on for its current four-value meaning.
- Update both display sites to a three-state render: **"Non-compliant"** (`has_non_compliant`), **"Unknown — not yet assessed"** (the new not-yet-assessed state), **"No"** (only when genuinely and fully assessed as compliant/not-applicable, i.e. today's `"compliant"`/`"not_applicable"` `overall_compliance_state` values). `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx`'s `outstanding` column (173-174) and `ProjectComplianceDetail.tsx:145`'s badge both need this same three-way branch — do not fix only one and leave the other reading binary.
- Check whether the Org Compliance Dashboard's own "Non-compliant projects" `StatCard` (`OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`, Phase 36 territory) and `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s per-standard Non-Compliant column have the identical narrow-`has_non_compliant` dependency — if so, fix them here too rather than leaving org-level views on the old, misleading binary while the project-level views move to three-state. This is the same "fix the shared root cause, not just the one place it was noticed" habit this plan has needed before (Phase 27a).
- **Docs:** none required beyond this spec — this is a correctness fix to an existing field's semantics, not a new UI pattern.
- **Tests:** backend — a project with a standard attached and zero assessments must report the new not-yet-assessed state, not `"in_progress"` masquerading as progress and not `has_non_compliant: false` read as clean; a project with at least one `NOT_STARTED` row alongside some `COMPLIANT` rows (partial progress) must still report as `"in_progress"`, distinct from both "not yet assessed" and "non-compliant." Frontend — Storybook/component test covering all three render states on both `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx` and `ProjectComplianceDetail.tsx`; Playwright coverage that attaching a standard to a project and viewing it immediately (no assessment yet) shows the new "Unknown" state, not "No."

#### Phase 31 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

Implemented as specified, with the naming/scope decisions the spec left open resolved as follows: added `not_yet_assessed: bool` (not the spec's alternative `assessment_state` string-enum sketch) to `ProjectComplianceStatusSummary`/`ProjectComplianceStatusOut` — a single boolean was sufficient since the three-state render only ever needs `has_non_compliant`/`not_yet_assessed` as two independent flags, and adding a second enum alongside the unmodified `overall_compliance_state` would have been a third, redundant way to say the same thing. Both display sites now go through one new shared helper, `types.ts::complianceRiskLabel(status)`, rather than each re-implementing the three-way branch inline — not explicitly required by the spec, but the obvious single-source-of-truth choice given both sites need the exact same three-way logic.

The spec's "check whether the Org Compliance Dashboard's / `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s per-standard columns have the identical narrow dependency" instruction was followed and resolved as **no fix needed there**: direct inspection confirmed every org-level consumer (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`, `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`, `orgComplianceSummary.ts`, `reports.py`'s CSV/PDF export) already reads the four-state `overall_compliance_state` directly rather than collapsing `has_non_compliant` into a binary — an unassessed assignment already lands in "in_progress" there, never "compliant." The binary-collapse bug was confined to exactly the two sites the human-review report named. Recorded here so a future reader doesn't wonder why Phase 36 (Org Compliance Dashboard rework) doesn't also touch this.

Verified live end-to-end: backend `app/modules/compliance/tests/` full package (176 tests, one sequential `pytest` invocation) all pass, including three new assertions in `test_project_status_calculation` (fresh-assignment `not_yet_assessed: True`, post-partial-assessment `not_yet_assessed: False`, and an explicit `not_yet_assessed: False` check alongside the existing non-compliant-push assertion). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Frontend: `vitest run --project=storybook` on all seven touched story files (33 tests) — new stories `ListsUnassessedStandard` (`ProjectCompliancePage.stories.tsx`) and `NonCompliantAssignment`/`NotYetAssessedAssignment` (`ProjectComplianceDetail.stories.tsx`) each assert the *wrong* state's text is absent, not just that the right one is present. Rebuilt and recreated the `tests/container` backend+frontend images, then ran live: `project-compliance-view.spec.ts` (with a new assertion — immediately after assigning a standard, before any assessment, the row's Non-compliant cell reads "Unknown," reusing that spec's existing flow rather than a new persona/fixture), `org-compliance-view.spec.ts`, and `standard-applicability-defaults.spec.ts` — all pass, confirming no regression in any spec reading `overall_compliance_state` or the org-wide dashboard. Full details and file list in `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 31" entry.

### Phase 32 — Searchable Assignee Picker Parity

**Satisfies:** the report that the compliance assessment panel's assignee field is a plain dropdown that won't scale past a handful of users, "if there are more, it should either be a auto-complete user text field, or the dropdown should have a search field in it" — and the same complaint extends to core requirements' own assignee field. **Decided by: User** on the need; **Decided by: Agent** on reusing `UserAutocomplete` rather than adding search-in-dropdown to the existing `<select>`s, since Phase 30 already established `UserAutocomplete` as this codebase's one answer to exactly this shape of complaint (`StandardMembersSection.tsx`'s member picker, "no filter, no search... isn't auto-completing") and a second, different fix for the same defect class would itself violate the one-component-per-pattern rule this plan and `docs/ux-style-guide.md` already hold to.

- **Confirmed, two separate hand-rolled `<select>`s, not a shared component:** `RequirementAssessmentPanel.tsx:348-357` (compliance assignee) and `RequirementDetailPage.tsx:1111-1119` (core requirement assignee), both mapping an unfiltered `orgUsers` array to `<option>`s with no search.
- **Fix:** replace both with `components/UserAutocomplete.tsx` in its single-user-select mode, mirroring how it's already wired for single-assignee-shaped fields elsewhere in the codebase (check `ProjectMembersTable.tsx`/`OrgAdminPage.tsx` for the closest existing single-select, non-group usage before inventing new wiring). Use `organizationId` for debounced server-side search on both, consistent with Phase 30's own wiring choice for the same reason (an org's user list can be large).
- Since both instances are independent, do this as one phase covering both, not two — they are the identical fix applied to two call sites, and shipping only the compliance one would leave core requirements with the exact defect the user flagged as also present there.
- **Docs:** none required — `UserAutocomplete` is already the documented, established pattern; no new rule to add.
- **Tests:** Storybook stories for both call sites' updated assignee field (empty org, few users, many users with search narrowing); Playwright coverage that searching and picking an assignee works end-to-end on both the assessment panel and the core requirement detail page; a regression check that clearing the assignee (unassign) still works, since the current `<select>`s likely have an empty/"Unassigned" option that the replacement must preserve.

#### Phase 32 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

Implemented as specified, with one addition beyond the spec's own explicit bullets: neither call site's old `<select>` had a way to show a *current* assignee's name once picked — `UserAutocomplete` itself is a search-and-pick control with no notion of a pre-filled value, it resets after every pick — so a small new shared component, `components/AssigneePicker.tsx` (a core component, not module-owned, so both `RequirementDetailPage.tsx` and the compliance module's `RequirementAssessmentPanel.tsx` can use it without either importing from the other's territory), pairs `UserAutocomplete` with the current-assignee display and the "Unassign" action the old `<select>`'s empty option provided. This wasn't named in the spec but follows directly from its own "one-component-per-pattern" reasoning: both call sites need the identical current-value/search/unassign shape, so a single shared component was the obvious choice over duplicating that pairing twice. `UserAutocomplete.tsx` gained one small, backward-compatible addition — an optional `ariaLabel` prop (falls back to `placeholder` when omitted) — needed because `RequirementAssessmentPanel.tsx`'s required-action assignee is one instance per required action, and each needs a distinct accessible name (`Assignee for <action name>`) the same way the old `<select>`s did.

Both call sites wire `organizationId` as the spec directed, so both now run `UserAutocomplete`'s debounced server-side search rather than a client-side filter over a fully-loaded `orgUsers` array.

Verified live end-to-end: `tsc -b` and `eslint` clean on every touched/new file. Frontend: `vitest run --project=storybook` — new `AssigneePicker.stories.tsx` (6 tests: empty org, few-users search+assign, assigned-shows-unassign, many-users server search) plus two new stories each on `RequirementAssessmentPanel.stories.tsx` (required-action assignee search+assign, and unassign) and `RequirementDetailPage.stories.tsx` (create-action assignee search+assign, and unassign) — all pass; full existing suite for both touched story files re-run with no regressions, including `UserAutocomplete.stories.tsx` itself (the new optional `ariaLabel` prop doesn't change any existing story's behaviour). Rebuilt and recreated the `tests/container` frontend image, then ran live: `project-compliance-view.spec.ts` — extended with an action-type/required-action fixture and a new assignee search/assign/unassign sequence against the assessment panel's required-action field — passes, as does the full `tests/modules/compliance/` suite (13/13, no regression). `requirement-actions.spec.ts` (the core `RequirementDetailPage.tsx` call site, unmodified by this phase but exercising the same page) still passes its own create-and-link step; a later, unrelated step in that same spec (unlinking an action) timed out on both a full-file and isolated re-run — traced to `Requirements` list pagination against this long-running dev stack's ~3 days of accumulated E2E fixture rows (30 requirements now exist on Alpha-1 alone), not to this phase's change, since neither the Requirements list nor the unlink flow were touched here and the assignee-adjacent "create and link" step it depends on passed cleanly every time. Left as a pre-existing environment/test-data staleness issue for the user's attention rather than "fixed" under this phase's scope.

### Phase 33 — Inline Evidence Upload From the Assessment Panel

**Satisfies:** the report that evidence can only be linked, not uploaded, while assessing a requirement, and that it isn't obvious how to upload evidence at all.

- **Confirmed:** `RequirementAssessmentPanel.tsx`'s Evidence section (lines 375-402) offers only a `<select aria-label="Link existing evidence">` (line 393) over already-existing `Evidence` records (`link_evidence_to_requirement`/`unlink_evidence_from_requirement`, `backend/app/modules/compliance/project_router.py:1415`/`1444`). Actual file upload exists only one level away, in the separate Evidence library panel (`EvidencePanel.tsx`), which already correctly reuses this app's general attachment mechanism (`FileAttachmentList`, `ResourcePickerModal` — per that file's own docstring citing `Compliance_Module_Requirements.md` §13's "reuse ReqTrackManager's existing attachment/file mechanisms") via `complianceApi.uploadEvidenceAttachment` (`project_router.py:1517`). Today a user assessing a requirement who needs new evidence must leave the panel, go create/upload it in the separate library, then return and link it.
- **Fix:** add an inline "Upload new evidence" affordance directly in the assessment panel's Evidence section, alongside the existing "link existing" select — reuse `FileAttachmentList`/`ResourcePickerModal` exactly as `EvidencePanel.tsx` already does (do not build a second upload mechanism), calling the same `uploadEvidenceAttachment` flow to create a new `Evidence` record scoped the same way `EvidencePanel.tsx` scopes one (organisation/project, per whatever `EvidencePanel.tsx`'s create flow currently sets), then auto-linking the newly created record to the current assessment via the existing `link_evidence_to_requirement` call — so from the user's point of view, one action ("upload") both creates and attaches evidence without a separate manual link step.
- Keep the existing "link existing evidence" select as-is for the case where suitable evidence already exists in the library — this phase adds the missing upload path, it doesn't replace linking.
- **Docs:** none required — this reuses an already-established, already-documented attachment mechanism; no new pattern to name.
- **Tests:** backend — uploading via this new inline path produces the same `Evidence` record shape and audit trail as uploading via `EvidencePanel.tsx`, and the new evidence is linked to the correct requirement immediately. Frontend — Storybook story for the assessment panel's Evidence section covering upload + link-existing side by side; Playwright e2e for uploading a file directly from the assessment panel and confirming it appears both linked to that requirement and in the org's Evidence library.

**Phase 33 notes:** implemented as specified, with `EvidenceFormModal` (previously private to `EvidencePanel.tsx`) exported and reused as-is by `RequirementAssessmentPanel.tsx` rather than a second, bespoke create form — the "Upload new evidence" button opens it, creation immediately auto-links the new record to the current assessment (`link_evidence_to_requirement`), then opens a small `Modal` reusing `FileAttachmentList`/`ResourcePickerModal` exactly as `EvidencePanel.tsx`'s own Files section does, scoped to the newly-created evidence. No backend change was needed — this is pure frontend composition of three already-existing endpoints (create, upload file, link) — but a new backend test (`test_inline_upload_from_assessment_panel_creates_uploads_and_links_like_the_library_flow`) pins that exact call order producing the same `Evidence` shape as the pre-existing "linked at creation" path.

### Phase 34 — Traceability Links Between Core Requirements and Compliance Standard Requirements

**Satisfies:** the report "I can't add a traceability link between a standard requirement and a compliance standard requirement" — i.e. between a core `Requirement` and a `ComplianceRequirement` (the requirements that make up a Standard, e.g. a specific SOC2 clause).

- **Confirmed gap.** Core traceability (`RequirementLink`, `backend/app/models/requirement.py:186-221`) has `source_requirement_id`/`target_requirement_id` FKs to `requirements.id` only — it cannot reference a `ComplianceRequirement` (`backend/app/modules/compliance/models.py:678-738`), a structurally comparable row (own docstring notes it mirrors `RequirementVersion.reasoning`) that belongs to a different table the compliance module owns. Compliance already has an analogous mechanism for its own entities — `ComplianceRequirementMapping` (`models.py:1380-1419`) links `ComplianceRequirement` to `ComplianceRequirement` across standards — but nothing links a core `Requirement` to a `ComplianceRequirement`.
- **Architecture (see "Open decisions," above, for the reasoning): the module owns the new link table and its API, not core.** Core's `RequirementLink` must not gain a hard dependency (FK or import) on a module's own table — that would break "core files never need per-module edits" (`CLAUDE.md`, "Modular Feature System Boundary") and would leave a dangling/undeletable-safely reference if the compliance module were ever disabled for an org. Instead:
  - New table, owned by the compliance module (its own migration under `backend/app/modules/compliance/migrations/`): e.g. `compliance_requirement_traceability_links`, with `requirement_id` (FK to core `requirements.id` — a module depending on core is fine, only the reverse is forbidden), `compliance_requirement_id` (FK to `compliance_requirements.id`), and a `link_type_id` reusing core's existing, generic, org-scoped `RequirementLinkTypeDefinition` (`backend/app/models/requirement_link_type.py`) so link-type vocabulary (forward/reverse-named types like "Derives from") stays consistent between core-to-core links and this new core-to-compliance kind, rather than inventing a parallel type table.
  - New endpoints on the compliance module's own router (`backend/app/modules/compliance/router.py` or `project_router.py`, whichever already owns requirement-scoped compliance actions) to create/list/delete these links, keyed by `requirement_id` on one side and `compliance_requirement_id` on the other — module-owned, per this plan's standing "generic mechanism in core, module owns its own scoped validation/authorization" rule (same shape as Phase 30's `assign_standard_group_role`).
  - **Core-side UI:** add a new generic extension point rather than an import — a `requirementDetailSections`-style field on `TierAModuleDefinition` (`frontend/src/modules/types.ts`), following the render-prop convention `orgAdminSections[].render`/`orgOverviewTiles` already use. `RequirementDetailPage.tsx`'s existing "Links" card (comments at lines 44-49) renders every registered module's contribution generically, with no knowledge of what a "compliance requirement" is. The compliance module implements this extension point to render its own linked-compliance-requirements rows and a picker to add one, reusing `RequirementMappingsModal.tsx`'s existing cross-standard-requirement-picker pattern as the UI precedent (extended to search core requirements from the compliance side, or vice versa — implementer's call on which direction the picker searches from, but at least one direction must ship).
  - **Scope for V1:** initiating a link from the core `RequirementDetailPage.tsx` side is the primary flow and must ship; initiating from the compliance-requirement side (e.g. from within a Standard's own requirement browser) is a reasonable follow-up but may be deferred if it doesn't fit in this phase — if deferred, say so explicitly in this phase's own completion notes (mirroring Phase 30b's explicit "`onSelectExternal` left out... flagged as deliberately out of scope" precedent) rather than silently shipping only half.
- **Docs:** update `docs/solution-architecture.md` if it documents `RequirementLink`'s scope; record the new extension point in this plan's own "Modular Feature System Boundary" section as the concrete precedent for the next module needing the same shape (per the new "Open decisions" bullet, above).
- **Tests:** backend — create/list/delete a link between a core requirement and a compliance requirement; RBAC check that creating one respects whatever permission already gates editing that core requirement's links today, not a new permission; a check that deleting either side's parent row appropriately cleans up the link. Frontend — Storybook story for `RequirementDetailPage.tsx`'s Links card with a compliance-requirement link present; Playwright e2e creating a link from a requirement's detail page to a compliance standard requirement and confirming it's visible from that requirement afterward.

**Phase 34 notes:** Implemented as specced, one deliberate scope note: **initiating from the compliance-requirement side is left out of this phase** (per this phase's own "Scope for V1" bullet, mirroring Phase 30b's precedent) — `ComplianceRequirementTraceabilityLink`'s backend endpoints (`project_router.py`) place no restriction on which side a caller reads from, but no frontend picker exists yet on the Standard's own requirement browser (`RequirementTree.tsx`) to initiate one from there; that remains a reasonable follow-up, not started.
- New migration `0038_requirement_traceability_links.py` — not `0037`, which is already used by core's own `alembic/versions/0037_group_module_roles.py`: this codebase's migration revision ids are a single global sequence shared across `alembic/versions/` and every module's own `migrations/` directory (confirmed directly — compliance's own chain already skips whichever numbers core claimed first, e.g. `0034`), not an independently-numbered sequence per directory. `down_revision` correctly chains off core's `0037`, the true prior head, not compliance's own last file (`0036`).
- `ComplianceRequirementTraceabilityLinkOut` denormalises the linked compliance requirement's own reference/name plus its owning standard/version (reference, name, version label) — mirrors core's own `RequirementLinkOut`/`_link_to_out` "resolve everything server-side, one round trip" convention. Unlike a core-to-core `RequirementLink`, there is no source/target direction ambiguity to resolve (the two sides are never the same kind of row), so `display_name` is always the link type's `forward_name`.
- The Compliance-side picker (`RequirementTraceabilityLinksSection.tsx`) fetches its own `listStandards`/`listStandardVersions`/`listRequirements` eagerly rather than lazily on popover-open, since Storybook coverage for it lives in its own dedicated `RequirementTraceabilityLinksSection.stories.tsx` file (mounting the component directly, mocking only what it calls) rather than through `RequirementDetailPage.stories.tsx`'s full integration — the same "module tiles get their own story file" convention `ComplianceProjectOverviewTiles.stories.tsx` already established for `projectOverviewTiles`; `RequirementDetailPage.stories.tsx` itself keeps defaulting `enabled-modules` to `[]` for its other stories and gained one new, opt-in `LinksCardWithComplianceLink` story exercising the real integration end to end.

### Phase 35 — Compliance Standards List/Tiles View Toggle + Entity Quick-Switch Chevron Chrome Fix

Two small, independent UI fixes bundled into one phase since both are quick, confirmed, and unrelated to each other in cause.

- **35a. Standards list view toggle.** `frontend/src/modules/compliance/StandardListPage.tsx` renders a single fixed `DirectoryTable` (list-only, lines 151-163) and does not import `ViewToggle`/`useViewMode` at all — unlike `ProjectListPage.tsx` (`useViewMode("projects")`, `<ViewToggle .../>` at line 478), which already established this exact pattern. **No new documentation is needed for this one** — `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: view toggle (tile vs. list)" (line 261) already states the generic rule ("Any list page presenting more than a handful of items... should use this component rather than picking one fixed layout") and is already in the pattern table (line 342); `StandardListPage.tsx` is simply a page that hasn't been brought into line with an already-stated rule, the same failure mode `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s own opening paragraph exists to stop. Fix: add `useViewMode("standards")` + `<ViewToggle .../>`, plus a tiles-mode render, following `ProjectListPage.tsx`'s own implementation directly.
- **35b. Entity quick-switch chevron has a boxed-button chrome.** Confirmed at `frontend/src/components/EntitySwitcher.tsx:107-117`: the chevron trigger uses the generic `.btn` class (`theme.css:106-120` — `1px solid var(--color-border)` border, `border-radius: 6px`, own background), giving the bare chevron a separate bordered-chip appearance next to the entity name rather than reading as part of it. **This is the same defect class Phase 29d already fixed once, but only for a different chevron** (`StandardNavSection.tsx`'s disclosure toggle) — that fix followed `CollapsibleSection.tsx`'s borderless `role="button"` row precedent (`CollapsibleSection.tsx:49-67`: no `.btn`, no border, shared hover/active background spans the whole row) and never touched `EntitySwitcher.tsx`, so the shared Phase 28 component — used on Org Overview, Projects, Standards, and Org management — still has it everywhere. Fix `EntitySwitcher.tsx` itself, at the source, the same way: a borderless icon-affordance for the chevron button, sharing a hover/active surface with the adjacent entity name, rather than a fourth one-off restyle of a chevron already fixed once elsewhere. Fixing the shared component fixes all four (or more) consuming pages in one change, unlike Phase 29d's narrower single-consumer patch.
- **Docs:** add an explicit note to `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: entity quick-switch" section (written in Phase 28) cross-referencing the chevron-chrome rule already stated for the expandable-nav-rail pattern (Phase 29d's addendum), so a future chevron/disclosure-toggle anywhere in the app has one clearly-linked rule to find rather than two separately-discovered instances of the same fix.
- **Tests:** Storybook story update for `EntitySwitcher` covering the restyled chevron's default/hover/active states; visual check (or existing Playwright coverage, if any snapshot-tests this component) that the fix doesn't regress the popover's own open/close/keyboard behaviour, which is unrelated to the chrome change.

**Phase 35 notes:** Implemented as specced, with one downstream fix the spec didn't anticipate. **35a** (`StandardListPage.tsx`) added `useViewMode("standards")`/`ViewToggle` and a tiles-mode card render (name, reference, org when shown, issuing organisation, status) directly mirroring `ProjectListPage.tsx`'s own tile card; defaults to tiles (`useViewMode`'s own default), same as `ProjectListPage`/`FavouritesPage`. **This changed the `/standards` page's default rendering from always-`DirectoryTable` to tiles-first**, which broke three existing Playwright specs that assumed the old fixed list-only shape:
- `compliance-standards-management.spec.ts` and `project-compliance-view.spec.ts` each clicked `getByRole("link", { name: ORG_NAMES.alpha })` to reach `/standards/settings/:orgId` — a plain substring match that, once every standard also renders as a tile card whose accessible name contains the org name too, resolved to many elements instead of one. Fixed with `exact: true`, which only the "Compliance settings" link's own accessible name (just the org name, nothing else) satisfies.
- `standard-applicability-defaults.spec.ts` clicked `getByRole("button", { name: reference })` to open a standard from the list — correct against the old `DirectoryTable`-only render (whose `onRowClick` rows are real `<button>`s), but a tile-mode card is a `<Link>` (role `link`), not a button. Fixed by switching those two lookups to `getByRole("link", { name: new RegExp(reference) })`.

All three fixes are locator-only, in the test files themselves, not the shipped feature — the tile view rendering standards as clickable cards is the intended Phase 35a behaviour; the existing specs' locators simply encoded the pre-35a fixed-list assumption. See this plan's own "Modular Feature System Boundary"-adjacent lesson from Phase 27a/35b (below): a page-level UI convention change (adding a view toggle whose default flips the previously-only rendering) needs the same "check every existing call site of the old shape" sweep a shared-component fix does, not just the target page's own tests. A full run of `tests/modules/compliance/` (15 specs) plus `entity-quick-switch.spec.ts` confirmed no other collisions, after rebuilding and recreating the `frontend` container per this repo's standing Docker note (Compose doesn't bind-mount source).

**35b** (`EntitySwitcher.tsx`) added a new `.entity-switcher-trigger` class in `theme.css` (borderless/transparent at rest, background on hover/focus) in place of `.btn`, per the spec. The "hover/active states" Storybook coverage the spec's own "Tests" bullet asked for was narrowed to a default-state/class assertion only (`ChevronChromeIsBorderless`) — `getComputedStyle` after `userEvent.hover()` does not reliably observe a CSS-only `:hover`/`:focus-visible` pseudo-class change in this codebase's Vitest browser-mode test runner (confirmed directly: the assertion failed even though the rule is correct and visibly works in a real browser), and no other story in this codebase snapshot-tests a CSS-only hover state either — this isn't a gap specific to this component. The class/border assertion is still a real regression guard for the actual chrome defect (no more `.btn` border/background chip). All 6 pre-existing `EntitySwitcher` stories plus the new one pass; `entity-quick-switch.spec.ts` and `standard-version-nav.spec.ts`'s own chevron-switch test both confirm the popover's open/close/keyboard behaviour is unaffected.

### Phase 36 — Org Compliance Dashboard Layout Rework

**Satisfies:** the report that the Org Compliance Dashboard's widgets vary significantly in height and look bad, plus a separate, related concern that it may not need to list projects directly at all. **Decided by: User** that the current layout is broken; **Decided by: Agent** on the specific redesign below — the user explicitly flagged their own suggestion as "only a first pass thought," so this phase's shape is this planning session's judgment call, not a settled instruction, and should be revisited if it doesn't hold up once built.

- **Root cause, confirmed directly, two distinct problems:**
  - The top `.grid.grid-metrics` of nine `StatCard`s (`OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx` lines 179-198) is the *correct* convention per `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s Phase 27b rule (grid-of-`StatCard`s remains right for grouped stat blocks under a `ResourceMenu` group) — but several of those cards render a variable-length `<ProjectList>` as `children` (e.g. "Non-compliant projects," "Projects with outstanding actions," "Projects with expired evidence"), so card content height varies row to row even though CSS Grid's default `stretch` equalizes heights *within* one row — a long list in one card still makes that whole row taller than its neighbours.
  - The second block of three "detail" cards below it (lines 202-241 — "Standards with the most outstanding issues," "Recently changed compliance assessments," "Upcoming compliance deadlines/reviews") uses flexbox (`className="row"`) with `alignItems: "flex-start"` — explicitly opting out of equal-height stretching — so these three cards' bottom edges never line up when their list lengths differ.
- **Fix, two parts:**
  - **Stop embedding project lists inside headline `StatCard`s.** Convert every list-bearing `StatCard` in the top grid back to a plain number (matching the other, already-plain `StatCard`s in that same grid), and make the card itself a link (mirroring the already-established "stat tile → click navigates to a filtered view" idiom named in `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s Phase 23 note, and `MetricTile`'s own clickable-card precedent) to a proper filtered project list — e.g. `ProjectListPage.tsx` or an equivalent compliance-scoped list, filtered by the same condition the card counts (non-compliant, outstanding actions, expired evidence). This fixes the height-variance root cause directly (every top-grid card is now a plain, equal-content number again) and separately answers the "I don't think we need to list the projects [here]" concern — the projects are still reachable, just via a real filtered list page with proper `DirectoryTable`/`FilterPanel` affordances (pagination, sorting, further filtering) instead of an unbounded list awkwardly embedded in a stat tile.
  - **Fix the second block's height stretching.** Either move these three "detail" cards into a second `.grid.grid-metrics`-family grid (consistent with the fix above and with Phase 27b's stated convention) or, if their content genuinely isn't stat-tile-shaped, keep flexbox but remove `alignItems: "flex-start"` (defaulting to `stretch`) and additionally bound each card's own list length (e.g. "show top 5, link to see all," mirroring `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s own expand-per-group idiom) so a long list in one card doesn't force a tall row even after heights are allowed to stretch equally.
  - **On "a way to view all projects for a compliance manager":** rather than adding a flat projects widget to this Dashboard tab, extend the sibling **Standards tab** (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`) — which already groups by standard with an expandable per-standard project list (lines 123-169) — with a filter that a Compliance Manager can use to pivot the same data by project instead of by standard (or add a lightweight "all projects" filter toggle to whatever list a click-through from the fixed top-grid cards, above, lands on). This directly follows the user's own suggested direction rather than inventing a fourth widget shape; treat the exact mechanism as an implementation-time judgment call within that constraint, since the user flagged this specific part as a first-pass idea rather than a firm spec.
- **Docs:** record the "top-grid `StatCard`s must stay plain numbers, drill-down lists live on a real filtered list page, not embedded in the tile" rule in `docs/ux-style-guide.md`, as a direct addendum to the existing Phase 27b/25c guidance on when grid-of-`StatCard` applies.
- **Tests:** Storybook stories for the reworked Dashboard tab's top grid (plain cards only) and the fixed-height detail-card row; Playwright coverage that clicking a headline card (e.g. "Non-compliant projects") navigates to a correctly pre-filtered project list; visual/manual check that card heights are now consistent at common viewport widths in both blocks.

#### Phase 36 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

The spec's own open implementation-time judgment calls were resolved as follows: (1) "a proper filtered project list" for the standards-status-derived tiles (Non-compliant projects, Projects subject to compliance) is `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx` itself, extended with a "Group by" project pivot, rather than `ProjectListPage.tsx` or a new page — that panel already carries `DirectoryTable`-equivalent expand/filter affordances over exactly this data, and a plain `ProjectListPage.tsx` has no compliance-state concept to filter by at all. (2) The remaining tiles (outstanding actions, expired/expiring evidence, overdue reviews, pending approval) link into `OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel.tsx` instead — that data (per-action/per-evidence/per-review rows, not per-project-standard-assignment rows) doesn't fit the Standards panel's row shape, and the Outstanding panel already lists exactly these five categories tagged by project; it gained a Category + Evidence-status filter pair rather than being left unfiltered. (3) The "pivot by project" mechanism itself is the literal option the spec named first (extend the Standards tab) rather than the parenthetical alternative (an "all projects" toggle on the outstanding-item destination) — implemented as a `groupBy` state generalising the existing per-standard grouping code (`groupByStandard`→`groupRows`), not a second parallel table. None of this changes anything Phase 34/35 already shipped.

## Feature-list gap review (2026-09-13)

Requested directly: "please review what we have currently built vs the initial compliance feature list." The original feature list is `docs/Compliance_Module_Requirements.md` (33 numbered sections, §1-§33). This plan's own "Verification" section (below) already asserts every section maps to at least one shipped phase, across Phases 0-30 — that claim is accurate at the level of "a phase touched this section," but **"shipped" is not the same as "correct" or "complete," and this round is direct evidence of the gap between those two claims**:

- **§10 (Compliance Status) and §20 (Overall Project Compliance Status)** were marked shipped by Phase 7/14, but Phase 31 (above) is a correctness bug in exactly this area — an unassessed standard reads as compliant. A section being "implemented" clearly did not mean its status semantics were reviewed for the fail-safe direction a compliance product needs (unknown must never look the same as verified-good) until this round's live use surfaced it.
- **§13 (Evidence)** was marked shipped by Phase 8, and the underlying upload mechanism is genuinely solid (`EvidencePanel.tsx` correctly reuses `FileAttachmentList`/`ResourcePickerModal`, per that section's own instruction to reuse existing attachment mechanisms) — but the *assessment workflow* (§5/§7/§10's actual point of use) only exposes the "link" half of that mechanism, not upload. The gap wasn't a missing capability, it was the capability not being surfaced at the one point in the workflow a user actually needs it.
- **§7/§9 assignment and assessment UX** (Project Compliance assignment/assessment, Phase 7/13) never received the same searchable-picker treatment Phase 30 gave the Standards member picker for the identical underlying complaint — meaning a defect class this plan already diagnosed and fixed once (Phase 30) was left unfixed in a second, equally-affected location the whole time. This is the same "fix the shared root cause everywhere, not just where it was noticed" lesson Phase 27a and Phase 35b both restate below — worth treating as a standing risk for any future single-location fix in this module: check every other call site of the same underlying pattern before calling a defect closed.
- **Traceability to core Requirements (Phase 34)** is not a documented gap at all — `Compliance_Module_Requirements.md`'s §19 (Cross-Standard Mapping) only ever specifies compliance-requirement-to-compliance-requirement mapping, and no section asks for a link between a compliance requirement and a core product `Requirement`. This is a genuinely new capability request beyond the original spec, not a missed implementation of an existing one — flagged here so a future reader doesn't go looking for the "original requirement" this gap supposedly violates.
- **UI polish items (Phases 32, 35, 36)** are all in areas the original requirements document is silent on (it specifies data/workflow requirements, not picker widget shape or dashboard card layout) — these are `docs/ux-style-guide.md` conformance gaps, not `Compliance_Module_Requirements.md` gaps. Notably, two of the three (32, 35b) are cases where this plan already fixed the identical defect class once elsewhere in the same module and simply didn't check for/apply it everywhere the pattern recurs — see the standing risk noted above.

**Net assessment:** the module is feature-complete against `Compliance_Module_Requirements.md`'s §1-§33 in the sense that every workflow it specifies exists end-to-end (create → publish → assign → assess → approve → review, per §24's lifecycle) — but "exists" has repeatedly meant "exists in a form that a live-usage pass finds rough edges in," across three prior human-review rounds (Phases 17, 20-26, 27-30) and now a fourth. There is no reason to expect this pattern stops here; a fifth round after Phases 31-36 land should be expected as normal for a module this size, not treated as a sign something is being done wrong.

## Compliance Module Phases 37-41 — Fifth Human-Review Round

**Added 2026-09-13.** This round came from the user's direct, live-usage review of five surfaces immediately after Phase 36 shipped: Entity quick-switch coverage, the Org Compliance Dashboard, the Compliance-by-standard panel, both Outstanding-items panels (org and project level), and evidence creation. As with the third and fourth rounds, each phase below cites the exact current code and tags each non-obvious call as **Decided by: User** or **Decided by: Agent**. This confirms the "Net assessment" note directly above: a fifth round, as expected.

### Phase 37 — Entity Quick-Switch Applied to Org Settings

**Satisfies:** "Can the Entity-quick-switch please be applied to org settings." **Decided by: User.**

- **Confirmed gap, already flagged as deferred.** Phase 28's own completion notes recorded that `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s two *degraded-state* headers (`OrgAdminPage.tsx:1623`, `1669` — a server admin hitting a disabled org, or one they aren't a member of) got the chevron, but the page's normal admin header did not, because it renders its title through `ResourceMenu`'s own `title` prop (`ResourceMenu.tsx:91`'s `<h1>`, wired at `OrgAdminPage.tsx:1979`) rather than a bespoke `<h1>` of its own — there was no attachment point without either duplicating `ResourceMenu`'s title rendering or plumbing a new prop through it for one caller. Those notes explicitly said "revisit only if a future review specifically asks for it" — this is that ask.
- **`ProjectAdminPage.tsx` needs no change.** Checked directly: it already renders its own `<h1>` + `EntitySwitcher` above `ResourceMenu` (`ProjectAdminPage.tsx:1142-1146`, confirmed by Phase 28's own spec line for this page) and calls `ResourceMenu` itself with no `title` prop (`ProjectAdminPage.tsx:1172`) — the normal-admin-header gap is specific to `OrgAdminPage.tsx`, not a `ResourceMenu` consumer pattern in general.
- **Fix.** Add a small, generic optional slot to `ResourceMenu` itself — e.g. `titleAdornment?: ReactNode`, rendered immediately after the `<h1>` at `ResourceMenu.tsx:91` (a flex row wrapping the heading and the adornment) — rather than a component-specific prop, so any future `ResourceMenu` consumer with the same "chevron next to my title" need can reuse it without a second plumbing change. Wire it at `OrgAdminPage.tsx:1979`'s `<ResourceMenu title={org.name} .../>` call: `titleAdornment={orgId && <EntitySwitcher label="Switch organisation" currentId={orgId} loadOptions={() => loadOrgSwitcherOptions("admin")} />}` — the identical loader (`utils/entitySwitcherLoaders.ts::loadOrgSwitcherOptions`, target `"admin"`) already used verbatim at the two degraded-header call sites, so no new loader or endpoint is needed.
- **Docs:** update `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: entity quick-switch" section (Phase 28) to note the new `ResourceMenu.titleAdornment` slot as the attachment point for a `ResourceMenu`-driven header, alongside the existing bespoke-`<h1>` case it already documents.
- **Tests:** Storybook story for `ResourceMenu` with `titleAdornment` set (and the existing no-adornment stories confirming nothing changes when it's omitted); extend `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s normal-admin-header story to include the mocked `?mine=true` org list `loadOrgSwitcherOptions("admin")` already needs; Playwright coverage that opening the chevron on Org Admin's normal header and picking a sibling org lands on that org's own Admin page (mirroring `entity-quick-switch.spec.ts`'s existing shape for the other four locations).

#### Phase 37 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

Implemented as specced, no deviations. `ResourceMenu.tsx` gained `titleAdornment?: ReactNode`, rendered in a new flex row alongside its `<h1>`; `OrgAdminPage.tsx`'s normal-header call site wires it with the exact same `EntitySwitcher`/`loadOrgSwitcherOptions("admin")` pairing already used verbatim at its two degraded-header call sites — no new loader or endpoint needed, confirming the spec's own prediction. `OrgAdminPage.stories.tsx`'s shared `mockOrgAdminApis` helper gained an `orgs` override (defaulting to `[]`) for `GET /api/v1/orgs?mine=true`, since that endpoint is now called on every render of this page's normal header rather than only by a story that opts in to exercising it.

Verified live end-to-end: `tsc -b`/`eslint` clean; full `vitest run --project=storybook` (111 files, 886/886) confirmed the `ResourceMenu` markup change regressed nothing at any other consumer (`OrgOverviewPage.tsx`, `ProjectAdminPage.tsx`, `PreferencesPage.tsx`, `ServerManagementPage.tsx`, `ComplianceSettingsPage.tsx`).
