Rebuilt and recreated the `tests/container` frontend image, then ran live: `entity-quick-switch.spec.ts` (2/2, including the new Org Settings test) plus a wider sweep of six Org Admin-adjacent specs (11/11) — no regressions. Full record in `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 37" entry.

### Phase 38 — Org Compliance Dashboard: Top-Justify Detail Cards + Full-Width "Recently Changed" Row

**Satisfies:** "In the org level compliance dashboard, the panels should be top justified, and I think `Recently changed compliance assessments` should be on its own row and take up full width." **Decided by: User.**

- **Root cause, confirmed directly.** The second block of three detail cards (`OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx:234-287`, "Standards with the most outstanding issues" / "Recently changed compliance assessments" / "Upcoming compliance deadlines/reviews") wraps them in `<div className="row" style={{ gap: "1rem", flexWrap: "wrap" }}>` with no `alignItems` override. Phase 36's own notes claimed removing the prior `alignItems: "flex-start"` would leave the row "defaulting to stretch" — but `.row` (`styles/theme.css:385-390`) itself sets `align-items: center`, not the browser's own flexbox default of `stretch`. So today, when the three cards have different content heights, they render vertically **centred** relative to one another rather than top-aligned — Phase 36 changed the mechanism but didn't achieve either of the two outcomes it discussed (true `stretch` or explicit `flex-start`); this is what reads as "not top justified."
- **Fix, two parts, both confined to `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx:234-287`:**
  - Give this specific row an explicit `alignItems: "flex-start"` (do not rely on `.row`'s shared default, which centres, or on plain CSS defaulting, which stretches — this row's own content is genuinely variable-length, so top-aligned-but-not-stretched is the correct rendering, consistent with `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s Phase 27b guidance that a grid/row of variable-content cards should have consistent tops even when heights differ).
  - Pull "Recently changed compliance assessments" out of the three-card row into its own full-width block below it — either a second `<div className="row">` containing only that one card with no `flex` sizing constraint, or simply a plain `<div className="card stack">` sibling outside any `.row` wrapper. The remaining two cards ("Standards with the most outstanding issues," "Upcoming compliance deadlines/reviews") stay paired in the original row.
- **Docs:** add a short addendum to `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s existing Phase 27b/36 guidance on stat/detail card rows, recording that `.row`'s own `align-items: center` default is a trap for any row of cards with variable content length — an explicit `alignItems` must always be set on such a row, never left to the shared class's default.
- **Tests:** Storybook story for the Dashboard's detail-card block with three cards of visibly different content lengths, asserting top alignment (not centred) and the "Recently changed" card spanning full width on its own row; visual/manual check at common viewport widths.

#### Phase 38 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

Implemented as specced, no deviations. `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx`'s detail-card row now sets `alignItems: "flex-start"` explicitly rather than relying on `.row`'s own `align-items: center` default; "Recently changed compliance assessments" moved out of that row into its own full-width `.card` sibling below it. `docs/ux-style-guide.md` gained a correction addendum directly after its Phase 36 paragraph, since that paragraph's advice to "let rows stretch (drop `alignItems: 'flex-start'`)" turns out to be wrong for `.row` specifically (it centres, not stretches) — future readers of that guidance will now see the correction immediately below it rather than needing to already know about this phase.

Verified live end-to-end: `tsc -b`/`eslint` clean; full `vitest run --project=storybook` (111 files, 887/887, one pre-existing unrelated `ProjectOverviewPage.stories.tsx` unhandled-rejection warning from its own intentional "Load Error" story) confirmed no regressions. Rebuilt and recreated the `tests/container` frontend image, then checked live via Playwright MCP against the seeded demo org (Solstice Robotics, `demo.admin@example.com`/`DemoDemo123!`): the two remaining detail cards render top-aligned despite differing content heights, and "Recently changed compliance assessments" renders as its own full-width row. No Playwright spec referenced this dashboard's detail cards by name, so none needed updating.

**Incidental fix, same session:** that live screenshot also surfaced that the top nine-card grid mixed `StatCard` (label-above-value, left-aligned) and `MetricTile` (value-above-label, centred) side by side — visually inconsistent even though each component was correct in isolation. Fixed `StatCard` (`components/StatCard.tsx`) to match `MetricTile`'s layout exactly; see `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s new "components rendered interchangeably in the same grid" principle. Full record in `docs/decisions.md`'s "Compliance module plan, Phase 38" entry.

### Phase 39 — Compliance-by-Standard Panel: Borderless Expander, Tree Indentation, Standard → Version → Project Grouping

**Satisfies:** three related complaints about `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s default (group-by-standard) view: "the standards expanders a) shouldn't have a bounding box, b) seem to be gradually moving further right, c) the project lists should indent a little to actually look like a tree. Maybe also do standard -> version -> project." **Decided by: User** on all three; **Decided by: Agent** on the exact mechanism for (b) and the nesting shape for the standard→version→project ask, both flagged below as needing live confirmation/implementer judgment.

- **39a. Bounding box, confirmed directly.** The expand/collapse control at `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:180-188` uses the shared `.btn` class (`theme.css:106-120` — its own border/border-radius/background), the exact same defect class this plan has already fixed twice for a disclosure toggle in a different component: Phase 29d (`StandardNavSection.tsx`'s Versions-group toggle, following `CollapsibleSection.tsx`'s borderless `role="button"` row precedent) and Phase 35b (`EntitySwitcher.tsx`'s chevron). **Fix by following the same precedent a third time**, at the source in `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`: a borderless disclosure row (no `.btn`) whose hover/active background spans the whole row (label + chevron together), consistent with `CollapsibleSection.tsx:52-67`.
- **39b. "Gradually moving further right."** Not conclusively isolated by static code reading: the button sits in a plain `<td>` with no per-row margin/padding that accumulates across sibling `<tr>`s in the current flat `.map` over `groups` (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:172-195`), so there is no obvious cumulative-drift mechanism in the code as written. The most likely explanation is that this is a symptom of 39a rather than a separate defect: the button's text is a literal `▾`/`▸` glyph (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:187`) whose rendered advance width can vary by font/glyph in a way a fixed-size icon component would not, and `.btn`'s own padding compounds with that — replacing both (39a's borderless-row fix, plus swapping the `▾`/`▸` text glyphs for a fixed-size Lucide icon, e.g. `ChevronDown`/`ChevronRight`, matching `StandardNavSection.tsx`'s own Phase 29d icon choice) may resolve it as a side effect. **The implementing session should reproduce this live in the browser before treating it as fixed** — per this repo's standing rule to confirm root causes directly rather than assume a fix without watching the actual rendering, note explicitly in this phase's completion notes whether it reproduced and whether 39a's fix actually resolved it, and open it as a distinct follow-up if not.
- **39c. Tree indentation.** The expanded project list (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:196-221`) renders a flush-left `<ul>` with no indentation relative to its parent standard row, so it doesn't read as a child of that row. Fix by reusing `RequirementTree.tsx`'s own established nested-list convention (`RequirementTree.tsx:365`: `marginLeft: "1.75rem", borderLeft: "2px solid var(--color-border)", paddingLeft: "0.75rem"`) rather than inventing new spacing values — apply the same margin/border/padding to this `<ul>` (or its wrapping `<td>` content) so each project row visually nests under its standard.
- **39d. Standard → Version → Project grouping.** Today the default (`groupBy === "standard"`) view's expanded row lists every project assignment for that standard flatly (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:200-217`), regardless of which version of the standard each project is on — a user has to read every row's own `{version_label}` suffix to work out which versions are actually in use. Add a second nesting level inside the expanded row: group `group.rows` by `standard_version_id` (client-side, the same `groupRows`-style aggregation this file already uses for its top-level grouping), render each version as its own labelled sub-heading (e.g. "v2.1"), and list that version's projects beneath it using 39c's tree-indent convention one level deeper (so Standard is the outer indent level, Version the middle, Project the innermost). This is additive to the existing "Group by: Standard/Project" pivot, not a third top-level `groupBy` option — it only changes how the already-expanded standard-mode row itself renders.
- **Docs:** add this phase's borderless-disclosure fix as a third cross-reference alongside Phase 29d/35b in `docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s chevron-chrome rule (per Phase 35b's own note that a future chevron/disclosure toggle anywhere in the app should find one clearly-linked rule, not a third separately-discovered instance); note the tree-indent convention reuse.
- **Tests:** Storybook stories for the restyled expander (borderless, hover/active spans the row) and the new standard→version→project nested render (a standard with two versions each carrying different projects); Playwright coverage that expanding a standard shows its versions and their projects correctly nested and that clicking through to a project still works.

#### Phase 39 notes

- **39a/39b reproduced and fixed together, as predicted.** Live browser reproduction (per the spec's own instruction) confirmed 39b was a symptom of 39a, not a separate defect: with the `▾`/`▸` text glyphs replaced by fixed-size `ChevronDown`/`ChevronRight` and the `.btn` chip replaced by a new borderless `.disclosure-toggle` class (`theme.css`, mirroring `.th-sort`'s "zero the cell's own padding, let the button re-add it" convention but with a hover background instead of a colour change, since the whole row is the click target here), the drift did not reproduce. `.disclosure-toggle` is written generically, not scoped to this one panel, for the next borderless-in-a-table-cell disclosure control.
- **39d shipped with one addition beyond the written spec, per a live follow-up from the user during implementation:** each version sub-group is itself collapsible when a standard has more than one version — but when it has exactly one, that sub-group renders pre-expanded with a plain `<h4>` heading and no toggle at all, since a lone version behind an already-explicit "expand this standard" click has nothing left to disambiguate. **Decided by: User.**
- **Found and fixed an unrelated, pre-existing routing bug while testing 39d's live-reproduction requirement**, per this repo's standing fix-don't-defer rule (`CLAUDE.md`). `useProjectEnabledModules.ts`'s Phase 13 fix for `App.tsx`'s wildcard-route redirect race (documented in that hook's own docstring) only covered a *fresh page load* landing directly on a project-scoped URL — it missed the same race one render earlier: a client-side navigation from a project-less page (e.g. `/org-overview`) straight into a project's own module route, for the first time in that page's lifetime, always lost the race deterministically (not a flaky timing issue — confirmed via a 2-second artificial delay before the click, which made no difference). `loaded`/`modules` briefly read stale values left over from the previous ("no project") state on that first render, `App.tsx` saw an apparently-already-loaded project with zero enabled modules, and bounced straight back to `/projects` before the real fetch ever ran. This is exactly the click this phase's own new Playwright test (a standard's expanded row -> a project link, reached via `/org-overview`) performs, which is how it surfaced — the pre-existing `org-compliance-view.spec.ts` test performs the identical click and should have caught this long ago, so this was evidently an existing, real gap in production behaviour, not something Phase 39's UI changes introduced (verified by reproducing it against an already-published, pre-existing standard/project fixture unrelated to this session's own work). Fixed in `useProjectEnabledModules.ts` by keying its returned state to the `projectId` it was actually resolved for and resetting synchronously during render when `projectId` changes (React's documented "adjusting state when a prop changes" pattern) instead of only via `useEffect`, which runs one render too late for this specific transition. See that file's own updated docstring for the full mechanism. Full compliance Playwright suite plus a sample of core navigation specs (`entity-quick-switch`, `org-overview`, `module-contributed-roles`, `org-admin-modules`) re-run clean after the fix.

### Phase 40 — Outstanding Compliance Items: Side Filter Panel + Project/Standard/Version/Sub-Section Filters (Org and Project Level)

**Satisfies:** "The outstanding compliance items dash, should have better filtering, and have this filter on the right for computer screens. Eg filters should include project, standard, standard version, and if a standard is selected, which sub sections. Same goes for the outstanding section in the project compliance page." **Decided by: User.**

- **40a. Filter placement (org level), confirmed directly, a quick fix.** `OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel.tsx` passes `layout="top"` to its `FilterPanel` (line ~68). Per `FilterPanel.tsx`'s own documented rule (`FilterPanel.tsx:36-50`), `"top"` exists specifically "for wide, many-column directories... where a 240px sidebar would otherwise eat into the table's available width" — this panel is a stack of card sections, not a wide directory table, so `"top"` was the wrong choice against the style guide's own stated criterion, and its sibling `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx` already uses the correct default (`layout="side"`, wrapped in a `side-grid`, `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:154-231`). Fix: drop the `layout="top"` override, wrap the panel's card sections in a `<div className="side-grid">` alongside the `FilterPanel` (mirroring `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s own structure exactly), so filters render as the right-hand sidebar on desktop and collapse per `FilterPanel`'s own existing mobile behaviour.
- **40b. Missing filter dimensions (org level).** Today `OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel.tsx` only filters by `category` and `evidence validity` (lines ~50-90) — no project, standard, or standard-version filter exists, unlike its sibling `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx` which already has all three. Add `Project`, `Standard`, and `Standard version` `FilterField`s, following `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx:249-279`'s own pattern directly (options derived client-side from whatever's already been fetched, version options narrowed by the selected standard the same way).
- **40c. Missing `standard_id`/`standard_version_id` fields, confirmed directly.** The four listing types these panels render (`NonCompliantRequirement`, `PendingApproval`, `OutstandingRequiredAction` — `types.ts:486-513`/`597-612` — and their `Org*` subclasses) carry only display strings (`standard_reference`, `standard_name`, `version_label`), not `standard_id`/`standard_version_id`; `ComplianceReview` (`types.ts:563-578`) carries `standard_id` but no reference/label at all. Filtering by standard/version needs stable identifiers, not string matching (which happens to work today only because standard references are unique per org in practice, but is the wrong thing to filter on when an id already exists one call away). **Fix, backend:** add `standard_id`/`standard_version_id` to each of these four `Out` schemas (and, symmetrically, `standard_reference`/`standard_name`/`version_label` to the review schema, which is currently missing them entirely) in `backend/app/modules/compliance/schemas.py` and their construction sites in `service.py`/`project_router.py` — this is the same "resolve everything server-side, denormalize onto the response row" convention this plan's own Phase 34 notes cite for `RequirementLinkOut`, applied here to fill a real, pre-existing gap rather than a new pattern. Frontend filters (40b, and the project-level equivalent in 40e) should filter on these ids once added, not on the display strings.
- **40d. Sub-section filter, confirmed there is no backend concept to reuse.** "Sub section" has no dedicated field anywhere in the data model — a standard's requirement hierarchy is purely `parent_requirement_id`-based (`ComplianceRequirement`, `types.ts:251-270`; `RequirementTree.tsx`'s own depth-first render), so a requirement's "section" is really its top-level ancestor (the `parent_requirement_id === null` requirement it descends from, e.g. "Clause 4" of ISO 27001). Implement client-side, once a standard is selected: fetch that standard's requirement tree (`complianceApi.listRequirements`, already used elsewhere, e.g. `RequirementTree.tsx`/`api.ts::buildRequirementTree`), derive the set of top-level requirements as the sub-section filter's options, and for each outstanding row resolve its `requirement_id` up the `parent_requirement_id` chain to find which top-level ancestor it belongs to (a small new helper, e.g. `findTopLevelAncestor(tree, requirementId)`, reusable by both the org and project panels per 40e). No backend change needed for this part — purely client-side aggregation over an already-fetchable flat listing, the same precedent `buildRequirementTree`/`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel`'s own grouping already established. This filter should only appear/be enabled once a single standard is selected (its options are meaningless across multiple standards at once).
- **40e. Project-level parity.** `OutstandingPanel.tsx` (the Project Compliance page's own "Outstanding" tab, `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx:220-223`) has **no filtering at all today** — confirmed directly, it imports no `FilterPanel`. Since it's already scoped to one project, a "Project" filter doesn't apply here, but Standard, Standard version, and (once a standard is selected) Sub-section all do, for the same reason they help at org scope: a project with several standards assigned currently shows every non-compliant requirement/pending approval/outstanding action/review from all of them in one flat set of four sections. Add the same `FilterPanel` (default `layout="side"`, wrapped in a `side-grid`) with Standard/Standard version/Sub-section fields, reusing 40c's new ids and 40d's helper rather than a second implementation.
- **Docs:** none required for placement (40a already corrects to an existing, documented rule); no new pattern for 40b/40e (reuses `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s existing filter-field conventions). Document the new sub-section filter as a brief addendum wherever `docs/ux-style-guide.md` already covers this module's filter panels, since it's a genuinely new filter shape (derived-from-tree, standard-scoped) other pages don't have yet.
- **Tests:** backend — the four `Out` schemas' new id fields are present and correct on an existing fixture (pin against regression); frontend — Storybook stories for both panels with the new filter fields (standard selected narrows version options; sub-section filter appears only once a standard is selected and correctly narrows rows); Playwright coverage that filtering by standard/version/sub-section on both the org-level and project-level Outstanding views narrows the visible rows correctly, and that the org-level panel's filter now renders as a right-hand sidebar, not a top bar.

#### Phase 40 notes

- **Scope decision beyond the written spec, Decided by: Agent.** The plan names four listing types for 40c (`NonCompliantRequirement`/`PendingApproval`/`OutstandingRequiredAction` plus `ComplianceReview`), but `Evidence` (the fifth Outstanding section, `OrgExpiringEvidence`/`ComplianceEvidence`) has no standard/version field anywhere in the data model at all — an evidence row can be linked to requirements across more than one standard, so it can't honestly carry a single owning standard. Evidence stays governed by Project + its own pre-existing Evidence-status filter only, unaffected by the new Standard/Standard version/Sub-section filters. Sub-section similarly only applies to the three per-requirement sections (Non-compliant/Pending/Actions) — Reviews are scoped to a standard/version as a whole, not to one specific requirement, so there is no per-review "section" to resolve.
- **`ComplianceReviewOut.standard_id` changed meaning, Decided by: Agent.** Before this phase it echoed the underlying `ComplianceReview.standard_id` model column verbatim — set only for a *standard-level* review, always `null` for a *project-level* one (`project_compliance_id` set instead). `build_review_out` now resolves the owning standard through either path (directly for a standard-level review, through the assignment's own standard version for a project-level one), so `standard_id`/`standard_reference`/`standard_name` are populated for both kinds and a review can be filtered by standard regardless of which way it's scoped — the whole point of this phase. `standard_version_id`/`version_label` stay `null` for a standard-level review, since it isn't tied to one specific version. Confirmed no existing consumer (frontend or backend) read the old null-for-project-level semantics as a signal — `ReviewsPanel.tsx` is already project-scoped and never branched on it, and the one backend test asserting `standard_id` predates this change and is against a standard-level review, so it still passes unchanged.
- **Sub-section fetch strategy.** Rather than deriving the standard version(s) to fetch from whatever's already in the currently-filtered rows, both panels fetch *every* version of the selected standard (`listStandardVersions` + `listRequirements` per version) as soon as a standard is picked, then derive sub-section options and resolve ancestors from that combined map — so switching the Standard version filter afterward never needs a second fetch, and the sub-section list is always complete even if the current Project/Category filters happen to be hiding every row for one of that standard's versions.
- **Test-authoring gotcha found while writing this phase's own new Playwright coverage, not an app bug — recorded here so the next spec touching a `FilterField` select doesn't rediscover it the hard way.** `page.getByLabel(fieldLabel, { exact: true })` hangs to a full timeout against any `FilterField`-wrapped `<select>` that has options at all — Playwright's `internal:label=` selector engine matches an exact query against the wrapping label's raw `textContent`, which includes every `<option>`'s own text for a label that wraps rather than points at its control (`for`/`id`). The real browser accessible-name computation excludes this correctly (this same markup works fine via Testing Library's `getByLabelText` in Storybook), so this is purely a Playwright-engine quirk. Fixed at the test layer only: `tests/playwright/tests/modules/compliance/helpers.ts::selectFilterOption(page, fieldLabel, optionLabel)` locates the label's own `<span>` by exact text and walks to the sibling `<select>` instead of trusting label association — use it for any future Playwright coverage of a `FilterField` control.

### Phase 41 — Inline File Attachment at Evidence Creation

**Satisfies:** "When evidence is created, I should be able to attach files on creation, not wait for creation and then add the file." **Decided by: User.**

- **Confirmed directly.** `EvidenceFormModal` (`EvidencePanel.tsx:277-342`, also reused as-is by Phase 33's inline upload-from-assessment-panel flow) collects only metadata fields — there is no file input anywhere in its create form. File attachment only becomes possible after `onSave` succeeds and the panel opens the newly-created record's own `SidePanel` (`EvidencePanel.tsx:157-206`), which is where `FileAttachmentList`'s real upload control (`onUpload` → `complianceApi.uploadEvidenceAttachment`, needs an existing `evidence.id`) lives — a user creating evidence today must save first, then separately open it and attach files, exactly as reported.
- **Fix.** Add a lightweight local staging list to `EvidenceFormModal`'s create mode (not `FileAttachmentList` itself, which is driven by real, already-uploaded `FileAsset[]` and has no notion of a not-yet-uploaded `File` — a create-time form has no evidence id yet to upload against): reuse `FileUploadTrigger` (`components/FileUploadTrigger.tsx`, already imported by `FileAttachmentList.tsx` for the identical "pick a file" affordance) to let the user pick one or more files into local state (an array of `File` objects, each removable before save, mirroring `FileAttachmentList`'s own remove-button convention for visual consistency even though the underlying data isn't a `FileAsset` yet). On `onSave`, after `complianceApi.createEvidence` returns the new record, upload each staged file via the existing `complianceApi.uploadEvidenceAttachment(projectId, newEvidence.id, file)` sequentially, before closing the modal and reloading — so creation and attachment complete as one user action. Only add this to the create path; the edit path already has a real `SidePanel` with a working `FileAttachmentList` and needs no change.
- Since `EvidenceFormModal` is shared with Phase 33's inline assessment-panel flow, that flow gains this for free — a user creating evidence from the assessment panel can now attach its supporting file(s) in the same step they create and auto-link it, rather than a second detour into the newly-created evidence's own file section (which Phase 33's own flow currently opens for exactly this purpose, per that phase's notes — that follow-up modal step becomes unnecessary once files can be attached during creation itself, and can be dropped once this phase's `onSave` upload path exists, unless a reason emerges to keep it as an "also add another file" convenience after creation).
- **Docs:** none required — this composes already-existing, already-documented mechanisms (`FileUploadTrigger`, `uploadEvidenceAttachment`) rather than introducing a new pattern.
- **Tests:** backend — none needed beyond what Phase 33's own test already pins (same `createEvidence` → `uploadEvidenceAttachment` call sequence, just triggered from one form submission instead of two user actions); frontend — Storybook story for `EvidenceFormModal`'s create mode with one or more staged files attached and removed before save; Playwright e2e creating a piece of evidence with a file attached in the same create flow and confirming the file appears immediately in the newly-created evidence's own Files section, without a separate upload step.

**Phase 41 notes.** Implemented as specified, with one call the spec left open resolved: kept `RequirementAssessmentPanel.tsx`'s follow-up "Attach files to..." modal rather than dropping it, but only open it when `createAndLinkEvidence` receives zero staged files — one or more staged files upload immediately and the modal is skipped, so a user who already attached a file at creation isn't asked again, while the fallback still exists for a user who didn't stage one. `onSave`'s signature grew a `files: File[]` second parameter (both call sites' edit-mode `onSave`s simply ignore it, which TypeScript's function-parameter-count covariance allows without a cast). Extended `project-compliance-view.spec.ts`'s existing evidence-creation steps in place rather than adding a new spec file, since Phase 13's own test already walked both evidence-creation paths (top-level Evidence tab, Phase 33's inline upload) end to end and this phase's behaviour change is local to those same two steps.

## Compliance Module Phases 42-43 — Sixth Human-Review Round

**Added 2026-09-13.** This round came from the user's direct read of an actual generated compliance report and live use of the Compliance-by-standard and Outstanding-items panels. As with prior rounds, each phase below cites the exact current code and tags each non-obvious call as **Decided by: User** or **Decided by: Agent**.

### Phase 42 — Compliance Report Content: Narrative Requirement Cell, Title Always Shown

**Satisfies:** "Compliance Reports must have titles for requirements (it's very hard to understand context in these reports without the title). As such I would aim for a report style, where rather than acting like a CSV with a column for each information piece, join some together like `docs/requirements.md`'s file does (such as the title, reasoning and clarification are all in the same cell, just with new lines between them, and bold prefixes)." **Decided by: User** on both the defect (missing title) and the target shape (`docs/requirements.md`'s own table, cited by name); **Decided by: Agent** on folding in `description` as a fourth bold-prefixed section and on leaving the CSV export's column-per-field shape alone (see below).

- **Root cause, confirmed directly — two independent defects, both in `backend/app/modules/compliance/reports.py` (built Phase 15).**
  - **Missing title.** `_requirement_path()` (`reports.py:186-200`) builds a report row's requirement label as a breadcrumb of `current.reference or current.name` for *every* ancestor, including the leaf requirement itself. Whenever a requirement has a `reference` set — the normal case for any numbered standard clause — its own `name` (the actual requirement text/title, e.g. "Equipment shall meet IPX9 water ingress requirements") is dropped entirely, and the PDF's main table shows only "3.2.1" with nothing to say what 3.2.1 actually requires. This is exactly the "very hard to understand context... without the title" complaint. `generate_project_compliance_csv` (`reports.py:634-645`) happens to still carry `requirement_name` as its own column, so the CSV isn't hit by this specific bug — the PDF, the artefact most likely to be printed or handed to an external auditor, is.
  - **Column-per-field, not narrative.** The PDF's main table (`generate_project_compliance_pdf`, `reports.py:574-589`: `Standard / Requirement / Applicability / Status / Approval / Actions / Evidence / Justification/Notes`) and the CSV's 17-column header (`reports.py:634-638`) both lay out every field in its own column — a reasonable shape for a machine-oriented export, but not the "read this like a document" shape `docs/requirements.md`'s own table already uses for core requirements (one `Requirement` cell holding the requirement text, then `<br>**Reasoning:** ...<br>**Clarification:** ...`, all in the same cell) that the user wants the PDF to match.
- **Fix — `ComplianceReportRequirementRow` (`reports.py:207-229`) and both PDF generators:**
  - Add `requirement_description: str` (from `ComplianceRequirement.description`) and `requirement_clarification: str` (from `ComplianceRequirement.last_clarification_note`) to the dataclass, alongside the existing `requirement_name`; populate both in `collect_project_compliance_report`'s per-requirement loop (`reports.py:404-422`) from the same in-scope `requirement` object `justification`/`notes` already read from.
  - Add a small pure helper, e.g. `_narrative_requirement_cell(row: ComplianceReportRequirementRow) -> str`, that builds one multi-line cell: the requirement's ancestor breadcrumb *excluding the leaf* (so section context like "3 > 3.2" still shows without duplicating the leaf's own title) on its own line; then the leaf's `requirement_reference` + `requirement_name` as the lead line (this row's title, always present regardless of whether `reference` is set — the actual fix); then, each only if non-empty, on its own blank-line-separated section: `**Reasoning:** {reasoning}`, `**Description:** {description}`, `**Clarification:** {clarification}`, `**Justification:** {justification}`, `**Notes:** {notes}` — the same bold-prefix-plus-blank-line convention `docs/requirements.md` uses, rendered here as a ReportLab `Paragraph`'s `<br/>`/`<b>...</b>` markup (safe: `_safe()` still escapes every raw field value before interpolation; only the literal structural tags this helper itself adds are unescaped, never user-supplied text — see `reports.py`'s own module docstring for why that distinction matters here).
  - PDF: replace the "Requirement" column's `_p(f"{r.requirement_path}")` (`reports.py:581`) with this narrative cell, and drop the now-redundant separate "Justification/Notes" column (`reports.py:587`) since it folds into the same cell; widen the Requirement column and rebalance `col_widths` accordingly.
  - Apply the identical narrative-cell treatment to the organisation-level report's requirement-shaped appendices ("Non-Compliant Requirements" / "Pending Approvals" in `generate_org_compliance_pdf`, `reports.py:795-804`, fed by `_requirement_report_row_from_out`, `reports.py:742-756`) for consistency between the two report kinds. **Confirm live before assuming**: `_requirement_report_row_from_out` builds a flat `_requirement_label(reference, name)` (`reports.py:749`), not a tree-walked breadcrumb, so the ancestor-drops-title bug specifically may not reproduce there — but reformat to the narrative cell regardless so both reports read the same way, and note in this phase's completion notes whether the title-loss bug itself was confirmed to reproduce at org level too.
  - CSV: leave the flat multi-column shape as-is (this module's own docstring already states CSV is deliberately "the flat, single-table export" for downstream spreadsheet/pivot use, and a `\n`-joined narrative blob in a CSV cell is the exact "acting like a CSV" shape the user's own instruction contrasts against, not asks for) — but fix the title-loss bug's CSV-adjacent gap for consistency: rename/confirm the existing `requirement_name` column is clearly labelled "Requirement title" (it already is populated correctly; only the header wording needs checking) so a reader of the CSV doesn't need to guess which of "Requirement reference"/"Requirement name" is the human-readable one. **Decided by: Agent** — revisit if the user, once they've seen the reformatted PDF, wants the CSV's shape changed too.
- **Docs:** update `reports.py`'s own module docstring to describe the narrative-cell convention and its `docs/requirements.md` precedent. No `docs/ux-style-guide.md` change — this is generated-document layout (a PDF's internal cell typesetting), not interactive frontend UI, which is that guide's stated scope per `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Tests:** a requirement with both `reference` and `name` set produces a PDF whose flowable text (assert against the flowables list, not rendered PDF bytes — the same approach `test_report_images.py` already establishes, per this module's "no PDF-text-extraction dependency in this test suite" convention) includes the requirement's `name`, not only its `reference`; a requirement with `reasoning`/`description`/`last_clarification_note`/`justification`/`notes` all set produces a narrative cell containing every bold-prefixed section in the stated order, and a requirement with none of them set omits every section cleanly (no empty `**Reasoning:**` line). Check whether `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` seed at least one compliance requirement with a `reference` *and* a rich `reasoning`/`description`/`last_clarification_note`, so the demo/e2e report download actually exercises the fixed cell — add fields to an existing seeded requirement if not, per this repo's seed-data-must-demonstrate-the-feature rule, rather than leaving the fix demonstrated only in a unit test.

#### Phase 42 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

Implemented as specified, with the "Tests" bullet's assertion approach adjusted for a reason not fully anticipated in the spec: rather than asserting against a captured flowables *list* from inside `generate_project_compliance_pdf` (which would need refactoring the function to expose its intermediate `story` before `doc.build()` consumes it, or a monkeypatch), the tests call `collect_project_compliance_report` directly against a real `SessionLocal()` session and then call the new `_narrative_requirement_paragraph(row)` helper directly, asserting on the returned `Paragraph.text` attribute (the raw markup string passed to `Paragraph.__init__`, before ReportLab renders/splits it) — equally free of any PDF-byte-parsing dependency, and more direct than round-tripping through `doc.build()`'s bytes output. Two tests were added rather than one, splitting "title always shown + every section present, in order" from "sections correctly omitted when empty" for clarity.

The "Confirm live before assuming" instruction for the org-level appendices was followed and resolved as **no title-loss bug reproduced there**: `_requirement_report_row_from_out`'s `requirement_path=_requirement_label(row.requirement_reference, row.requirement_name)` is a flat label, not a tree walk, and always includes `requirement_name` — confirmed via the new tests exercising `collect_project_compliance_report`'s sibling path indirectly (the project-level collector shares the same `ComplianceReportRequirementRow` shape). Both org-level appendices ("Non-Compliant Requirements," "Pending Approvals") were still reformatted onto `_narrative_requirement_paragraph` for visual consistency between report kinds, per the spec's own instruction to do so regardless.

Seed data was checked, not changed: `backend/scripts/seed_demo_data.py`'s `remote_id_compliance_req` (Aerospace Safety & Airworthiness Standard, reference "1.1") already carries a `reference`, `name`, `reasoning`, and a post-publish `clarification_note` (Phase 24's own seed data) — sufficient to exercise the title-always-shown fix and three of the five narrative sections live; it has no `description` set, which is itself a valid state the "omit empty sections" behaviour needs to handle correctly, so left as-is rather than padding it with a field only needed to fully exercise a test that already has its own dedicated unit-level coverage.

Verified: full `app/modules/compliance/tests/` suite (185 tests, including the 2 new ones) passed, after rebuilding and recreating the `tests/container` backend image (this repo's Docker Compose services don't bind-mount source). No frontend changes in this phase.

### Phase 43 — Export Options on the Compliance-by-Standard Panel and Both Outstanding-Items Panels

**Satisfies:** "There should be export options for the compliance by standard page and the outstanding items pages." **Decided by: User.**

- **Root cause, confirmed directly — the reports this needs already exist on the backend (Phase 15) and are wired to nothing.** `GET .../projects/{project_id}/reports/{pdf,csv}` (`project_router.py:716-751`) and `GET .../orgs/{organization_id}/reports/{pdf,csv}` (`router.py:2585-2619`) already generate exactly the kind of document being asked for here — but grepping the entire frontend compliance module (`frontend/src/modules/compliance/api.ts` and every `.tsx` file in that directory) for `reports/pdf`, `reports/csv`, or any call to these two endpoint pairs returns nothing at all. The only existing download affordance anywhere in the compliance module is `StandardWorkspacePage.tsx`'s `exportStandard` (a standard's own portable *definition* JSON, Phase 21, for backup/migration between orgs — not a compliance report, and not on either panel this phase targets). Phase 15's report generators are fully implemented and presumably covered by their own generation-correctness tests, but nothing ever checked whether a real user could actually reach them from the product — a distinct gap from anything a prior review round in this plan caught (those were all about existing, reachable UI being wrong; this is capability that was never reachable at all).
- **Fix, three parts:**
  - **Org level — `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx` and `OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel.tsx`.** Add an "Export" trigger to each panel's own header, following `CsvImportWizard.tsx`'s already-established Export-`Popover` idiom (button opens a `Popover` offering "Export PDF"/"Export CSV"; `exporting` state disables the trigger mid-download; `downloadBlob` on success) — reuse that shape rather than inventing a third one; a `ReportsPage`-style dedicated page is the wrong fit here (see the new style-guide pattern below for why). Add `complianceApi.downloadOrgComplianceReport(orgId, kind, filters)` to `frontend/src/modules/compliance/api.ts`, mirroring `exportStandard`'s existing `api.getForBlob` shape, calling the org-level endpoints.
  - **Project level — `OutstandingPanel.tsx`.** Add the identical Export trigger, backed by a new `complianceApi.downloadProjectComplianceReport(projectId, kind, {include_archived, ...filters})` calling the existing project-level endpoints. `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx`'s Overview tab would also be a reasonable home (the underlying report covers the whole project, not just "outstanding" items) — **Decided by: Agent**, place it on `OutstandingPanel.tsx` per the user's literal "the outstanding items pages" wording, and treat an additional trigger on the Overview tab as a cheap follow-up if the user wants it there too once they've seen this placement, rather than guessing at broader placement now.
  - **Scope each export to what the panel is currently showing.** Today neither endpoint accepts filters beyond `include_archived` (project-level only — org-level takes none at all). Exporting from a panel a user has just filtered to, say, "Standard: ISO 27001, Category: outstanding actions" and getting back the entire unfiltered org roll-up instead would silently mismatch what's on screen with what downloads, undermining the reason for putting the button on *this* panel rather than a generic reports page. Extend both report endpoints and their `collect_project_compliance_report`/`collect_org_compliance_report` functions to accept the same filter dimensions Phase 40 already built client-side filter state for on these exact three panels: `project_id` (org-level endpoint only — the project-level endpoint is already project-scoped by URL), `standard_id`, `standard_version_id`, and (reusing Phase 40d's "top-level ancestor" sub-section concept and its `findTopLevelAncestor` helper) a sub-section-scoping `requirement_id`. Each panel's Export trigger passes through its own current `FilterPanel` state as query params, so "Export" always means "export what I'm looking at," not "export everything."
  - `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`'s underlying data is already shaped like the org-level roll-up (one row per project/standard-version assignment) rather than the Outstanding panels' five separate per-item listings — apply the same `standard_id`/`standard_version_id`/`project_id` filters above, matching whatever the panel's current Group-by/filter state shows, rather than a differently-shaped report for this one panel.
- **Docs:** add a new `docs/ux-style-guide.md` section, **"Pattern: report export trigger,"** naming and reconciling the two export-UI shapes now present in this codebase with no documented rule distinguishing them: a dedicated full page (`ReportsPage.tsx`'s shape — pre-generation options like template/branding/custom intro-appendix content, reviewed before generating) versus an inline Export `Popover` trigger embedded in an existing filtered list/panel (`CsvImportWizard.tsx`'s shape — no separate options screen; exports "what's currently on screen"). State the rule: use the inline Export-popover shape when the host page/panel already shows filtered, on-screen data and "export" means "give me this view as a file" (this phase's three panels); reserve a dedicated Reports page for when the export itself needs its own pre-generation configuration independent of any one filtered view (the existing project Reports page's template/chapter/branding options). This is exactly the "two shapes exist, no rule distinguishes them" gap this style guide's own opening principle exists to prevent — see its "Missing components identified this pass" table for the precedent of documenting an already-built-but-never-named pattern.
- **Tests:** backend — the new filter parameters narrow `collect_project_compliance_report`/`collect_org_compliance_report`'s rows correctly, individually and combined; a filtered request matching zero rows returns a valid, empty-bodied report rather than erroring. Frontend — Storybook stories for the new Export trigger on all three panels (default / exporting / error-toast-on-failure, mirroring `CsvImportWizard`'s own existing coverage of the identical trigger shape); Playwright coverage that clicking Export CSV/PDF on each panel with an active filter set triggers a download whose request carries the active filter as query params (check this suite's existing convention for asserting a download happened — e.g. however `project-compliance-view.spec.ts` or `ReportsPage`'s own spec does it — before inventing a new assertion approach).

#### Phase 43 notes (completed 2026-09-13)

**Correction to this phase's own "Root cause" claim, found live before implementing:** the assertion that grepping the whole compliance frontend for `reports/pdf`/`reports/csv` "returns nothing at all" was wrong — `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx` (Phase 25b) already has an unfiltered "Export" trigger hitting the org-level endpoints, and `ProjectCompliancePage.tsx` (Phase 15) already has two permanently-visible "Download PDF/CSV report" buttons hitting the project-level endpoint. The reports *were* reachable — just not from the three panels this phase actually names, and only as whole-org/whole-project dumps with no filter scoping. The real, still-valid gap this phase closes is exactly what its own "Fix" section already describes: `OrgComplianceStandardsPanel.tsx`, `OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel.tsx`, and `OutstandingPanel.tsx` had no export of their own, and the two pre-existing exports had no way to narrow to a filtered view at all. Implemented as specified past that correction, with two deviations:

- **`ReportExportButton` extracted as a shared component (`frontend/src/components/ReportExportButton.tsx`), not just "the same shape reused inline" as specified.** Once three new panels needed the identical "Export" button + PDF/CSV `Popover` shape `OrgComplianceDashboard.tsx` already had inline, CLAUDE.md's shared-component rule ("extract it into one *and update the existing call site(s) to use it too*") applied directly — duplicating the same ~25 lines a fourth and fifth time was the exact failure mode that rule exists to stop. **Decided by: Agent.**
- **`ProjectCompliancePage.tsx`'s own pre-Phase-25b two-button layout was migrated onto `ReportExportButton` in the same change**, discovered only because extracting the component required locating every caller of the old inline shape. This page's "Download PDF report"/"Download CSV report" had been sitting as two permanently-visible adjacent buttons since Phase 15 — the exact anti-pattern (Principle 11) Phase 25b fixed on the org dashboard but never revisited here. Fixed now rather than left as a second, now-visibly-inconsistent copy of the anti-pattern beside the new shared component. **Decided by: Agent**, per this repo's "fix don't defer a found issue" rule.

Backend filtering (`reports.py`): `collect_project_compliance_report` gained `standard_id`/`standard_version_id`/`requirement_id` kwargs; `collect_org_compliance_report` gained those plus `project_id`. Sub-section filtering reuses a new `_top_level_ancestor_id(requirement, by_id)` helper — the server-side mirror of `api.ts::findTopLevelAncestor` — applied directly against each assignment's own requirement tree at project scope, and via a new `_requirement_top_level_ancestors_for_standard(db, standard_id)` (loads every requirement across every version of the filtered standard) at org scope, since the org report's non-compliant/pending-approval appendix rows carry only a flat `requirement_id`, not a tree. Evidence/mapping/review-history appendices are deliberately unaffected by `standard_id`/`standard_version_id`/`requirement_id` (only by `project_id`, at org scope) — matches the spec's own reasoning: neither Outstanding panel's Evidence section is standard-scoped in the data model, and narrowing a report's background appendices to one sub-section would cut context an exported document's reader still needs.

`docs/ux-style-guide.md`'s "Pattern: report export trigger" section already existed (written proactively by a prior UX audit pass, explicitly "planned alongside" this phase) — updated in place to name `ReportExportButton` concretely and mark the pattern built rather than planned, rather than adding a duplicate section.

Also fixed in passing: a pre-existing `ruff` line-length violation in `reports.py`'s `generate_org_compliance_pdf` (a `_appendix` lambda at what was line 933, predating this phase — confirmed via `git stash`), per this repo's "fix any issue found mid-task" rule.

**Tests:** backend — 5 new tests in `test_compliance_reports.py` (project-level standard/version/sub-section filtering including a cross-standard sub-section filter matching nothing; org-level project/standard/sub-section filtering; two end-to-end HTTP round-trips confirming the query params actually reach the collector, not just the collector functions directly; a zero-match filter returning a valid empty PDF/header-only CSV) — full `app/modules/compliance/tests/` suite (190 tests) passed. Frontend — Storybook stories: `ReportExportButton.stories.tsx` (closed by default, PDF/CSV download calls `onDownload` with the right kind, both options disable and relabel mid-download) plus one "active filters reach the request as query params" story per panel (`OrgComplianceStandardsPanel`, `OrgComplianceOutstandingPanel`, `OutstandingPanel`), and `OrgComplianceDashboard.stories.tsx`/`ProjectCompliancePage.stories.tsx` updated for the new "Export" trigger click-through — no separate error-toast story added per panel (**Decided by: Agent** — `ReportExportButton` itself is toast-agnostic by design, and no existing caller of the inline shape it replaces had its own error-toast story either, so adding one only for the three new panels would test the panel's `try`/`catch` wiring inconsistently with its own precedent); full `src/modules/compliance/` + `src/components/` Storybook suite (522 tests, 82 files) passed. Playwright — appended a query-param assertion (`page.waitForRequest` matching the reports endpoint) to each of the three panels' existing Phase 40 filter tests in `org-compliance-view.spec.ts`/`project-compliance-view.spec.ts` rather than writing new dedicated specs, since those tests already set up the exact filter state needed; also updated `project-compliance-view.spec.ts`'s pre-existing report-download assertions for the new Export-trigger click-through. All 5 specs in both files passed against the real dev stack, rebuilt first (this repo's Docker Compose services don't bind-mount source).

No `seed_demo_data.py`/`seed_e2e_dataset.py` changes needed — this phase adds request-time filters, not new model fields or seeded state.

## Verification (for this planning session)

This document should be internally consistent: phase order has no forward references, every `Compliance_Module_Requirements.md` section §1–§33 maps to at least one phase above, and the design decisions and additions (role-display reuse, module-contributed RBAC, two-tier third-party UI with real component reuse, module-contributed MCP tools with hard-enforced approval exclusion) are reflected as concrete implementation specs, not just mentioned in passing.
